                 DEMAND NO.34
    MUNICIPAL ADMINISTRATION AND
       WATER SUPPLY DEPARTMENT
             Policy Note – 2025-2026
               VISION STATEMENT


Provide a decent quality of life, clean, green

and sustainable environment, quality basic

amenities, vibrant public spaces, induce

economic      growth,   generate   employment

and mitigate poverty through responsive

city governance leading to a decent quality

of life for all by following a comprehensive,

inclusive,     integrated,   transparent    and

participatory       approach       of      urban

development.




                        1
1. PREAMBLE
      Urbanization refers to the shift of
populations from rural to urban areas, leading to
city expansion and an economic transition from
agrarian to industrial economies. While this
process has occurred for centuries, globalization
and     industrialization   have     significantly
accelerated urban growth in recent decades.
Understanding these trends in urbanization is
crucial for sustainable and balanced urban
planning.
     As a key indicator of development,
urbanization is closely linked to economic growth
and quality of life.
      India, one of the world's largest urban
systems, had 37.70 crore (31.14%) urban
residents in 2011, surpassing many highly
urbanized nations. Urban growth is driven by
natural population increase, migration and rural
re-classification. However, uneven urbanization
leads to socio-economic disparities while highly
urbanized areas prosper, whereas less urbanized
regions face economic stagnation. Addressing
these inequalities is essential for inclusive and
sustainable development. Strategic investments

                        2
in infrastructure and economic opportunities can
ensure balanced urban growth across regions.
Urban Development in India
       India, the world's most populous country,
had 140 crore people in 2023 and the second-
largest urban system globally. In 1901, only
2.6 crore people lived in urban areas.
Urbanization in India has been gradually
increasing due to the slow re-classification of
rural areas. By 2011, the urban population
reached     37.70   crore   (31.14%),   growing
significantly from 11% in 1901. The highest
growth rate was recorded between 1971–1981,
driven by industrialization. Despite economic
growth, urban expansion slowed in the 2000s.
The number of towns and cities increased from
1,827 in 1901 to 7,935 in 2011.
Urbanization in Tamil Nadu
      Tamil Nadu has emerged as India’s
second-largest      economy,      propelled     by
industrialization, trade, and urban growth. In
recent years, liberalization, a thriving IT sector,
and a technology driven workforce have
accelerated economic development, driving


                         3
urban expansion. Urbanization serves as a key
indicator of economic progress, with expanding
cities playing a crucial role in contributing to
national GDP.
      Over the decades, Tamil Nadu has shown
consistent urban growth, with its urban
population increasing from 89.90 lakh (26.99%)
in 1961 to 3.495 crore (48.45%) in 2011. The
number of towns has also increased significantly,
from 339 in 1961 to 1,097 in 2011.
       This rapid urban expansion brings forth
many opportunities and various challenges.
While urban areas drive economic growth, they
also    require    significant  investment    in
infrastructure, housing, transportation, and
environmental sustainability. As Tamil Nadu
continues to urbanize, balanced development
policies will be crucial to ensuring sustainable
urban growth while maintaining economic
momentum.
Key Areas of Infrastructure Development
      In the water supply sector, Tamil Nadu
faces a growing demand for safe drinking water,
with a urban population of approximately 3.8


                        4
crore and 93 lakh households. The current urban
water demand is 6,400 MLD, whereas the
existing supply is 4,400 MLD, resulting in a
shortfall of 2,000 MLD. Tamil Nadu addresses
this challenge through targeted household water
supply connections to the tune of 73.87 lakh,
with    ongoing    implementation   of  various
schemes. The AMRUT 2.0 projects of the State
include 24x7 water supply for 5.5 lakh service
connections in pilot towns.
      Similarly, in the sanitation sector, the used
water generation is 3,500 MLD, while the
combined capacity of sewage treatment facilities
is 2,656 MLD, leaving a significant gap of
844 MLD. Further, 60% of households have
sewer and septage connections and the
challenge becomes more difficult with city/town
expansions underway.
      Another   key    area   of   infrastructure
development is Solid Waste Management.
The daily waste generation in the urban local
bodies account for close to 14,600 TPD which is
taken care of through Integrated waste
management with emphasis on segregation,
recycling, and waste-to-energy projects.

                        5
       An emerging area in the realm of
infrastructure development over the recent
years has been water reuse and sustainability,
wherein Tamil Nadu as of now, has a tertiary
treatment capacity of 100 MLD of wastewater
paving the way for its reuse for industrial
purposes, reducing pressure on availability of
water for drinking and domestic purposes. This
is supplemented with the ongoing efforts in
rainwater harvesting and groundwater recharge.
       Urban Roads and Mobility are a key factor
in economic growth, accessibility and quality of
life, etc. Tamil Nadu Government is undertaking
several appropriate responses such as roads
widening and upgrading to enhance traffic flow
and reduce congestion. In the last four years
Tamil Nadu Government has undertaken
development of 18,900.87 km of roads, apart
from modernisation of bus stands and public
transport through establishment of integrated
bus stands in major towns. 134 bus stands have
been taken up for implementation in the last
four years.
     A key challenge in urban development in
the current era has been ensuring a more

                       6
sustainable, resilient and livable cities for the
future, balancing growth and sustainable
environment.      Addressing      these   concerns,
Tamil Nadu has been providing a significant
thrust on blue-green infrastructure development
over the past few years so as to effectively meet
the urban challenges such as climate change,
water resources management and public health.
This process has been essentially integrating a
natural system into the urban landscape.
Tamil Nadu Government is undertaking creation/
revamping of 1,034 parks, rejuvenation of
34 lakes and multiple biodiversity corridors in
urban areas. Restoration and conservation of
1,224        water       bodies       for     flood
resilience, investment of Rs.28,174.64 crore in
sustainable urban initiatives is some of the
far-reaching initiatives in this regard.
       Tamil Nadu, through well planned and
strategic investments in the areas of urban
development,     with     sustained     focus    on
infrastructure upgradation and modernization, is
striving to ensure its long-term growth and
development, economic and environmental
resilience and sustainability, and future proofing.


                        7
       The       department       of      Municipal
Administration and Water Supply with its
commitment to pioneering urban planning and
infrastructure     development,       responsibility
towards environmental concerns, take pride in
its significant contribution to achieving the lofty
ideal of inclusive socio-economic growth of the
State, as envisioned in the Growth Model.
      In the process, the State continues to
consolidate its position as a leader in urban
development in India.
Human Resources Development
       Knowledge centres and Libraries are
being built to improve the knowledge and the
skills of students studying in schools and
colleges in corporations and municipalities,
students preparing for competitive exams and all
the public.
Recruitment of      Engineers     and   Technical
staff
      Steps have been taken to fill up
613 Assistant Engineer, 168 Assistant Engineer
(Planning), 427 Work Inspector, 205 Overseer,
177    Draughtsman,    24   Junior   Engineer,

                         8
153 Junior Engineer (Planning), 102 Town
Planning Inspector, 257 Technical Assistant,
443    Sanitary      Inspector  posts   totaling
2569    vacant      posts    in the   Municipal
Administration, Town Panchayats Administration,
Greater Chennai Corporation, Tamil Nadu Water
Supply and Drainage Board and Chennai
Metropolitan Water Supply and Sewerage Board
under the control of Municipal Administration &
Water Supply Department and appointments
orders are to be issued soon.

Bodies.

     (CTP)

the year 2023-24, the Hon’ble Minister for

2.1.1 Creation and Regularisation of Posts

     The Corporations and Municipalities have
been classified into different grades, based on
population and annual income respectively and
new posts have been created as well as
sanctioned posts have been rationalized in
accordance with the said grades of Corporations
and Municipalities, vide G.O.(Ms)No.152, dated
20.10.2022      and     G.O.(Ms)No.10,   dated
23.01.2023 of Municipal Administration and
Water Supply Department.
     Necessary action has been taken to fill up
the vacancies of these posts meant for direct


                      15
                      15
recruitment, out of the total posts sanctioned in
the said two orders of the Government and the
process of recruitment is in final stage.

2.2 Functions of Urban Local Bodies

2.2.1 Expansion of limits of Corporations
      and Municipalities

       Based on various factors, including,
urbanization and the recommendations of the
respective   District  Collectors,   Government
Orders have been issued declaring the intention
to merge the adjoining town panchayats and
village panchayats with municipal corporations
and municipalities, expanding their boundaries.

      G.O.(Ms)No.201, Municipal Administration
and     Water   Supply    Department,       dated
31.12.2024 has been issued declaring the
intention  to   expand    the    boundaries    of
16 municipal corporations viz., Greater Chennai,
Coimbatore, Cuddalore, Dindigul, Erode, Karur,
Hosur, Madurai, Salem, Tiruchirappalli, Tiruppur,
Avadi, Kumbakonam, Thanjavur, Thoothukudi,
and Sivakasi. It is proposed to include
4 municipalities, 5 town panchayats and


                       16
                       16
149 village panchayats             with       16     municipal
corporations.

      G.O.(Ms)No.202, Municipal Administration
and     Water       Supply        Department,           dated
31.12.2024       has    been      issued    declaring      the
intention      to   expand        the      boundaries       of
41    Municipalities,      viz.    Ariyalur,        Pallapatti,
Pallipalayam,       Rasipuram,          Komarapalayam,
Krishnagiri,        Mayiladuthurai,            Perambalur,
Ramanathapuram,           Keelakkarai,         Paramakudi,
Udumalaipettai,          Dharapuram,               Kodaikanal,
Mettupalayam, Pollachi, Chidambaram, Panruti,
Dharmapuri,         Bhavani,         Gobichettipalayam,
Punjaipuliyampatti,         Sivagangai,             Kovilpatti,
Musiri, Lalgudi, Thuraiyur, Tiruvallur, Ponneri,
Tiruttani,     Arani,    Tiruvathipuram,           Vandavasi,
Mannargudi,             Tiruvarur,            Virudhunagar,
Aruppukkottai, Rajapalayam, Theni-Allinagaram,
Periyakulam and Nagapattinam. It is proposed to
merge    1     Town Panchayat           and    147     Village
Panchayats with 41 Municipalities.

2.2.2     Constitution    of   New     Municipal
          Corporations

      As per the announcement made by the
Hon’ble Minister for Municipal Administration
during the demand for grant of this department
for the year 2023-24, 4 new municipal
corporations have been constituted vide,
G.O.(Ms)Nos.102, 103, 104 and 105, Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 10.08.2024, respectively, as detailed
below:

(i)    Karaikudi municipality has been constituted
       as municipal corporation by merging with 2
       town panchayats and 5 village panchayats;
(ii)   Tiruvannamalai municipality has been
       constituted as municipal corporation by
       merging with 18 village panchayats and Adi
       Annamalai forest area;
(iii) Pudukkottai    municipality    has  been
      constituted as municipal corporation by
      merging with 11 village panchayats and
      Kaspa forests (west) area; and



                         18
                         18
(iv) Namakkal      municipality    has   been
     constituted as municipal corporation by
     merging with 12 village panchayats.


2.2.3 Constitution of New Municipalities

     The    Hon'ble    Minister   for   Municipal
Administration made an announcement on the
floor of the Legislative Assembly during 2023-24
that    the    7    town    panchayats   namely,
Sriperumbudur,       Mamallapuram,    Perundurai,
Avinashi, Kothagiri, Sankagiri and Thiruvaiyaru
will be upgraded as municipalities. Now, orders
have been issued constituting the 3 town
panchayats      such   as     Sriperumbudur,
Mamallapuram       and    Thiruvaiyaru    as
municipalities vide G.O.(Ms)No.15, Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 05.02.2025.

     Further, based on population and three
years average annual income, the intention to
constitute   13   new      municipalities   viz.,
Perundurai, Avinashi, Kothagiri, Sangagiri,
Harur,     Kavundapadi,     Polur,     Chengam,
Kanniyakumari,          Sulur,          Mohanur,


                       19
                       19
Naravarikuppam and Veppampattu through
stand-alone upgradation or through merger of
adjoining village panchayats/town panchayats,
has been declared vide G.O.(Ms)No.203,
Municipal Administration and Water Supply
Department, dated 31.12.2024.

2.3 Infrastructure development

2.3.1 Water Supply

      Due to the expansion of corporations and
municipalities,  increasing     households     and
population, the demand for drinking water
supplied to the public is increasing. The policy of
the Government is to provide protected drinking
water supply to all the people in urban areas
and floating population, by increasing the
duration of drinking water distribution through
integrating drinking water projects which are
completed in different timelines and to
rejuvenate    the   water   supply     schemes,
identifying new water sources and to complete
new water supply schemes in a timely manner.

2.3.3 Streetlights

     A   total   of   9.50    lakh   streetlights   are
being maintained in 24 corporations (other
than     Greater   Chennai   Corporation)   and
137      municipalities.   Further,   additional
streetlights are being provided wherever
required, to meet the norms of one streetlight
for every 30 metres. Considering the LUX value
of the lamp, the wattage category, safe
transportation, minimum maintenance charges
and environmental safety, action has been taken
to install Energy efficient LED streetlights in



                         24
                         24
ULBs to reduce energy consumption charges
by 40%.

     As announced in the 2021-22 budget, the
conversion of 3,31,895 existing streetlights into
LED   lights   at   an    estimated     cost   of
Rs.374.09 crore and the installation of
77,667 new streetlights at an estimated cost of
Rs.118.35 crore are being implemented across
16 corporations and 119 municipalities under
SUIDF and 15th Finance Commission Grants
(2021-22 & 2022-23).

     Additionally, under SUIDF Rs.64.81 crore
has been allocated for installing 33,660 new
LED streetlights in 6 corporations, while
Rs.20.41 crore has been sanctioned for
conversion of 17,704 existing streetlights into
LED lights in 10 newly upgraded municipalities.

     So far, 4,37,067 LED streetlights have
been installed and the remaining works are in
progress.

2.3.5 Flood Mitigation Works

     In order to prevent the risk of flooding in
Greater Chennai Corporation area and Chennai
suburbs and to find a permanent solution to the
same, a Flood Management Advisory Committee
headed by Dr.V.Thirupukazh, I.A.S., (Retired)
was appointed, vide G.O.(D)No.362, Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 22.10.2021. Hon'ble Chief Minister held
number of consultation meetings with this
committee.

     To prevent flood damage in suburban areas
of   Chennai   viz.,   Tambaram    and   Avadi
corporations and Mangadu, Kundrathur and
Tiruverkadu    municipalities,  sanction   was
accorded for taking up 30.31 km of stormwater
drains in 21 locations at an estimated cost of
Rs.82.15 crore, vide G.O.No.214, Revenue and
Disaster   Management      Department,   dated
17.05.2022. All the sanctioned works have been
completed.

    In order to prevent floods in Thoothukudi

2.5 Urban Health Infrastructure
2.5.1 National Urban Health Mission
      The National Urban Health Mission was
approved as a sub-mission of the National
Health Mission (NHM) during the year 2013.
With focus on the urban poor, the mission aims
to   meet   the   health    needs       of   the    urban
population, by providing essential primary health
care services at low cost of treatment. It is
implemented       in    State     capitals,        district
headquarters and other cities with a population
of 50,000 and above. It is primarily set up to
cater to the needs of the marginalized groups,
such as slum dwellers, street vendors, homeless
people, etc.

      Under    this    mission,   during      the    year
2022-23,    construction    of    new    buildings     for
43 existing urban primary health centers in

                           36
                           36
municipalities and corporations at an estimated
cost of Rs.51.60 crore and upgradation of
4   health   centres    in   2     corporations   and   1
municipality     at     an        estimated   cost      of
Rs.2.40 crore have been taken up. 15 works
have been completed and other works are in
progress.

2.5.2 15th Finance Commission Health Grant
      The 15th Finance Commission, in order to
strengthen the public health infrastructure and
service delivery for rural and urban population,
provides component specific grants.

      With a focus on comprehensive primary
health   care,   15th   Finance       Commission     has
sanctioned grants for diagnostic infrastructure
and additional buildings. The following works
have been sanctioned for the financial years
from 2021-22 to 2024-25, of which 644 works
are completed and the remaining works are in
various stages of progress.

                              38

     The first phase of the UGSS has been

                nd     rd

       The Government is committed to extend

                              40
                              40
2.7   Modern   Libraries        (Library     cum
      Knowledge Centres)
      In      corporations   (except     GCC),
municipalities and town panchayats, 100 Library
cum Knowledge Centres have been sanctioned in
three types of designs at an estimated cost of
Rs.193.56 crore. These centres help in
enhancing the skills of students and job
aspirants and cater the needs of the public
interested in intellectual pursuits. These centres
act as vital gateways to local, national and
international information, empowering the public
and students with knowledge.

      The Hon’ble Chief Minister of Tamilnadu
inaugurated 71 number of Library cum
Knowledge Centres through video conference as
first phase on 05.01.2024. At present, 84
number of Library cum Knowledge Centres are
functioning effectively and balance 16 centres
are under construction.

     These centres are well-equipped with a
comprehensive collection of 2,754 books sourced
from 244 publishers, as recommended by the
Department of Public Libraries and all books are


                       41
                       41
available in all centres. This curated selection is
specifically designed to support the students and
aspirants in their preparation for competitive
examinations, assisting them in achieving their
goals. This includes the books relevant for
competitive examinations conducted by the
Tamil Nadu Public Service Commission (TNPSC),
Tamil Nadu Uniformed Services Recruitment
Board (TNUSRB), the Union Public Service
Commission    (UPSC),  the Staff Selection
Commission     (SSC)  and  other  relevant
recruitment agencies.

   To handle the books, retired staff acting as
mentors and outsourced librarians have been
employed in each centre.

       All Library cum Knowledge Centres are
equipped with computers with internet access
and have training rooms with facilities for
efficient training of the students. Currently,
special online classes for various competitive
exams     are  being   conducted  by    Anna
Administrative Staff College on weekends.
Further, a committee headed by the respective



                        42
                        42
District Collector has been formed to monitor the
functioning of these centres.

2.8.2 Namakku Naame Thittam (Urban)

      In    order   to   improve   the   self-support
mechanism of public and to enhance increased
public participation in creating and maintaining
community infrastructure, the Government has
introduced Namakku Naame Thittam (Urban). In
this scheme, 1/3rd of estimate cost is fixed as
public contribution and the balance amount will
be borne by Government.

     Under this scheme, for the benefit of the
public and to create assets to urban local bodies,
infrastructure works such as renovation of water
bodies, formation and improvement of parks and
play grounds, installation of traffic islands,
fountains, streetlights and CCTV cameras,
planting of trees with installation of tree-guards,
construction of buildings for schools, colleges,
shopping complexes and any other building of
public benefit, roads and bridges are taken up.



                          45
                          45
      Further, the public contribution amount for
Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes has
been fixed as one fifth of the estimate amount
so that the people belonging to Scheduled
Castes and Scheduled Tribes could be much
benefitted under this scheme.
      A sum of Rs.150.00 crore for the year
2021-22, Rs.200.00 crore for the year 2022-23,
Rs.100.00 crore for the year 2023-24 and
Rs.100.00 crore for the year 2024-25 has been
allocated for implementing the scheme. The
details of works sanctioned in municipalities and
corporations from 2021-22 to 2024-25 are given
below:

2.9 Union Government Schemes
2.9.1 Smart Cities Mission
      The Smart Cities Mission was launched by
the Government of India in 2015 with the
objective    of  upgrading        the    sustainable
infrastructure,    clean       and       sustainable
environment and giving solution for continuous
growth and quality life of all citizen in cities. This
scheme provides smart solutions for service
delivery on sustainable and inclusive growth of
cities. An area of city is selected and the works
implemented in that area will be a replicable




                         47
                         47
model and act as a light house project to other
cities.

      The 11 cities viz., Chennai, Coimbatore,
Madurai,         Salem,        Thanjavur,         Vellore,
Thoothukudi,              Tirunelveli,           Tiruppur,
Tiruchirappalli and Erode have been selected for
implementation of smart cities mission. Water
supply     improvement           works,     underground
sewerage         system       works,       bus      stand
improvements, rejuvenation of water bodies,
solar streetlights, improvement of parks are
taken up for implementation at an estimated
cost of Rs.1,000 crore for each city in the ratio
of 50:50    by Union Government and State
Government. Under this mission, a total of
682      works     have      been        taken    up    in
10 corporations (except Chennai Corporation) at
a cost of Rs.9,656.71 crore, out of which
653 works have been completed at an estimated
cost of Rs.8,289.66 crore and other works are in
progress at various stages. The project period
has been extended upto March 2025.



                            48
                            48
2.9.2 Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and
      Urban Transformation (AMRUT)
      Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban
Transformation (AMRUT) was launched on
25.06.2015. This scheme is implemented with
the contribution of the Central Government and
State Government. In Tamil Nadu, 28 cities viz.,
Chennai, Coimbatore, Madurai, Thiruchirappalli,
Salem,    Tirunelveli,  Tiruppur,   Thoothukudi,
Thanjavur, Dindigul, Erode, Vellore, Nagercoil,
Avadi and Hosur corporations and Pallavapuram,
Tambaram,        Cuddalore,       Kancheepuram,
Thiruvannamalai, Kumbakonam, Rajapalayam,
Pudukkottai, Ambur, Karaikudi, Nagapattinam,
Rameswaram municipalities and Velankanni
town panchayat have been selected for
implementation of AMRUT.
     Under AMRUT, 18 water supply projects,
18   underground     sewerage    schemes and
409 parks have been implemented at an
estimated cost of Rs.11,441.42 crore.
     Out of 445 projects, 433 projects have
been completed and 12 projects are in progress
at various stages. The mission period is



                      49
                      49
extended   by   Union      Government       upto
December 31, 2025.
     Tamil Nadu, which is the pioneer in
implementing all schemes, is implementing
AMRUT also in an effective manner.
2.9.3 Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and
      Urban Transformation 2.0 (AMRUT 2.0)
      With the consideration of the need for
implementing AMRUT in all towns, the Atal
Mission    for    Rejuvenation    and    Urban
Transformation     2.0   was    launched    on
01.10.2021. In Tamil Nadu 25 corporations
(including   Greater    Chennai   Corporation),
137 municipalities and 487 town panchayats are
benefitting under AMRUT 2.0. The scheme
implementation period is 5 years commencing
from October 2021. The important components
of AMRUT 2.0 are water supply to all
households, underground sewerage scheme and
septage management, reuse of the used water
through tertiary treatment, rejuvenation of
water bodies and green parks development.

      Under AMRUT 2.0, the Union Government
allocation for Tamil Nadu is Rs.4,942 crore;


                      50
                      50
Government of Tamil Nadu allocation is
Rs.3,713.41 crore and the contribution of Urban
Local Bodies is Rs.5,354.83 crore.

      A total of 446 projects have been taken up
at an estimated cost of Rs.6,655.80 crore so far.
This includes 42 water supply projects and
combined water supply projects at an estimated
cost of Rs.3,087.87 crore; 14 underground
sewerage projects at an estimated cost of
Rs.3,360.65 crore; 186 projects of water bodies’
improvement     at   an   estimated    cost   of
Rs.131.67 crore; 204 green parks at an
estimated cost of Rs.75.59 crore.

      Out of 446 projects, 315 projects have
been completed and the remaining projects are
in progress at various stages.

2.9.4 Swachh Bharath Mission (Urban)
      The main objective of SBM(U) is to make
all the cities garbage free and open defecation
free. Under SBM(U), 161 ULBs have been
declared as Open Defecation Free (ODF). The
Government is committed to make all the cities
truly open defecation free by providing universal
access to toilets.

                       51
                       51
        Under the Mission, 5,07,143 Individual
House Hold Latrines (IHHL), and 22,337 seats of
Community toilets and Public Toilets have been
constructed       so     far.        Under     this      mission
procurement        of   vehicles        such        as   battery
operated vehicles, light commercial vehicles and
tipper lorries for primary collection / solid waste
managaemt         activities     along       with    works   for
scientific processing of solid waste, construction
of wet and dry waste processing centres such as
Micro Composting Centres, Resource Recovery
Centres and other infrastructure works have
been taken up and completed.

2.9.5 Swachh Bharath Mission 2.0
        Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0 was launched
on 1st October 2021 with a vision to convert all
the      cities     Garbage            Free         by    2026.
The Mission period is 5 years, i.e. upto October
2026.

        The   fund      sharing       pattern       under    the
Swachh Bharat Mission (U) 2.0 is as follows:

(a)    Sanitation

       ULB wise action plan has been prepared
for the requirement of sanitation and solid waste
management          based     on   the     existing    and
projected population for the year 2026, as per
the SBM 2.0 guidelines.

      To sustain the Open Defecation Free status
in all statutory towns, 8,981 number of
Individual House hold Latrines have been
sanctioned     since   2021-22,     of    which
8,213 nos. have been completed and the
remaining 768 numbers are in various stages of
construction.

       Community Toilets are constructed for
providing sanitation facilities to the house holds
without land for the construction of IHHL. Under

                              53
                              53
SBM    2.0,   from        2021-22   to      till   date
466 numbers of community toilets, 342 numbers
of public toilets and 230 numbers of urinals have
been   sanctioned    at    an   estimated     cost   of
Rs.220.03 crore. The works are in various stages
of implementation.

(b) Solid Waste Management

      In 24 corporations and 137 municipalities
in the State, around 8,000 MT of solid waste is
generated daily. Out of this, nearly 54% is wet
waste and 46% is dry waste. Scientific
management of solid and liquid waste in an
environment friendly manner is an important
duty of the Urban Local Bodies. The wet waste is
scientifically  processed      through    micro
composting centres, bio methanation plants,
windrow composting and on-site composting
centres and converted into manure. Similarly,
the dry waste is collected and transported to
Resource Recovery Centres (RRC) and the
recyclable waste is disposed through the
identified   vendors for   recycling. The
non-recyclable and combustible waste is


                          54
                          54
separated and transported to nearby cement
plants, for use it as RDF in co-processing.

      Based on the SBM 2.0 guidelines, City
Solid Waste Action Plan (CSWAP) has been
prepared for all the 161 ULBs and gap has been
arrived at for the projected waste generation till
the year 2026. The State High Powered
Committee of SBM (U) 2.0 has approved the City
Solid Waste Action Plan to establish various
infrastructure facilities like 154 micro compost
centres, 6 bio-methanation plants, 6 bio-CNG
plants, 213 manual material recovery facilities, 5
Transfer stations, 3 fully automatic material
recovery facilities and 2 waste to energy plants
for processing both wet and dry waste.

     To prevent the dust and air pollution, road
sweeping machines have been procured for
5 corporations viz, Coimbatore, Madurai,
Tiruchirappalli, Salem and Thoothukudi and are
put to use. During 2023-24, 3 numbers of road
sweeping machines have been sanctioned for
Avadi and Thoothukudi Corporations. During
2024-25, 2 numbers of road sweeping machines



                       55
                       55
have     been    sanctioned     for    Tambaram
corporation.

(c) Bio-mining of Legacy Waste
     Reclamation of dump yards filled with
legacy waste through bio mining is under
progress in various ULBs. The total quantity of
legacy waste accumulated in 76 ULBs under the
administrative  purview      of   Directorate   of
Municipal Administration is 43 lakh cu.m.

     So far, bio mining works have been
completed in 41 ULBs and 9 lakh cu.m of legacy
waste have been remediated and 179.22 acres
of land has been retrieved. In 39 ULBs,
bio-mining works are in various stages.

       A part of the retrieved land in various
cities will be used for setting up solid waste
processing facilities and remaining land will be
used for public purposes, which include
developing parks and urban green spaces.

(d) Used Water Management
      Under SBM 2.0, 17 Sewage Treatment
Plants for underground sewerage schemes with
capacity of 269.19 MLD at an estimated cost of


                       56
                       56
Rs.1,094.28 crore, 54 Sewage Treatment
Plants/Sewage Treatment cum Faecal Sludge
Treatment Plant with interception and diversion
structures, with a capacity of 211.16 MLD at a
cost of Rs.647.06 crore have been sanctioned
and    works    are   in   different stage   of
implementation. To desludge and transport the
faecal matter to treatment plants, 115 cesspool
tankers have been procured for 95 ULBs at a
cost of Rs.49.73 crore and put into use.

(e) Organic Manure – Sezhippu
      All the wet waste generated are converted
into organic manure in the micro compost
centres and on-site compost centres. The
manure is scientifically analysed for N.P.K
contents and presence of heavy metals as per
the specification of Fertilizer Control Order,
1985, to ensure supply of quality manure.
The analysed quality manures is being
distributed under the common brand name
‘Sezhippu’ in all the urban local bodies in the
State. So far, 1.58 lakh MT of manure has been
distributed to farmers and for kitchen gardens.




                      57
                      57
2.10 National     Urban     Livelihood   Mission
     (NULM)
      Under the National Urban Livelihood
Mission, the components of Shelter for Urban
Homeless (SUH) and Support to Urban Street
Vendors (SUSV) are implemented by the
Directorate of Municipal Administration.

2.10.1 Shelter for Urban Homeless (SUH)
       It is the policy decision of the Government
to provide permanent shelters with all basic
infrastructure facilities like water supply,
sanitation, safety and security to the street
dwellers in the corporations and municipalities.
Under this component, with Government of India
contribution, 177 shelters have been constructed
at an estimated cost of Rs.97.70 crore in
23 corporations and 67 municipalities and all are
functional. Further, during 2023-24, 11 new
shelters have been sanctioned and all works are
under progress.

2.10.2 Support to Urban Street Vendors
       (SUSV)
      Vending carts are provided at free of cost
to the street vendors, doing vending activities in


                       58
                       58
the    street   sides   of    corporations   and
municipalities. Under this component, with
Government of India contribution, sanction has
been accorded for procurement of 4,577 vending
carts at an estimated cost of Rs.35.28 crore and
the same have been procured and distributed to
the street vendors.

      Further,  at   an    estimated    cost   of
Rs.1.45 crore, 10 vending markets have been
developed in 10 municipalities during 2023-24
and made functional for the benefit of the street
vendors and general public.

increased by Rs.50.00 crore, annually. This fund
is used for taking up capital works in urban local
bodies.

2.13.1 Sixth State Finance Commission
     In accordance with the recommendations of
the Sixth State Finance Commission, orders
have been issued regarding the distribution of
6th State Finance Commission grants to urban
local bodies and the implementation of schemes
vide G.O.(Ms)No.60, Finance Department, dated
28.02.2023. The main features are as follows:

• 10% of the total own tax revenue of the
  state should be distributed to urban local
  bodies.

• Funds should be distributed to urban local
  bodies and rural local bodies in the ratio
  of 49:51.

• 16% of the urban share should be
  distributed to the metropolitan city i.e. the
  Greater Chennai Corporation and the
  Chennai Metropolitan Water Supply and
  Sewerage Board.

• The remaining 84% of the urban share
  should be distributed to other corporations,
  municipalities and town panchayats.


                      66
                      66
• Funds should be distributed to the
  corporations (excluding Greater Chennai
  Corporation),   municipalities   and town
  panchayats in the ratio of 31:36:33

• The Sixth State Finance Commission has
  approved the distribution of funds to urban
  local bodies based on the following
  weightages:

2.14    15th Finance Commission

      The    15th Finance     Commission     was
constituted under Article 280 of the Constitution
of India, so as to make recommendations for
devolution     of  central  taxes    to   State
Governments and local bodies. The award period
of the 15th Finance Commission is from 2020-21
to 2025-26.

       Based on population, the 15th Finance
Commission         has    divided     ULBs      into    two
categories        viz.,   Million    Plus     Cities    and
Non-Million Plus Cities. The Greater Chennai
Corporation,          Coimbatore,       Madurai         and
Tiruchirappalli      Corporations     are     under     the
category     of     Million   Plus   Cities    and     other
corporations, all municipalities, town panchayats
and Cantonment Boards, namely, St.Thomas
Mount and Wellington are under the category of
Non-Million Plus Cities.



                              74
                              74
       The Union Government is providing tied
and untied grants to Non-Million Plus ULBs on
the     basis      of      15th        Finance           Commission
recommendations. For Million Plus Cities, funds
for solid waste management and ambient air
quality are being provided.

       The        15th     Finance            Commission                has
recommended grants on the basis of population
to Cantonment boards also. The details of funds
released for the years from 2022-23 to 2024-25
are furnished below:

Table 2.16
                                                             (Rs. in crore)

                         2022-23           2023-24             2024-25

                    Allocation Received Allocation Received Allocation Received

2.14.1 Million        Plus     Urban       Agglomeration
       Cities
      Larger cities will have tendency to grow
faster and grants are provided with special
emphasis on meeting the challenges of
improving the measures of ambient air quality,
improving conservation of ground water and
efficient solid waste management and sanitation.
Fifteenth Finance Commission has recommended
only tied grants for million plus cities, which are
disbursed on fulfilling certain criteria.
      Government of India has appointed the
Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs (MoHUA)
and Ministry of Environment Forest and Climate
Change     as    the   nodal   ministries  for
recommending the grants for water, sanitation,
solid waste management and ambient air

                              76
                              76
quality. These ministries have fixed service level
bench     marks   and     certain   criteria   for
recommending disbursal of grants to such cities.

      Under the solid waste management
component, as per MoU, the eligible works like
water   bodies  rejuvenation,   waste   water
treatment, water supply, procurement of
vehicles, solid waste management through
outsourcing and other works have been taken up
at an estimated cost of Rs.126.20 crore during
the financial year 2023-24 and funds tied up for
19      works     in    the    financial   year
2024-25.

      During 2023-24, under Ambient Air Quality
Fund, laying of end-to-end roads and 25 number
of other works at an estimated cost of
Rs.22.56 crore have been sanctioned. For the
year 2024-25, 5 works have been taken up at an
estimated cost of Rs.18.68 crore.

2.14.2 Non-Million Plus Cities
      60% of the basic grants are tied and can
be used for the basic services of (a) sanitation
and maintenance of open defecation free (ODF)
status and (b) supply of drinking water, rain

                       77
                       77
water harvesting and water recycling. As far as
possible, the local bodies shall earmark one half
of these tied grants to each of these two critical
services. However, if any local body has fully
saturated the needs in one category, fund can
be utilized for other category.

      Balance 40% of the basic grants are
untied, which may be used for any component,
which are location–specific development like
regulation of land use, urban planning, fire
services, water supply, public health and
sanitation,    roads,    drains    and     culverts,
improvements of burial grounds, slaughter
houses     and    tanneries    regulation,    public
amenities    including streetlighting, parking
spaces, bus stops, etc., except for salary or
other establishment expenditure.

     Under Tied Grant, for financial year
2023-24,   water supply,   urban    forest,
procurement of vehicles and solid waste
management works through outsourcing have
been taken up. A total of 611 works have
been taken up at an estimated cost of
Rs.216.07 crore.


                        78
                        78
      Under Untied Grant component, for the
financial year 2023-24, laying of roads and other
works have been taken up. A total of 435 works
have been taken up at an estimated cost of
Rs.254.58 crore.
     Under Tied Grant, for the financial year
2024-25, water supply, procurement of vehicles
and     solid  waste    management   through
outsourcing have been taken up. A total of
103 works have been taken up at an estimated
cost of Rs.89.18 crore.

     Under Untied Grant for financial year
2024-25, laying of roads and other works have
been taken up. A total of 127 works have
been taken up       at   an    estimated        cost   of
Rs.177.45 crore.

2.15 Tamil Nadu Institute                 of     Urban
     Studies (TNIUS)
      The   Tamil   Nadu      Institute    of     Urban
Studies (TNIUS), Coimbatore was established in
1981, with a view to achieve greater efficiency
in the functioning of the administration of urban
local bodies. The following are the objectives of
the Institute:

                         79
                         79
 •   To create awareness on latest management
     techniques in various branches of municipal
     administration.

 •   To disseminate knowledge on urban issues
     and urban development through training
     programmes,        seminars, conferences,
     publications, etc.

 •   To     conduct   research    studies     and
     consultancy services in urban development,
     municipal    administration   and    related
     disciplines.

 •   To assist in the development of urban
     infrastructure.

     The annual contributions collected from the
municipal corporations, municipalities and town
panchayats constitute a major source of revenue
for the operation of Tamil Nadu Institute of
Urban Studies.

2.15.1 In-service and        Special   Training
       Programme
     During the year 2024, the Institute
conducted 5 batches of one month residential
‘Foundation Training Programme’ for the Junior

                       80
                       80
Assistants of corporations in order to enhance
job role through developing their hard and soft
skills.

       The Institue conducted three days
pre-retirement training program in four batches,
tailored for officials nearing retirement, so as to
ensure smooth        transition   and   knowledge
transfer,   while   providing    guidance     on
post-retirement opportunities and challenges.

2.15.2 Councillors’ Training Programme

      The Institute is conducting Training
Programme to the councillors of corporations,
municipalities and town panchayats on Tamil
Nadu Urban Local Bodies Act, 1998 and Rules,
2023 and matters relating to council functions,
duties and responsibilities, revenue collections,
municipal services, including town planning and
human resource development.

2.15.3 Infrastructure        Development        at
       Institute
        In order to enhance the infrastructure
facilities of the institute, it is proposed to
construct additional class rooms, seminar halls,


                        81
                        81
labs etc., at an estimated cost of Rs.35.00 crore
through Coimbatore municipal corporation as a
deposit work. It is proposed to complete the
works in two years time.

2.16 State Special Schemes
2.16.1 Chief Minister’s Breakfast Scheme
      The      Morning      Breakfast       Scheme        was
launched by the Hon'ble Chief Minister on
15.09.2022 in Madurai corporation to provide
morning breakfast to students from 1st standard
to   5th    standard.      Initially      implemented      in
15 corporations and 23 municipalities, then this
scheme has been extended to the remaining
5 corporations, 115 municipalities and 62 town
panchayats on 25.08.2023.

      As     on    date,     the    scheme        is   under
implementation in 2,256 schools within the
jurisdiction      of   220        urban     local      bodies
(municipalities,        corporations            and     town
panchayats)       in   which       food    is    served    to
2,00,472 students. Rs.118.89 crore has been
sanctioned from the financial year 2022-23 to till



                             82
                             82
date and the activities of the scheme are
monitored through a mobile application.

Table 2.17

       Total No.           G.O.(Ms)No.33, Dated 07.06.2023
Total
           of    No. of Schools in No. of Schools Total No.
No. of                                                      Total No. of
       Common Corporations and        in Town        of
ULBs                                                         Students
       kitchens   Municipalities    panchayats Schools

 220      163          2,039            217        2,256     2,00,472



      Due to the successful implementation of
the scheme, the attendance of the students in all
schools has increased, which in turn resulted in
increase in literacy percentage of poor students.
The huge success of Hon'ble Chief Minister's
breakfast     scheme     has    impressed    the
neighbouring and other states in the country
which led to seek advice from the Government
regarding implementation of the prestigious
scheme in the respective states.

     Under this scheme, by 5.00 a.m.
preparation of food begins in common kitchens
and completed by 6.30 a.m. The cooked food is
filled in covered hot boxes and sent to schools
through a safety vehicle. Food vehicle will depart
from kitchen by 7.00 a.m. and the hot boxes are

                                  83
                                  83
handed over to the school incharge by 8.30 a.m.
Food is distributed to students before 9.00 a.m.
daily.

2.16.2 Sanitation    Workers’      Development
       Scheme
     The Sanitation Workers’ Development
Scheme is a flagship scheme launched by the
Hon'ble Chief Minister on 09.12.2022 for the
enhanced safety and security of the sanitation
workers, for the socio economic development
and alternative livelihood of the sanitation
workers and their dependents.

     The    scheme    aims    to   provide   skill
development training to the workers and their
dependents of all the formal and informal
workers who are in touch with faecal matters in
Urban Local Bodies and also in the private sector
and institutions.

     An enumeration was conducted in all
urban local bodies with the technical support of
Urban Management Centre, Ahmedabad and as
on date, 95,077 sanitation workers have been
enumerated.



                       84
                       84
      The identified core sanitation workers will
be given appropriate training and action is being
taken for the converge by the eligible schemes
available   in   other   departments     of   the
Government.

2.17 Town Planning       - Single Window
     Portal and Self Certification Scheme
      In order to address the needs of common
public and to ease the process of building plan
approval, the Government has introduced online
services for submission of building application by
simplifying the process for submission of
documents related to approval of building plans
through online. As soon as the building
application file is approved, the demand for
payment of fees is generated and sent to
applicant directly through SMS and e-mail and
the applicant is expected to remit such fee also
through online mode only. Since, the building
plan approval is an end to end online service
without human intervention, there is no need for
public to visit the offices of municipality/
corporation under any circumstances.




                       85
                       85
      A Common Single Window Portal has been
developed for issue of planning permission by
Directorate of Town and Country Planning and
Chennai Metropolitan Development Authority for
the construction of buildings exceeding the
powers delegated to the local bodies and
subsequently to issue building permits by local
bodies through online, has been implemented
from 21.08.2023.

       As a result of this, service system delays
in issuing building permits are avoided and issue
of final orders are ensured within the stipulated
time.

       Further,    G.O.(Ms)No.94,      Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 18.07.2024 has provided the way for
obtaining building permissions online quickly by
the citizens themselves on self-certification
basis.

     Following   this   order,   a   new   module
integrated in the single window system has been
introduced on 19.07.2024 to facilitate sanction
of    building     permissions       based     on



                        86
                        86
self-certification,   without   requirement    of
completion certificate.

      As per this scheme, plot sizes upto
2,500 sq.ft. and built-up area upto 3,500 sq.ft.
for construction of residential structures having
ground or ground plus one floor are eligible for
instant approval. So far, 10,286 buildings in
corporations    and     4,770   buildings  in
municipalities, totally 15,056 buildings have
been given approval through the online portal
upto 18.03.2025.

      Application and procedure for obtaining
planning permission for residential structures
has been simplified. Also, delays in obtaining
planning permission are avoided to a large
extent.

2.19.1 Madurai Corporation

         Madurai Corporation was constituted on
01.05.1971 and the extent of the corporation is
147.99          sq.km.        As      per     2011        census,         the
population           was           15,73,616            and        current
population          is     20,40,000          approximately.              The
corporation has 100 wards. For administrative
convenience, the corporation area is divided into
5 zones.




                                      89
                                      89
Water Supply
    The total water requirement of the city is
265 MLD and currently 160 MLD of drinking
water is supplied once in every two days to
86 wards and once in every 3 days to the
remaining 14 wards.

Underground Sewerage
      Out of 100 wards, 72 wards have
underground sewerage facility. In 22 wards of
the newly added vaigai vadakarai area,
44,974 connections have been provided under
smart cities mission. Further, administrative
sanction has been accorded at an estimated cost
of Rs.471.89 crore for underground sewerage
work in left out areas and the works are in
progress.

Solid Waste Management
     Out of the total 870 MT of solid waste
generated daily, 522 MT of wet waste is
composted at vellakkal solid waste mangement
centre, 38 micro composting centres and Onsite
Composting Centres.




                      90
                      90
Streetlights
       Madurai Corporation has a total of 60,729
streetlights.   53,282      of   them are    LED
streetlights. At present 7,447 numbers of other
streetlights have been converted as LED lights.
Also, 451 new LED streetlights are being
installed at high traffic roads.

Roads
      The city has a total of 11,149 number of
roads for a length of 1,686.80 km. During the
last 3 years, 4,680 number of road works at an
estimated cost of Rs.427.21 crore were
sanctioned under various schemes covering
802.89 km. Out of which, 779.54 km works have
been completed and the remaining works are in
progress.

       At present, 398 damaged roads for a
length of 69.72 km have been taken up for
restoration at a cost of Rs.44.98 crore and the
works will be commenced soon.

Gasifier Crematorium
     Construction     of    7    new     gasifier
crematoriums    at   an    estimated   cost    of


                      91
                      91
Rs.9.10 crore has been completed and put into
public use.

Library cum Knowledge Centre
      Library   cum    Knowledge  Centre   at
Tamukkam Ground at an estimated cost of
Rs.2.50 crore has been completed and put into
use for the students and public.

Parks
      Madurai Corporation has a total of
118 parks, out of which 37 parks have been
developed     and    put  into    public   use.
10 parks are under construction at an estimated
cost of Rs.4.93 crore.

Bus Stands
      The corporation has 4 operating bus
stands, namely, M.G.R. integrated bus stand
(class – A) in Mattuthavani area, Omni bus stand
in Mattuthavani, Periyar bus stand and
Arappalayam bus stand which are in use for city
buses.

Bio-Mining
     Under Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0 the work
of   processing 4,86,523    metric   tons  of

                      92
                      92
accumulated solid waste at vellakkal landfill site
using Bio-Mining method at an estimated cost of
Rs.34.95 crore is in progress.

2.19.2 Coimbatore Corporation
     Coimbatore Corporation was constituted
on 01.05.1981. The present area of the
Corporation is 257.04 sq.km. The population as
per 2011 census was 16,04,925 and the present
population is 22,88,052. There are 100 wards in
the Corporation.

Water Supply

     In this Corporation, 5 drinking water
schemes have been designed to extract 447.75
million litres of water daily from the Siruvani,
Aliyar and Bhavani rivers. The city's daily water
demand is approximately 308.46 million litres
and water supplied per day is 290 million litres.
Out of 5,77,553 households, 3,16,394 house
service connections have been provided.

Underground Sewerage

     There are 4 sewage treatment plants with
a total capacity of 200.53 MLD in the
corporation. A total of 1,14,074 sewerage


                       93
                       93
connections have been provided so far out of the
total 5,77,553 number of households. Out of the
total 100 wards, 38 wards have sewerage
facilities. Additionally, underground sewage
works are being carried out in 47 wards.

Solid Waste Management

       The total solid waste generated in the city
daily is 950 to 1100 TPD. Of this, 605 MT is wet
waste, and the rest is dry waste. The
Corporation has 22 micro compost centres to
process wet waste and 19 resource recovery
centres to handle dry waste.

Roads
      The city has a total of 3,212 km of roads.
During 2022-23 and 2023-24, 328.77 km and
335.05 km of road works have been completed.
During 2024-25, 1,269 roads with a length of
196.87 km have been taken up at an estimated
cost of Rs. 100.95 crore, and 1,200 roads with a
length of 182.86 km have been completed and
the remaining road works are in progress.




                       94
                       94
Streetlights

      The corporation areas are provided with
1,04,160 streetlights. Additionally, detailed
project report has been prepared for the
sanction of 5,497 new LED streetlights under the
Nirbhaya Scheme, at an estimated cost of
Rs.23.00 crore.

2.19.3 Tiruchirappalli Corporation
     Tiruchirappalli corporation was constituted
on 01.06.1994 and the extent of the corporation
as on date is 167.23 sq.km. The population as


                        98
                        98
per 2011 census was 9,15,569 and present
population is estimated as 10,81,234. The
corporation has 65 wards and divided into
5 zones for administrative purposes.

Water Supply
      The daily water requirement for the
current population is 146 MLD and is being
supplied daily.

Underground Sewerage
      The city has a sewage treatment plant
with a capacity of 58.64 MLD. 55.00 MLD of
sewage generated daily is treated in this plant
and discharged into Korai river. So far, a total of
69,671 house service connections have been
provided out of the total 2.74 lakh households.
Underground drainage facility has been provided
for    26    wards     out    of    the    total
65 wards. Works are being carried out for
providing underground drainage network in the
remaining wards under AMRUT Phase II, III
and IV.

      Further, under AMRUT 2.0 underground
drainage works are being carried out in the left


                        99
                        99
out 8 wards (Phase IV) at an estimated cost
of Rs.230.61 crore.

      Construction of 100 MLD capacity sewage
treatment plant using modern SBR Technology
in Panjappur at an estimated cost of
Rs.233.87 crore is in progress.

Solid Waste Management
     Out of the 470 MT of solid waste
generated daily, 256 MT of wet waste is
composted at 40 composting centers. 5 resource
recovery centers are functioning for collection
and processing the remaining 214 MT of dry
waste.

Streetlights
       Under the smart cities mission 8,264
sodium metal halide/CFL lights have been
replaced with LED lights at a cost of
Rs.23.40 crore and 816 new LED lights have
been installed. Further, works are in progress to
install 6,264 new LED lights in the left out places
with    funding    under    the   State    Urban
Infrastructure Development Fund (SUIDF). All
43,284 streetlights in the corporation have been
converted in to energy-saving LED lights.

                       100
                       100
Roads
      The city has 1,352.811 km of roads. Out of
these, 1,494 number of roads with a length of
165.10 km damaged due to underground
sewerage projects, drinking water improvement
works and natural calamities are being restored
in 2024-25 at an estimated cost of Rs.95.06
crore. 1,425 number of works have been
completed and the remaining 69 works are in
progress.

Parks
      Under Namakku Naame Thittam 2023-24,
beautification of park at Warnes road in ward
no.53 was taken up at an estimated cost of
Rs.1.00 crore. The work is completed and put
into public use.

Bus stands
      In Tiruchirappalli Corporation, Central Bus
Stand (Class-A) and Chathiram Bus Stand
(Class-B) are in use.

     In Panjappur, the Integrated Bus Terminus
along with Multipurpose Complex as well as
Truck Terminus and other infrastructure works


                      101
                      101
have been taken up at an estimated cost of
Rs.492.55 crore are nearing completion. The bus
terminal complex will be brought into public use
soon.

     Construction of Srirangam Bus Stand is
being carried out at an estimated cost of
Rs.11.10 crore under Capital Grant Fund
(2022-23).

      Construction of Mary's railway flyover
connecting Uraiyur Chathiram bus stand at an
estimated cost of Rs.34.10 crore under
Infrastructure and Amenities fund (2022-23) is
underway.

2.19.4 Tirunelveli Corporation
     Tirunelveli Corporation was constituted on
01.06.1994. The population as per 2011 census
was 4,74,838 and the present population is
estimated as 5,77,546. The corporation consists
of 55 wards with an extent of 108.65 sq.km and
divided   into   4   zones   for   administrative
convenience.




                      102
                      102
Water Supply

       The drinking water supply demand of the
city for the current population is 83 MLD.

Underground Sewerage
      The corporation has a sewage treatment
plant with a treatment capacity of 24.2 MLD in
which 12.00 MLD of sewage generated daily is
being treated. So far, 27,464 house service
connections have been provided out of a total of
1,73,031 households. Out of the total 55 wards,
10 wards have underground drainage facility.
Further, underground drainage works are being
carried out in all the remaining wards under
AMRUT.

Solid Waste Management

     Of the total 200 to 222 MT of solid waste
generated, 133 MT of wet waste is composted at
47 micro-composting centers and the remaining
89 MT of dry waste is collected and processed at
the Ramayanpatti landfill.

Streetlights
      The city has a total of 31,214 LED
streetlights. Under 15th Finance Commission

                       103
                       103
grant (2022-23) installation of 1,968 new
streetlights have been taken up, of which 1,661
have been installed. The work of installing the
remaining 307 new streetlights is in progress.

Roads
     The city has a total of 935.66 km of roads.
During 2023-24, 511 number of roads with a
length of 94.069 km have been taken up at an
estimated cost of Rs.42.80 crore and 84.608 km
of road works have been completed. The
remaining 9.461 km of road works are in
progress.

     During 2024-25, 386 roads with a length
of 82.639 km have been taken up at an
estimated cost of Rs.43.90 crore and 40.36 km
of road works have been completed and the
remaining 42.279 km of road works are in
progress.

Crematorium
     Modernisation of 2 gasifier crematoriums
at a cost of Rs.1.00 crore has been completed
and are functional. Work on construction of one
more gasifier crematorium at an estimated cost

                     105

2.19.15 Tambaram Corporation
      Tambaram Corporation was constituted on
03.11.2021 and the extent of the Corporation is
87.64 sq.km. Population as per 2011 census is
7,23,017 and the current population is
11,79,884. The Corporation is divided into 70
wards for administrative convenience, which are
divided into 5 zones.

Water supply
      The corporation’s own water source is
Palaru basin, which provides 13.50 MLD of


                     136
                     136
drinking water; Chennai Metropolitan Water
Supply and Sewerage Board (CMWSSB) provides
14.50 MLD of drinking water; Tamil Nadu Water
Supply and Drainage Board provides 8.06 MLD
of drinking water; local water sources such as
open wells provide 8.34 MLD of drinking water;
and mini power pumps and water treatment
plant provide 24.60 MLD, making a total of
72.00 MLD of drinking water which is supplied at
the rate of 73 LPCD. The total water requirement
of the city is 159.28 MLD. Out of a total of
2,94,791 households, 1,83,472 households have
been provided with house service connections.

Underground Sewerage
      Out of the total 70 wards in the
corporation, 38 wards have underground
sewerage facilities. Underground sewerage work
is being carried out in 11 wards. A sewage
treatment plant with a capacity of 30 MLD is in
operation in West Tambaram area, in which 16
MLD of sewage generated daily is treated and
discharged   into   the   Adyar   River.   In
Pallavapuram area, 12 MLD of sewage generated
daily is treated at the Perungudi sewage


                      137
                      137
treatment plant by the Chennai Metropolitan
Water Supply and Sewerage Board. 85,650
households   have    been    provided with
underground sewerage connections.

Solid Waste Management
      The total solid waste generated in the city
is 350 to 400 TPD. Of this, 160 TPD is wet waste
and the rest is dry waste. There are 32 micro-
composting centres for processing wet waste
and 5 resource recovery centres for handling dry
waste. Further, setting up of a 150 MT capacity
compressed biogas (Bio CNG) tank has been
taken up at Venkatamangalam landfill.

Streetlights
      The city has a total of 46,536 streetlights.
Of these, 37,803 non-LED streetlights have been
converted to LED lights. With this, all the lights
have been converted to LED streetlights.

Roads

      The total length of roads in the city is
980 km. During 2021-22, 2022-23 and 2023-24,
a total of 247.27 km of roads were taken up.
During 2024-25, 81.20 km of roads have been

                       138
                       138
taken up. During 2025-26, it is proposed to take
up road works for a length of 210 km at an
estimated cost of Rs.142.00 crore.

Crematorium

      A total of 10 gasifier crematoriums are
located within the limits of the corporation. In
addition, works are in progress to construct two
gasifier    crematoriums     under      Kalaignar
Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam (2024-25) at an
estimated cost of Rs.2.94 crore.

Library cum Knowledge Centre
      2    Library   cum    Knowledge    Centres
constructed    at   an     estimated   cost    of
Rs.4.00 crore are functioning since 2024.

Parks
     Corporation is maintaining 206 parks.
Under AMRUT 2.0, 8 works have been taken up
at an estimated cost of Rs.2.03 crore, 7 works
have been completed and 1 work is nearing
completion.




                      139
                      139
Daily Market
      3 daily markets are functioning in the
corporation. Further, under Kalaignar Nagarpura

and is currently in use. Further, construction of a
gasifier crematorium in Chengai Thoppu area in
ward no.41, at an estimated cost of
Rs.1.41 crore under Kalaignar Nagarpura
Mempattu Thittam (2022-23) is in progress.

Library cum Knowledge Centre
      Library   cum   Knowledge    Centre   at   an
estimated cost of Rs.1.94 crore taken up under
Kalaignar   Nagarpura     Mempattu     Thittam




                       167
                       167
(2022-23) has been completed and put into
use.

Parks
     Two works taken up at an estimated cost
of Rs.0.80 crore under Kalaignar Nagarpura
Mempattu Thittam (2022-23) and both the
works have been completed.

Bus Stand
      Construction of a new bus stand (Type A)
at an estimated cost of Rs.18.90 crore under
Kalaignar    Nagarpura    Mempattu     Thittam

Markets
      A daily market with 160 shops sanctioned
at an estimated cost of Rs.6.42 crore under
Kalaignar    Nagarpura    Mempattu     Thittam
(2022-23) has been completed.

3. Greater Chennai Corporation
      As the second oldest Municipal Corporation
in the world, the Greater Chennai Corporation
has a history of 337 years. The Chennai
Corporation through vibrant growth during
various periods of time has developed into an
modern and highly developed urban local
government in India.

       Chennai is a pioneer city with a blend of
classical heritage and modern innovations.
Chennai is a leader in most of the key
advancement areas such as education, health,
sanitation, technology, manufacturing etc., and
is a growth icon of the country.

      The population of Chennai metropolitan
city which spreads over an area of 426 sq.km is
66.72 lakh as per the 2011 census. The current
population is estimated to be around 91 lakh.

      The Greater Chennai Corporation, rich in
diverse    specialties,  is   divided     into
200 divisions for administrative purposes and is
managed under 15 zones.

                       169

3.1 Infrastructure
3.1.1 Roads
(i) Bus Route Roads and Interior Roads
     Greater Chennai Corporation maintains
35,128 no. of roads with a length of
5,688.57 km, which includes 488 bus route
roads with a length of 418.57 km and


                                     170
                                     170
34,640 interior   roads     with   a   length   of
5,270 km.

       The maintenance of bus route roads and
interior roads is taken up with the capital funds
of the Greater Chennai Corporation and the
grants provided under Tamil Nadu Urban Road
Infrastructure Programme (TURIP), Singara
Chennai 2.0, Nagarpura Salai Mempattu Thittam
(NSMT) and State Finance Commission (SFC)
grants.

     During the financial year 2023-24,
6,517 number of bus route roads and interior
roads to a length of 1,100.03 km have been
taken up at an estimated cost of Rs.707.21 crore
under Singara Chennai 2.0, TURIP, NSMT and
SFC grants. Out of these, 6,072 number of road
works are completed and the balance works are
under progress.

     During the financial year 2024-25,
3,276 number of bus route roads and interior
roads to a length of 580.62 km have been taken
up at an estimated cost of Rs.408.86 crore
under    various   schemes.   Out   of  these,



                      171
                      171
2,715 roads are completed and the balance
works are under progress.

(ii) Bus Shelters and Bus Terminus
      Greater Chennai Corporation maintains
1,558 bus shelters, built under various schemes,
including 637 bus shelters set up under a Build,
Operate, Transfer (BOT) model. During the
financial year 2024-25, 765 no. of bus shelters
will be improved and potential advertisement
space will be created through Public Private
Partnership    (PPP)  mode    on Rehabilitate,
Operate, Transfer (ROT) basis for a period of 8
years. Further, the bus bays at Ekkattuthangal,
Guindy Race Course, West Saidapet and bus
terminus at Manali, IOCL, Saligramam, Toll Gate
and Kolathur Kumaran Nagar will be renovated
under Member of Parliament Local Area
Development Scheme and Greater Chennai
Corporation capital funds.

3.1.3 Disaster management
3.1.3.1 Stormwater Drains
      Chennai city has been facing the challenge
of urban flooding during monsoon periods. To
mitigate the floods, the Greater Chennai
Corporation has been constructing stormwater
drains under various schemes viz., Singara
Chennai 2.0, Infrastructure and Amenities fund,
Flood Relief Fund, State Disaster Mitigation
Fund, etc. Further, construction of integrated
stormwater drains has been taken up under
Externally Aided Projects funded by the World
Bank, Asian Development Bank (ADB) and the
German Development Bank (KfW).

       The    Greater     Chennai Corporation
maintains 11,769 numbers of stormwater drains
for a length of 3,041 km.

3.1.3.2 Kosasthalaiyar Basin
     The    Kosasthalaiyar  Basin   Integrated
Stormwater Drain (ISWD) project, which is
funded by Asian Development Bank, has been
taken up to address the long-standing need for
stormwater drains in North Chennai. The
primary objective of this project is to mitigate
the hazard of flooding during monsoons. The
project involves the construction of integrated
stormwater drains in the Kosasthalaiyar basin,

                                  180
                                  180
for a length of 769 km. at an estimated cost of
Rs.3,059 crore. Work is in progress. So far,
88.39% of the works have been completed. The
remaining works will be completed soon. Upon
completion of the project, 30 lakh people living
in   Ambattur,      Manali,    Madhavaram,  and
Thiruvotriyur will benefitted.

3.1.3.3 Kovalam Basin
      Construction of integrated stormwater
drain in the kovalam basin has been taken up in
three phases at an estimated cost of
Rs.1,715.25 crore. In the first phase, integrated
stormwater drain works for a length of
41.42     km    at  an    estimated     cost   of
Rs.150.45 crore have been taken up. So far,
91.62% of the works have been completed. In
Phase II, integrated stormwater drain works
have been taken up for a length of 120.09 km at
an estimated cost of Rs.447.03 crore. So far,
85.49% of the works have been completed. In
Phase III, works for a length of 140.10 km at an
estimated cost of Rs.760.10 crore have been
taken up. On completion of this project, 20 lakh




                      181
                      181
people residing in Perungudi, Sholinganallur and
part of Alandur zones will be benefitted.

3.1.3.4 Construction of stormwater drain
        under Greater Chennai Corporation
        Capital Fund

      Construction of stormwater drain has been
taken up for a length of 22 km at an estimated
cost of Rs.110.67 crore and the works are in
progress. Further, 31.57 km length of
stormwater drain works at an estimated cost of
Rs.124.61 crore will be taken up.

3.1.3.5 Improvements to Canals

      As North-East monsoon preparedness, the
Greater Chennai Corporation has to enhance
flood protection in 35 out of 44 canals. For this,
the existing retaining walls will be raised with a
1.5 m chain link fencing. New retaining walls will
also be built wherever needed. The total
estimated cost of this initiative will be
Rs.256.17 crore.

3.1.3.6 Restoration of waterbodies

     Works for restoration and rejuvenation of
water bodies under the control of Greater

                       182
                       182
Chennai Corporation are taken up under various
scheme viz., restoration of 5 water bodies under
AMRUT    2.0    at    an   estimated   cost   of
Rs.14.84 crore, restoration of 20 water bodies
under Singara Chennai 2.0 (2023-24) at an
estimated cost of Rs.9.25 crore, restoration of
19 water bodies under capital fund at an
estimated cost of Rs.24.51 crore and restoration
of biodiversity and ecosystem services in
kadapakkam lake at an estimated cost of
Rs.58.33 crore. All 45 works are in progress.

3.1.4 Special Projects
3.1.4.1     Operation and Maintenance of
            Toilets under Public Private
            Partnership (PPP) mode
       This project is one of the iconic projects of
Hon’ble Chief Minister. Following the success and
learnings of the pilot project done in 372
locations with 3,270 seats in zones 5, 6 and
Marina, the public toilets in the remaining zones
are being taken up for comprehensive operation
and maintenance work, as follows:




                        183
                        183
Table 3.6
Package             Zones              No. of Locations No. of Seats

   2      1, 2, 3 & 4                        285           2301

   4      11, 12, 13,14 & 15                 322           2105

                               Total        1002           7166



      It is a 9-year “Design, Build, Finance,
Operate and Transfer” (DBFOT) project, in which
1 year will be given for designing, building and
commissioning and 8 years for operation and
maintenance. The project is structured as a
Hybrid    Annuity   Model   –    Public   Private
Partnership, monitored by third party agency
(Independent Engineers).
3.1.4.2 Blue Flag Beach
      The Blue Flag Beach programme promotes
sustainable development in freshwater and
marine areas. It challenges local authorities and
beach operators to achieve high standards in the
four    categories,     viz.   water      quality,
environmental    management,     environmental
education and safety. Over the years, the Blue
Flag has become a highly respected and
recognized eco-label, working to bring together

                                184
                                184
the tourism and environmental sectors at local,
regional and national levels. This project is
proposed at Marina.
3.1.4.3 Integrated Sponge Park
      The project upgrades the Manali MMDA
colony football grounds into an 1.9 acre
Integrated Sponge Park with a design strategy
that combines blue-green infrastructure and
social infrastructure.

3.1.7 e-Governance
       Greater Chennai Corporation provides the
following services to the public through its
website and digital communication platforms:
   •    Property tax payment
   •    Birth and death certificate issuance
   •    Urban planning-related information
   •    Public grievance redressal system
   •    WhatsApp services
   •    1913 Call Center, and
   •    Services related to the Namma Chennai
        mobile application
      Additionally, the Enterprise Resource
Planning (ERP) system of Greater Chennai
Corporation      facilitates      the       following
administrative functions:
   •    Finance – Digital financial management



                         189
                         189
  •    Project management
  •    Legal case management
  •    General administration
  •    Pension management
  •    Revenue management
      The ERP software is integrated with the
corporation's online services, Namma Chennai
app, and the 1913 control room. Currently,
Greater Chennai Corporation’s online payment
services are available through more than ten
banks and also through digital payment methods
such as BBPS and UPI.

   Furthermore, the corporation’s data is linked
with the Guidance Tamil Nadu Single Window
Portal, Chennai Metropolitan Water Supply and
Sewerage Board, and Tamil Nadu Registration
Department for land ownership, name transfer,
and other related services.

Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP 2.0)
     Since 2008, Greater Chennai Corporation
has been using a comprehensive Enterprise
Resource Planning (ERP) software. Considering
the increasing usage of online services, data-


                       190
                       190
sharing needs and technological advancements,
the ERP 2.0 project is proposed. This upgrade
aims to provide faster service, simpler
processes, enhanced transparency, and cost and
time efficiency.

     The Greater Chennai Corporation is taking
necessary measures to streamline tax collection
and     revenue  generation. ERP   2.0  will
significantly expand business operations to
improve user experience and operational
efficiency.

Key Features of ERP 2.0
•   Microservices-based digital infrastructure –
    Developed in accordance       with   modern
    technological standards.
•   Enhanced applications for public and
    corporation officials/employees to improve
    usability.
•   Geo-based revenue management system -
    To enhance revenue collection and identify
    and rectify revenue losses with a user-
    friendly interface.




                      191
                      191
•   Upgraded       security,     efficiency,   and
    accessibility using the latest technology.
•   Improved service efficiency for the public
    through       modern          technological
    advancements.
•   Inter-departmental data-sharing framework
    to  integrate    with      other   government
    departments.
•   Dedicated online services for corporation
    officials, pensioners,    contractors,   legal
    service    providers     handling     Chennai
    Corporation cases, and licensed surveyors.
•   AI/ML-based real-time dashboard for data
    analysis and monitoring.
•   Disaster recovery facility to ensure data
    security and operational continuity.
