3.1.8.1        Parks  and   Playfields -    New
               Developments (OSR Lands)
       During the financial year 2024-25,
development of new parks in the vacant open
space reservation (OSR) lands vested with
Greater Chennai Corporation has been taken up.
All the newly developed parks are designed as
differently-abled friendly. Further, the new parks
will have minimal use of concrete and plentiful
greenery so that they act as sponge parks in the
rainy season.

      Under Singara Chennai 2.0 from 2021-22
to 2024-25, a total of 206 parks and 110
playfield works have been sanctioned at an
estimated cost of Rs.80.66 crore and Rs.45.36
crore, respectively. Out of which, 186 park and
97 playfield works were completed at an
estimated    cost  of    Rs.65.10   crore   and
Rs.38.37 crore, respectively. Balance works are
in progress.

                            194
                            194
3.1.8.2 Urban Greening
      Greater Chennai Corporation is making
sustained efforts to increase the green cover in
Chennai city by planting native tree saplings in
various    parts    of    the    city.    Greater
Chennai    Corporation     has    planted    over
3,74,082 trees between the year 2022 to
2024. It is proposed to plant 1 lakh tree saplings
with community participation and Resident
Welfare Associations.

3.2 Health and Sanitation
3.2.1 Solid Waste Management
      On an average, 6,150 MT of solid waste is
generated daily in Greater Chennai Corporation.
It is collected at the household level. The
source-segregated wet and dry wastes are
channelized to the decentralized processing
facilities and the remaining waste is transferred
to the dumping sites at Perungudi and
Kodungaiyur.

       At present, solid waste management
activities are being carried out in 5 out of
15 zones by Greater Chennai Corporation and in



                       195
                       195
the remaining      10   zones      through     private
concessionaires.

3.2.1.1 Infrastructure
       Solid Waste Management involves the
collection of waste at the point of waste
generation, transportation to the respective
processing centers, and further processing.

      For carrying out these activities, the
Greater    Chennai     Corporation  operates
2,457 battery- operated vehicles (BOVs),
172 HMV/LMV compactors, 54 HMV/LMV tippers,
14 Mechanical sweepers etc. Likewise, the
private concessionaires operate 3,529 BOVs,
148 HMV/LMV compactors, 147 HMV/LMV
tippers, and 45 mechanical sweepers.

      In    Greater    Chennai    Corporation
4,060 permanent employees, 424 temporary
workers and 4,989 self help group workers are
engaged for Solid Waste Management activities.
Further, the private operators have engaged
12,249 workers for            carrying   out    these
conservancy operations.




                        196
                        196
3.2.1.2 Wet and Dry Waste Processing
       In line with Solid Waste Management
Rules, 2016, the Greater Chennai Corporation
initiated decentralized facilities to process the
source- segregated wet and dry waste.

        At present, 28 wet waste processing
facilities such as micro composting centers,
windrow composting centers, Bio-CNG plants,
and Garden waste processing centers have a
capacity of handling 576 metric tons per day of
wet waste. From the wet waste processing units,
presently, 720 tons of manure is produced every
month and 2,428 MT Bio CNG gas has been
produced from till date.

      Two Bio-CNG plants of 100 MT capacity
each, are in operation at Chetpet and
Madhavaram. Additional five Bio-CNG plants,
with a total capacity of 500 MT are being set up
to process the wet waste and are under various
stages of construction. The details of the wet
waste   processing   facilities   are   as   given   in
Table 3.9.

3.2.1.3 Disposal            of     legacy          waste        (Bio-
        Mining)
(a) Perungudi
     The Perungudi site located in the southern
part of the city is spread over an area of
225.16 acre and has been in use for more than
35 years as a disposal site. Solid waste from
Zones 9 to 15 is sent to the Perungudi site.

         The estimated volume of legacy waste
above ground level is around 30.61 lakh cubic
meters. The Greater Chennai Corporation has
initiated     reclamation         of   the        Perungudi      site
through         the bio-mining               process        under
6 packages, at a project cost of Rs.350.65 crore,
under the Swachh Bharat Mission (SBM). Bio-
mining work is in progress. So far, around


                                 199
                                 199
24.34 lakh cubic meters of legacy waste have
been    processed;      this    is     expected    to   be
completed soon. Further, an additional quantity
of 2.5 lakh cubic meters of waste will be taken
up for bio-mining under the Swachh Bharat
Mission 2.0 fund. After completion of the project,
200 acre of land will be reclaimed. The reclaimed
land is proposed to be used for setting up of
Integrated Waste Processing Facilities (IWPF)

(b) Kodungaiyur

       The   Kodungaiyur        site    located    in   the
northern part of the city, has an extent of
342.93 acre and has been in use for more than
40 years. Zones 1 to 8 use this facility. The
estimated    quantity    of    legacy     waste,    above
ground level and 2 meter below the ground
level, is around 66.52 lakh MT. Bio-mining work
is started at Kodungaiyur dumping ground at a
cost of Rs.648.38 crore under Swachh Bharat
Mission and KfW funds and is in progress. So far,
around 5,39,280 MT of legacy waste has been
processed.


                          200
                          200
3.2.1.4 Construction   and   Demolition
        (C & D) Waste Management

        Presently, the Construction and Demolition
waste collection in Chennai is around 800 MT.
To improve the service delivery, the collection
and      transportation        of    construction        and
demolition     waste    has been outsourced and
operations      have      commenced.            And      also
15 designated locations has been allotted by
Greater          Chennai             Corporation           to
dump construction         and        demolition       waste.
        Greater Chennai Corporation has installed
two facilities for processing construction and
demolition waste on Public-Private Partnership
(PPP) mode, one at Kodungaiyur and another
one     at   Perungudi,    which      have     started   the
processing. So far 4,84,066 MT of construction
and demolition waste has been processed with
an average of 250 MT in each location daily.
Final    products      such     as    coarse     and     fine
aggregates are used in construction work.




                              201
                              201
3.2.1.5 Mechanical Infrastructure
     The Mechanical Engineering Department
has a total fleet strength of 3,355 vehicles,
which include vehicles for transportation of solid
waste and construction and demolition waste,
such as battery-operated vehicles, Compactor,
HMV/LMV tippers, Frontend loaders, Bulldozers
and Hydraulic excavators, etc.

      Greater Chennai Corporation also has
special equipments such as 2 numbers of
amphibian vehicles, 6 numbers of Robotic
Multipurpose excavators, 3 numbers of Mini
amphibians for clearing floating garbage and
water hyacinth in canals, and 7 numbers of
Vehicle-mounted high capacity Suction cum
Jetting machines with water recycling facility for
clearing storm water drains. 9 numbers of
hydraulic-operated tree-pruning machines are
also deployed to remove overhanging tree
branches on a need basis and to clear fallen
trees, poles etc., during disasters. To clear the
sandy area in beaches, and make it litter- free,
9 numbers of tractor-driven beach cleaning
machines are deployed at Marina Beach, Elliots


                       202
                       202
Beach at Besant Nagar, Palavakkam Beach, and
other  beaches    in  the   Greater  Chennai
Corporation area.

      Further, 59 numbers of Mechanical
Sweepers (including concessionaire vehicles) are
used to clean the sand accumulated on the
roads.

       To increase the efficiency of door-to-door
collection of garbage, a huge fleet containing
5,966 numbers of battery – operated vehicles
(including concessionaire vehicles) are being
deployed in Zones I to XV.

3.2.2 Public Health
3.2.2.1 Public Health Services

      The Public Health Department provides
comprehensive public health, preventive and
treatment services to the people of Chennai city
including healthcare services, vector control and
implementation of various National programs,
registration of births and deaths. Public Health
Department also maintains shelters for the
urban homeless. It has the regulatory function
to implement the Tamil Nadu Urban Local Bodies


                      203
                      203
Act, 1998 and the Tamil Nadu Public Health Act
for disease prevention.

3.2.2.2 Primary Health Care services
      The Greater Chennai Corporation has a
well-established health  infrastructure that
provides comprehensive health care services,
such as Antenatal and Postnatal care, Child
health care, Basic diagnostics and treatment for
communicable and non-communicable diseases
under one roof. The health care services are
provided through a network of 140 Urban
Primary Health Centres (UPHC), 140 Urban
Health and Wellness Centers, 16 Urban
Community Health Centres (UCHC) and 3
Maternity hospitals (EOC).

3.2.2.3 Urban Health and Wellness Centres

      The Fifteenth Finance Commission has
provided grants of Rs.88.30 crore to establish
Health and Wellness Centers. In the 1st Phase,
140 Urban Health and Wellness Centres
(UHWCs) have been established. The Health and
Wellness Centers were inaugurated by the
Hon’ble Chief Minister on 06.06.2023. These
Centers function from 8.00 AM to 12.00 Noon
and 4.00 PM to 8.00 PM. 29,50,378 number of
patients benefited till date.

3.2.2.4 National    Quality         Assurance
        Standards (NQAS)
      In order to strengthen the quality of
services provided in the Urban Primary Health
Centers, it is proposed to get National Quality
Accreditation Certification from the Government
of India. So far, 25 Urban Primary Health
Centers have been certified under National
Quality Assurance Standards.

3.2.2.5 Dialysis Centers
      At present 9 Dialysis Centers are
functioning under Chief Minster Health Insurance
Scheme in Greater Chennai Corporation. From

                      205
                      205
01.04.2024      to   28.02.2025,    108 Dialysis
machines are functioning and 410 patients have
been dialysis in rotation basis. And 44,411
dialysis cycle in rotation were done.

3.2.2.6 Polyclinics
       Polyclinics are  “Outpatient Specialty
Clinics” established in Urban Primary Health
Centers. The GCC is operating 40 Polyclinics.
They provide specialty services on fixed days.
The services include Consultation and Lab
Investigations. The services are provided from
4.30 p.m. to 8.30 p.m. Total number of
beneficiaries during January 2024 to December
2024 is 1,72,344.

3.2.2.7 Communicable Diseases Hospital
      The Communicable Diseases Hospital at
Tondiarpet is an exclusive hospital for
communicable diseases. This hospital has been
functioning since 1914. This is a 360-bedded
hospital with out-patient and in-patient services
functioning 24 hours.

       The details of in-patient and out-patient
who have been treated from 2021 to 2024 are
as follows:

                        207
                        207
3.2.2.8 Disease Control Programme
(a) Makkalai Thedi Maruthuvam
     "Makkalai Thedi Maruthuvam" (MTM)
scheme was launched by the Government of
Tamil Nadu on August 5, 2021 in Zone 6 and
subsequently to all the remaining 14 zones of
Greater Chennai Corporation. This program
offers comprehensive "Home Based Health Care
Services" to ensure continuity of care and meet
the health needs of beneficiaries.
      Field teams comprising Women Health
Volunteers (WHVs), Palliative Staff Nurses and
Physiotherapists deliver    services   at  the
doorsteps. WHVs provide screening, follow-up
including Drug Box Delivery, referrals and
community health meetings. Physiotherapists
and palliative care nurses offer home-based
services to various beneficiaries, including the
elderly, cancer patients and those with chronic
illnesses including end stage renal disease.

      Screening and treatment for oral, breast,
and cervical cancers have been conducted. The
number of patients identified and treated with
oral cancer, breast cancer and cervical cancer

                      208
                      208
are 5 cases (Oral), 35 cases (Breast) and
20 cases (Cervical) in March, 2022 has increased
to 482 cases (Oral), 829 cases (Breast) and
295 cases (Cervical) of cancer, respectively, in
2023-24.

      Physiotherapy and palliative care services
are   provided to a significant number of
beneficiaries. From January 2024 till January
2025, 15,859 patients received physiotherapy
treatment. 9,370 patients received palliative
care and 78 patients received CAPD bags.

3.2.2.9 Birth and Death Registration

        Birth and death certificates have been
made     available       online     and   free     of    cost
(www.chennaicorporation.gov.in)                         since
07.09.2008. All the birth and death records,
since the year 1930, have been computerized in
GCC.     Birth   /   Death    certificates       are    made
available online in the official website of GCC
(www.chennaicorporation.gov.in)               and        can
be downloaded at free of cost. During the year
2022,      42,46,751        birth     certificates       and
15,82,041 death certificates; during the year
2023,      46,04,976        birth     certificates       and
14,92,284 death certificates; during the year
2024,      43,01,961        birth     certificates       and
12,57,807        death     certificates      have       been
generated.




                            212
                            212
      A     total    of    201    burial    grounds    are
functioning under the control of the Greater
Chennai Corporation in Zones 1 to 15. Out of
these, burial services only are available in 93
burial grounds and Cremation services only are
available in 51 burial grounds.

      Burial and cremation services done in
these burial grounds are provided free of cost.

3.2.2.10      Vector Control Activities
Controlling Mosquito borne diseases and
Zoonotic diseases
     In order to control Mosquito borne and
Zoonotic diseases 1,044 permanent workers and
2,446 temporary workers, totally 3,490 workers
are engaged to carry out the mosquito control
activities. About 17 lakh houses in chennai city
are divided into sectors, with about 500 houses
per sector.

      To     carryout       anti-mosquito      activities,
logistics           were          deployed            using
94     vehicle-mounted            fogging      machines,
324 portable fogging machines. For anti-larval
activity 322 compression sprayers, 156 battery

                            213
                            213
sprayers, 2 mini fogging machines and 54 power
sprayers and 15 vehicle mounted sprayers are
used to spray larvicide in stormwater drains,
open drains and canals. Drones are deployed to
spray larvicide in major waterways like Cooum
river, Adyar river and Buckingham canal.

      Amphibian, Mini Amphibian and Robotic
Excavators are used in rivers and canals for
desilting and removing of water hyacinth plants
for mosquito larval control activities. 9 Fiber
boats are used for removing floating materials
and water hyacinth plants followed by spraying
of larvicides.

3.2.2.12 Veterinary Activities
      The population of stray dogs in Greater
Chennai Corporation areas is kept under control
by conducting Animal Birth Control Rules,2023.
Complaints from general public are attended by
catching stray dogs and transporting the dogs to
Animal Birth Control centers which are located at


                                    215
                                    215
Pulianthope, Lloyds colony, Kannammapet,
Meenambakkam and Sholinganallur. The dogs
undergo animal birth control surgery and after
post-operative care and Anti Rabies vaccination
stray dogs are released in the same area from
where they were caught. During 2023-24 and
2024-25 (upto February 2025) 41,392 numbers
of stray dogs were caught and 30,847 numbers
of stray dogs were sterilized and vaccinated
against Rabies. Pet clinics are functioning at
TVK Nagar, Nungambakkam, Kannamapettai,
Meenambakkam, Llyods colony and Pulianthope
to provide free treatment and Anti Rabies
vaccination of pet animals. From the year 2023
and upto February 2025, 65,168 numbers of pet
animals have been treated and 9,304 numbers
of Pet licenses have been issued to pet owners.

      In order to control stray cattle in Greater
Chennai Corporation, each zone is allotted with
trained catchers and cattle vehicle for catching
and transportation of stray cattle caught to
pounds functioning at Pudupet and Perambur.
The cattle caught and are impounded at pounds
are tagged and maintained until release. The
impounded cattle are released to the owners

                      216
                      216
after being fined. From the year 2023 and upto
February 2025, 5,885 number of stray dogs and
300 number of cattles were impounded.

      Modern cow sheds for the use of cattle
owners at all Zones, except Zones 6,7 and 13 is
being    constructed.     Modern    cow    shed
construction at Division 53 of Zone 5 is nearing
completion.

survey is completed for 200 wards. The work of

Chennai Corporation strives to provide quality

through Chennai schools.

schools 3,739 teachers, and 140 permanent
non-teaching staff under its administration,
catering to 1,12,788 students. Among these
schools, 272 Primary & Middle schools have
Kindergarten sections and 12,621 students are
receiving kindergarten education from these
schools.

      During the academic year 2024-25, the

students’ attendance, every year cash award of
Rs.1,000/- is given to students who secure
100% attendance in the academic year.

Greater Chennai Corporation, the following

3.4.2 Areas of Special Focus

       The goal of the ‘Ennum Ezhuthum’ mission
is to ensure that by 2025, all students of classes
1 to 5 in government schools in Tamil Nadu can
read with comprehension and possess basic
arithmetic skills. Hence, the Greater Chennai
Corporation has taken significant steps to
implement the Ennum Ezhuthum program in all
its Primary and Middle Schools with the aim of
enhancing the quality of teaching and learning.


                       228
                       228
      To enhance the ambience of the
classrooms and to keep the classrooms dust
free, all Greater Chennai Corporation Primary
and Middle Schools are provided with green
boards.

      In Greater Chennai Corporation schools,

9th standard are being trained in Karate in order
to groom them with confidence and courage.

      In Greater Chennai Corporation schools, as
an appreciation, the first 50 rank-holding
students in 10th standard Public Examination in
across all Chennai schools were taken on an
educational tour to ISRO Mahendragiri and
Nuclear Power Plant – Kudankulam. This was a
great learning experience for these students.

       Greater   Chennai    Corporation     has
introduced bridge courses to the students in all
Chennai schools across all subjects during the
first two months of every academic year to
address learning gaps that arise due to summer
vacation.
       Greater Chennai Corporation schools
effectively utilize the libraries, encouraging

3.4.6 Gender clubs
        The Gender and Policy Lab (GPL) is an
initiative by the Greater Chennai Corporation
(Greater Chennai Corporation) aimed at making
the city safer for women and promoting gender
mainstreaming. The Gender and Policy Lab's
goal is to improve women's access to
opportunities by enhancing safety and gender
responsiveness        in   public   spaces     and
transportation. Gender clubs are an extension of
this initiative, train the students on topics such
as     gender   roles,  adolescence,   healthy
relationships, gender equity, gender-based
violence etc. The program has been launched in
all Middle, High and Higher secondary schools

                       232
                       232
with each club consisting of 30 students per
school.

3.4.7 Community      outreach    and       Student
      counseling
     In 2024-25, 10 dedicated psychological
counselors have been appointed to provide
necessary psychological counseling to students
to address their psychological problems.

3.5 Vada Chennai Valarchi Thittam

      The Tamil Nadu Government has launched
ambitious Scheme "Vada Chennai Valarchi
Thittam", aimed at ensuring balanced and
equitable development across all regions of
Chennai city to uplift the underdeveloped areas
and integrate them into the city's growth
trajectory. The infrastructure projects are under
implementation in the North and Central Region
of the Greater Chennai Corporation.

     Under "Vada Chennai Valarchi Thittam",
the Greater Chennai Corporation has taken up
105    projects   at   a   estimated    cost   of
Rs.1,159.60 crore, out of which, 66 projects at a
estimated cost of Rs.921.43 crore are under
various stages of implementation with funds tied

                      233
                      233
up under various schemes, namely, Singara
Chennai 2.0, National Clean Air Program,
Namakku Naame Thittam, AMRUT 2.0, Member
of Parliament Local Area Development Fund,
CITIIS, Swachh Bharat Mission , Infrastructure
and Amenities Fund, Member of Legislative
Assembly Constituency Development Scheme
Fund and the own funds of Greater Chennai
Corporation. Further, 39 projects at a cost of
Rs.238.17 crore have already been tied-up
under    Chennai   Metropolitan   Development
Authority funds.

     Out of the total of 105 number of works,
27 works have been completed at an estimated
cost of Rs.35.78 crore and the balance works are
under progress.

                                 237
                                 237
4. Commissionerate of Town Panchayats
     Town Panchayats are medium and small
towns under Municipal Administration and Water
Supply Department. According to Section 243 Q
of the Indian Constitution, these towns are
categorized as Town Panchayats in Tamil Nadu.
These Town Panchayats play a significant role in
the economic development of nearby villages,
such as providing market facilities, employment
etc.
     Moreover, people working in big cities often
seek their residence in these medium towns.
Many Town Panchayats serve as economic hubs,
tourist places and significant temple towns with
heritage importance. Therefore, it is essential to
develop these medium and small towns with
proper basic amenities and robust infrastructure
as a key economic activity.
    Tamil Nadu has a total of 487 Town
Panchayats,    covering     an     area     of
5,948.291 square kilometers, with a total of
7,576 wards. The current population of these
Town Panchayats is approximately 78,87,523.




                       238
                       238
     The primary responsibilities of Town
Panchayats       include    developing      basic
infrastructure, providing essential services like
drinking water and sanitation to the public and
ensuring an improved quality of life by creating
and enhancing a healthy environment.
4.1 Administration
      There are 487 Town Panchayats in the
37 districts excluding Chennai district. The Head
of the Department is the Commissioner of
Town Panchayats. The Town Panchayats in
37 districts are divided into 17 zones and each
zone is administered through the Assistant
Director of Town Panchayats. The activities of
the Town Panchayats are overseen by the
Executive Officers of Town Panchayat. The
concerned District Collectors provide necessary
guidance and monitor the functioning of the
Town Panchayats.

4.2 Functions of Town Panchayats
   The 74th Constitutional Amendment Act was
introduced in 1992. Accordingly, the following
18 functions are defined for Urban Local Bodies.
Some of these functions are carried out directly
by the Town Panchayats, while some are
executed by other related departments.
  1.    Regulation    of    Land          use     and
        construction of buildings.
  2.    Roads and bridges.
  3.    Water supply for domestic, industrial,
        and commercial purposes.

                             243
                             243
4.   Public health, sanitation conservancy
     and solid waste management.
5.   Urban forestry, environmental protection
     and enhancement of environmental
     features.
6.   Protection of the welfare of marginalized
     sections of society, including persons
     with disabilities and mentally challenged
     individuals.
7.   Provision of urban amenities such as
     parks, gardens and play grounds.
8.   Provision of Cremation shed, burial
     grounds,   cremation    platforms and
     Modernised Gasifier Crematoriums.
9.   Key statistical details, including birth and
     death registrations.
10. Street lighting, vehicle parking spaces,
    bus stands and other public amenities.
11. Regulation    of    slaughter   houses    and
    tanneries.
12. Urban planning / Town planning.
13. Planning for        economic    and      social
    development.
14. Fire services.
15. Slum improvement.



                       244
                       244
     16. Urban poverty alleviation.
     17. Promotion of cultural, educational and
         aesthetic features.
     18. Animal Feed, Prevention of cruelty to
         animals.
4.2.1 Revenue Sources
    The most significant source of revenue for
the    Town    Panchayat   is  property   tax.
Additionally, revenue is generated through
sources like professional tax, non-tax, water
charges, surcharges on stamp duties. State
Government devolution funds and grants from
the Central Finance Commission are equally
important.
4.2.2 Water Supply
      Town Panchayats are taking various efforts
to    provide    protected      water     supply    to   all
households in their jurisdiction. These include
regulating      the    water    supply,    ensuring      the
provision of prescribed quantity of water to all,
augmentation          of   additional   water      sources,
construction of overhead tanks for sufficient
storage, replacing the damaged pumping main



                             245
                             245
and distribution pipelines and extension of water
supply pipelines in Town Panchayats.
     In all Town Panchayats, water is supplied to
the public at the rate of 70 litre per capita per
day (LPCD), while in Town Panchayats with
Under Ground Sewerage Systems (UGSS),
action is being taken to provide water supply at
the rate of 135 liters per person per day. Out of
the total 487 Town Panchayats, 325 are supplied
with drinking water through the Combined Water
Supply Schemes maintained by TWAD Board.
The remaining Town Panchayats provide water
supply through local sources. Currently, 42% of
households     have    their   household    water
connections. The goal is to provide functional
household tap connections to all households,
ensuring universal access to drinking water.
     Key activities of Town Panchayats include
augmenting and maintaining water sources,
restoring ponds and wells, reusing of recycled
treated water and also establishment of new
rainwater    harvesting      arrangements      and
restoration of existing rain water harvesting
structures etc., in all types of buildings through



                       246
                       246
community contributions are important    for
achieving sustainable water economics.
    Major water-related reforms envisaged in
Town Panchayats include accessing 20% of
water requirements from treated and recycled
water, reduction of non revenue water below
20% and restoring water bodies.
     Under the AMRUT 2.0, 157 water supply
projects at a total estimated cost of
Rs.3482.92 crore are being implemented.
Additionally, 21 water supply improvement
projects at a total estimated cost of
Rs.132.80 crore are being taken up under
Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam for the
year 2024-25. Once these projects are
completed,     the  number   of    household
connections in Town Panchayats will increase
significantly.
4.2.3 Roads
     Roads    are   important  not  only for
transportation but also plays a key role in
connecting cities and villages and play a
significant role in enhancing the economy of
these areas. Improved surface of the road is


                    247
                    247
necessary for making them suitable for use by
vehicles and pedestrians.
     Further, most of the roads were damaged
due to execution of water supply schemes,
under ground sewerage schemes, gas pipes,
telecom cables and also natural calamities.
Particularly in Town Panchayats, transitional
body from rural to urban acting as the market
hub for selling agricultural produce of the
adjoining rural areas using the roads. Hence,
conversion of earthern road to all weather roads
by formation and restoration and improvement
of roads damaged is necessary for improving the
quality of urban community.
     Town panchayats in the State is maintaining
various types of roads with a total length of
20,306.14 kilometers. The types of roads are
provided in Table 4.4.
Table 4.4
          Types of Road               Length

 Paver Block Roads                 1,037.27 km
 Earthen Roads                     2,807.05 km
 Metal Roads                        127.26 km
 Gravel Roads                        57.09 km
                          Total   20,306.14 km



                           248
                           248
       In the year 2021-22, 998.78 km of roads
were    upgraded     with       an        estimated    cost   of
Rs.658.06 crore under various schemes and
about 1,261.37 km of roads were upgraded with
an estimated cost of Rs.819.21 crore during the
year     2022-23.         In        the     year      2023-24,
1,463.84 km of roads have been taken up for

Rs.859.68 crore and in the year 2024-25,
1,149.64 km of roads are being upgraded with
an     estimated     cost           of     Rs.720.79     crore.
The remaining roads will be gradually upgraded
in a phased manner.

        In the last four years, 1,789.21 km of
earthern roads are converted and upgraded into
all weather roads with an estimated cost of
Rs.1,306.14 crore. Additionally, 3,084.41 km of
existing damaged roads have been taken up for

                                                              250
                                                              250
4.2.4 Street Lighting
     Street lights in Town Panchayats play a
crucial role in ensuring public safety and
preventing accidents. In 487 Town Panchayats
across Tamil Nadu, about 4,12,128 street lights
are maintained.
      To preserve the environment and reduce
the maintenance costs for street lights, steps
have been taken to convert the existing
conventional street lights with energy-efficient
LED lights in the Town Panchayats. Under the
State Urban Infrastructure Development Fund,
Rs.155.56 crore has been allocated for the
conversion of 2,66,953 lights into energy
efficient LED lights. As of today, 2,60,222 lights
have been converted and the remaining works
are in progress.
4.2.5 Bus Stands
     Bus Stands in small and medium towns like
Town Panchayats not only facilitate public
transportation but also boost local business
activities. In Town Panchayats, about 203 Bus
Stands of different classification like B, C and D
types are maintained. These Bus Stands are


                       251
                       251
either newly constructed or renovated under
various schemes such as the Kalaignar
Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam, Capital Grant
Fund and Operation and Maintenance Gap Filling
Fund.
     Under Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu
Thittam (KNMT) in the year 2021-22, 10 Bus
Stands at an estimated cost of Rs.44.69 crore
were sanctioned and 21 Bus Stands were
improved under the Operation and Maintenance
Gap Filling Fund, for Rs.5.44 crore.
    During the financial year 2022-23, 13 Bus
Stands have been sanctioned for Rs.22.13 crore
under KNMT and Capital Grant Fund. In addition
to that, 15 Bus Stands have been taken up
under Operation and Maintenance Gap Filling
Fund, costing Rs.6.79 crore. In the year
2023-24, 7 Bus Stands at an estimated cost of
Rs.12.28 crore under KNMT was sanctioned.
      During 2024-25, 14 Bus Stands have been
taken up at an estimated cost of Rs.39.25 crore
under Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam
(KNMT) and 3 Bus Stands under Operation and
Maintenance Scheme at an estimated cost of
Rs.1.81 crore for improvement.

                     252
                     252
    In the past four years, 83 new Bus Stands
and improvement works to the existing Bus
Stands have been undertaken at a cost of
Rs.132.39 crore.
4.2.6 Shandies
     Markets are very essential in facilitating the
availability of essential commodities at lower
prices, locally produced goods and enabling
farmers from nearby villages to market their
agricultural products. These markets not only
serve as places for buying and selling goods, but
also contribute to the economic vitality, social
and cultural development of Town Panchayat
areas.
     Thus, the economy of Town Panchayats and
surrounding rural areas is enhanced. Every year,
new markets are established under the Kalaignar
Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam and Capital Grant
Fund. Similarly, existing markets are being
renovated under the Operation and Maintenance
Fund. In the past four years, a total of
83 Market Development Works have been taken
up at a cost of Rs.160.93 crore.




                       253
                       253
4.3 Sanitation
     Creating and maintaining a clean and
healthy environment in Town Panchayats is a
core responsibility. Various measures have been
taken to create a healthy community.
     To achieve the goal of eliminating open
defecation, funding has been provided for the
construction of Individual Household Latrine,
community toilets and public toilets. Till date,
36,581 individual toilets have been sanctioned
under the Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0. Of these,
31,999 individual toilets have been completed
and put into use, with the remaining works in
various stages of progress. In areas where
individual toilets could not be built due to lack of
available land, community toilets (CTs) have
been constructed. A total of 3,404 seats have
been sanctioned for the construction of
378 community toilets, of which 220 have been
completed and functional. Remaining works are
in progress at various stages. Additionally,
50 public toilets with 385 seats have been
sanctioned for construction in the year 2022-23,
of which 49 have been completed and are



                        254
                        254
operational, while the remaining 1 is nearing
completion.
     Under 15th CFC grants, Rs.9.27 crore has
been      sanctioned     for  renovation    of
204 community toilets in 160 Town Panchayats
and all the works have been completed. Special
attention is given for maintaining community
and public toilets which are equipped with QR
codes to address their feed back and other
grievances of the public.
    In the year 2024-25, Rs.15.40 crore has
been     sanctioned      for     renovation of
235 community toilets. An additional amount of
Rs.3.31 crore has been allocated for the
renovation of 48 public toilets.
4.3.1 Battery Operated Vehicles
     Sanitation workers are facing difficulty in
using the manually operated push carts and
tricycles and also a challenge in efficient waste
collection mechanism. To address the shortfall in
primary waste collection, sanitation workers
sometimes need to increase their workload,
including extra trips. To improve the operations
on   Solid   Waste   Management     (SWM)    and

                      255
                      255
enhance workers' confidence and dignity, Town
Panchayats have decided to purchase Battery
Operated Vehicles (BOVs) with higher capacity.
These electric vehicles are being purchased in
place of manual push carts and tricycles due to
their     higher   lifespan   and   minimum   repair
requirements.

        A total of 3,069 Battery Operated Vehicles
have been sanctioned of which 2,995 have been
purchased and put into use. The remaining
vehicles are in the process of procurement.

4.3.2 Solid Waste Management
     Solid Waste Management plays a significant
role in maintaining environmental cleanliness
and protecting human health. As per the Solid
Waste Management Rules, 2016, all Town
Panchayats are carrying out Solid Waste
Management activities. In support of this effort,
the Government has prioritised for improvement
in existing infrastructure.
    Across       487       Town      Panchayats,
approximately 1,840 metric tons of waste is
generated daily, including 998 metric tons of wet


                          256
                          256
waste, 709 metric tons of dry waste and
133 metric tons of inerts. 59 Town Panchayats
that produce 5 metric tons and above quantity
of waste per day have received necessary
consent from the Tamil Nadu Pollution Control
Board for processing their solid waste.
      At present, most of the Town Panchayats
use   Battery       Operated      Vehicles   (BOVs)   for
carrying out door-to-door collection of solid
waste. Route charts have been prepared and
structured for every vehicle for efficient waste
collection. At present, 96% of households in
Town Panchayats have been covered under the
door-to-door collection. Efforts are focused on
increasing      the     home      composting    to    the
remaining gaps. Recyclable dry waste is sold to
vendors, while non-recyclable waste is sent to
cement factories for co-processing. A total of
14,583 outsourced workers and 4,975 sanitation
workers       are     involved    in   waste   collection
including segregation of waste to ensure the
efficient cleaning of streets and solid waste
collection.



                            257
                            257
      Between the financial years 2022 and 2025,
Rs.83.38 crore has been allocated to 409 Town
Panchayats for managing 725 metric tons of wet
waste      and   Rs.30.92       crore   for   391     Town
Panchayats to handle 547 metric tons of dry
waste. Of which 338 dry waste processing
projects     (597.5      metric    tons)      have    been
completed and remaining 71 (127.5 metric tons)
are   in     progress.    288     composting      centers
(409.50 metric tons) have been completed and
the balance 103 are (137.50 metric tons) in
progress at various stages.

4.3.3 Organic / Vermi Composting

      Waste collected from Town Panchayats is
transported      to      Composting        Centres        and
Resource Recovery Parks where it is segregated
further. Organic waste such as fruits, vegetables
and leaves are converted into manure and vermi
compost. On an average, daily about 100 to
150 metric tons of organic compost is produced,
and    249    Town    Panchayats        produce      15    to
20 metric tons of vermi-compost daily. Revenue



                           258
                           258
of approximately Rs.40 to 45 lakh is generated
annually from the sale of the compost.

4.3.4 Used Water Management
     Town Panchayats places great importance
on Water Management to preserve the
environment. To ensure that untreated sewage
and effluent waste are not discharged into open
areas, Sewage Treatment Plants (STPs) and
Fecal Sludge Treatment Plants (FSTPs) are being
established. These treatment centers focus on
safe waste collection, transportation, and
increased reuse.
     To expand the usage of STPs and FSTPs
across the state, Town Panchayats are working
on identifying land and preparing Detailed
Project Reports (DPRs).
4.3.5 Underground       Sewerage         Schemes
      (UGSS)
     Underground     Sewerage    Schemes     in
Orathanadu, Vallam, S.Kannanoor, Velankanni,
Perundurai,   Palanichettipatti, Thirumazhisai,
Mannachanallur, Melachokkanathapuram and
Thiruporur have been successfully completed
and are operated in 10 Town panchayats.

                      259
                      259
4.3.6   Faecal Sludge        and   Waste   Water
        Management
      Currently, 10 Faecal Sludge Treatment
Plants (FSTPs) are operational in Town
Panchayats and FSTPs at Karunguzhi and
Periyanaickanpalayam have proven to be
successful, serving as models for other Urban
Local Bodies (ULBs). These Treatment Plants are
visited by faculties, students from educational
institutions, officials from other States and also
the public for improving their technical
knowhow.
      An integrated approach is being followed to
establish Waste Water Treatment Plants in the
State. The Town Panchayats have been divided
into clusters and this scheme is being
implemented. So far, approval was obtained for
9 Sewage Treatment Plants and 43 Sewage
Treatment Plants cum Faecal Sludge Treatment
Plants under the Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0.
Furthermore, it involves coordinating Urban
Local Bodies and Rural Local Bodies for highly
efficient operations. Additionally, efforts to
identify land are continuously being carried out
across the state in various districts. To prevent

                       260
                       260
manual cleaning of septic tanks, 185 Town
Panchayats have been granted permission to
procure 185 cess pool vehicles and the
procurement process is underway.
4.3.7 Toll-Free Helpline (14420)
      The Toll-Free Helpline Number 14420 has
been established in Tamil Nadu to report
sanitation-related complaints and services such
as cleaning of septic tanks, maintaining
underground drainage systems and any manual
cleaning operations.
4.4 Gasifier Crematoriums
       Since  traditional cremation    methods
involve the use of firewood in cremation
grounds, which increase the carbon levels in the
atmosphere, leading to environmental damage,
gasifier crematoriums are being established in
Town Panchayats to preserve the environment.
      25 Modern Gasifier Crematoriums have
been    established    and    are   operational.
Additionally, in the year 2021-22, 34 Gasifier
Crematoriums      were   sanctioned   with   an
estimated value of Rs.50.35 crore and all works
have been completed. In the year 2022-23,

                      261
                      261
37 Gasifier Crematoriums were established with
an estimated value of Rs.51.95 crore. Out of
these, 34 projects have been completed and the
remaining works are ongoing. For the year
2023-24, 28 Gasifier Crematoriums have been
sanctioned with an estimated value of
Rs.44.70 crore. Out of these, 23 projects have
been completed and the remaining works are
under progress. Furthermore, for the year
2024-25, Rs.38.22 crore was allocated for
establishing 25 Gasifier Crematoriums. These
Gasifier Crematoriums are maintained through
Trusts, NGOs / Charitable Organizations as well
as by some Town Panchayats.
4.5 Environmental Improvement through
    Urban Greening
       A healthy, green environment plays a
crucial role in human well-being. Therefore,
managing and maintaining the environment
efficiently is an essential task for everyone. To
improve and protect the environment, various
measures are being taken including tree
plantation by the Town Panchayats.
       Encouragement is being given for planting
traditional trees such as Mango, Jackfruit,

                      262
                      262
Guava. Steps are being taken to plant valuable
trees like Teak, Ebony and Tamarind in Town
Panchayat, to strengthen the banks of water
bodies such as lakes and ponds, the planting of
palm trees around the banks is being
encouraged. So far, 5,85,567 saplings have
been planted in Town Panchayats.
4.5.1 Renovation of Water Bodies
      Water bodies play an important role in
water management within Town Panchayats.
Therefore, restoration by renovating the existing
water bodies in Town Panchayats is a crucial
task. Renovating water bodies involves desilting,
cleaning, restructuring the pathways and
channels for rain water to enter, planting trees
along the banks and fencing to prevent
encroachments. Through this process, the
quality of water improves and the depletion of
groundwater is also prevented.
      2,212 water bodies are maintained by
Town Panchayats in which 1,182 have already
been renovated. Efforts have been made to
renovate 830 out of the remaining 1,030 water
bodies. Among these 830, the renovation of
658 water bodies have been completed and

                      263
                      263
brought into use. The remaining works on the
172 water bodies are in progress at various
stages. The remaining 200 water bodies will be
renovated gradually. Details are provided in
Table 4.6.

4.5.2 Parks
       The urban parks created in Town
Panchayats serve as indicators of the quality of
life for the local population. These parks offer
various benefits such as improving air quality,
controlling noise and air pollution, enhancing
biodiversity and mitigating climate change on a
smaller scale.
      Out of 536 parks in Town Panchayats,
improvement works in 160 parks have been
completed    in    2022-23.     In   2023-24,
development works for 202 parks were
undertaken, with 151 of those completed, while
the remaining works are in progress at various
stages.


                              265
                              265
4.5.3 Rainwater Harvesting
      Under the Urban Local Bodies Act, 1998
and Building Regulations, Rainwater Harvesting
has been made mandatory in every building.
This has had a significant impact on replenishing
groundwater levels across the state. Efforts are
focused on refurbishing existing rainwater
harvesting structures in Government and private
buildings and creating new ones. Town
Panchayats continue to take various steps to
raise awareness among the public regarding the
importance of rainwater harvesting.
4.6 Union Government Schemes
4.6.1 Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0 (SBM 2.0)
       Under the Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0 for
the financial year 2021-22, a total of
7,295 Individual Household Latrines (IHHLs),
100 community toilets with 847 seats and
75 urinal toilets with 450 seats have been
approved. Additionally, for the 2022-23 financial
year, a total of 27,900 individual household
latrines, 90 community toilets, 50 public toilets
and 38 urinal toilets have been approved. In
2023-24, 1,386 individual household toilets


                      266
                      266
(IHHL) and 73 community toilets were approved.
For the 2024-25 financial year, 115 community
toilets and 72 urinal toilets were approved.
       Of these, 31,999 individual household
toilets, 220 community toilets, 49 public toilets,
113 urinals, 108 retrofitting of the existing
community toilets and 27 retrofitting of existing
public toilets have been completed and the
remaining works are in progress.
4.6.2   Bio-Mining of Legacy          Waste    at
         Dumping Sites
     The process of recovering waste-filled
dumps is currently being carried out through
Bio-Mining. In 131 Town Panchayats, a total of
147 projects have been identified to recover old
and abandoned waste dumpsites through
bio-mining remediation. These sites cover an
area of 257 acres and contain approximately
11.4 lakh cubic meters of legacy waste. The
total estimated cost for removal of legacy waste
is Rs.76.32 crore. Bio-mining activities have
been successfully completed in 86 Town
Panchayats (covering 96 projects), resulting in
the removal of 7.68 lakh cubic meters of legacy
waste and the reclamation of 149 acres of land.

                       267
                       267
The remaining 51 bio-mining projects are in
progress at various stages. The Centre for
Environmental Studies at the Anna University
campus in Chennai provides technical guidance
for bio-mining activities.
4.6.3 Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana (Urban)
      [PMAY (U)]
     The Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana is being
implemented with the funding from both the
Union and State Governments to benefit the
marginalized and middle-class sections of the
Town Panchayats. Under the Pradhan Mantri
Awas Yojana, the fourth component, which is for
beneficiaries to build their own houses, is being
carried out by the Town Panchayats. Each
selected house under this scheme is allocated
Rs.2.10 lakh, with Rs.1.50 lakh provided by the
Union Government and Rs.0.60 lakh by the
State Government. Any additional costs above
the subsidy for construction of house will be
borne by the beneficiary. At the State level, the
Tamil Nadu Urban Habitat Development Board
functions as the Nodal agency for coordinating
this scheme.



                      268
                      268
    A    total   of   89,790   houses   have     been
approved under this scheme, out of which
70,578    houses      have   been   completed.    The
remaining 19,212 houses are in various stages
of progress and will be completed soon.

4.6.4 National Urban Livelihood Mission
    (NULM)

    Under NULM's seven components, the Town
Panchayats Department is only involved in two
aspects: Shelters for the Homeless in Urban
Areas (SUH) and         Support to Urban Street
Vendors (SUSV).

1. Homeless Shelters

    38 shelters have been sanctioned for
construction and all shelters have been
completed and put into beneficial use. Out of
which, 3 regular shelters and 35 special shelters
for use of attendants of in-patients in
Government hospitals.
2.(a) Support to Urban Street Vendors
(SUSV)
    A new census regarding street vendors in
Town Panchayats has been conducted to create

                         269
                         269
an environment for smooth business operations
and upto date 51,153 identity cards have been
issued. An amount of Rs.2,57,18,340/- has been
allocated for conducting the census, issuing
identity cards and forming street vendor groups.
Additionally,   228    mobile        carts   have        been
provided to street vendors in Velankanni Town
Panchayats      at     an     estimated           cost     of
Rs.1,88,34,000/-.

(b) PM-SVANidhi
    The      Pradhan       Mantri      Street      Vendors
Atmanirbhar Nidhi (PM-SVANidhi) Scheme was
launched to provide financial assistance to Street
Vendors. By providing an unsecured capital loan
of Rs.10,000/- to Street Vendors, this Scheme
has contributed to their immediate finances and
business improvement.

    Under this scheme, till date, 1,08,359 loan
applications have been uploaded on the portal.
Of these, 78,193 loan applications have been
approved and loans have been provided to
76,292       applicants.          Additionally,          Town
Panchayats in collaboration with Banks, regularly

                            270
                            270
organize Meetings at the Regional level to help
beneficiaries avail the loan assistance.

4.6.5 15th Finance Commission – National
       Health Mission Grant
     Funds   are   being    allocated  for   the
construction of Primary Health Centres, Block
Public     Health      Unit      and      Health
Sub Centres in urban areas, as per the
recommendations     of    the     15th   Finance
Commission.
    In 2021-22, 42 works were sanctioned with
an allocation of Rs.21.00 crore and all works
have been completed.
     In 2022-23, Rs.11.10 crore was allocated
for 24 works out of which 12 works have been
completed and 12 works are in progress at
various stages.
     During the year 2023-24, 69 works were
approved with an allocation of Rs.38.45 crore,
with 20 completed and the remaining 49 works
are in progress at various stages.
    In     the    financial   year    2024-25,
administrative approval has been accorded for
57 works with a budget of Rs.40.15 crore and all

                       271
                       271
these works are being carried out at various
stages.
4.7 State Government Schemes
4.7.1 Kalaignar   Nagarpura           Mempattu
      Thittam (KNMT)
     The aim of this scheme is not only to create
basic infrastructure facilities in Town Panchayats
but also to create capital assets such as Bus
Stands, Markets, and Knowledge Centers in
Town Panchayats.
      Every year Rs.500 crore is allotted for Town
Panchayats under the scheme. In 2021-22,
various works including the conversion of
Earthen Roads to BT, Cement and Paver Block
Roads, Bus Stands, Markets and other public
facilities have been taken up at an estimated
cost of Rs.473.42 crore. Out of 800 works,
798 works were completed with the remaining
2 works in progress at various stages.
    Sanction has been accorded for the year
2022-23 to take up 15 Shandies, 11 Bus stands,
35 Crematoriums, 3 Water Bodies, 1 Park
improvement work, 1 Community Hall and
223 Road Works at a total estimated cost of


                       272
                       272
Rs.495.84 crore, including fund tie-up for water
supply projects taken up under AMRUT 2.0. Out
of these, 285 works have been completed and
the remaining 4 works are in progress at various
stages.
     During the year 2023-24, sanction has been
accorded for taking up 9 shandies work, 7 bus
stands, 26 crematoriums, 50 water bodies,
63 park improvement works, 74 storm water
drains & culverts, 208 road works, 2 knowledge
centers and battery-operated vehicles, at a total
estimated cost of Rs.495.00 crore, including
fund tie-up for the water supply projects taken
up under AMRUT 2.0 schemes. Out of these,
391 works have been completed and the
remaining 49 works are in progress at various
stages.
     Sanction has been accorded for the year
2024-25 to take up 30 shandies work, 14 bus
stands, 25 crematoriums, 34 water bodies,
1 park improvement work, 5 storm water drains,
1,269 road works, 6 Bridges, 21 water supply
works and 3 Padithurai works at an estimated
cost of Rs.497.50 crore. Out of these, 626 works
have been completed and the remaining

                      273
                      273
782 works are in progress at various stages. In
the forthcoming years, more infrastructure
works will be taken up.
4.7.2 Namakku Namme Thittam (Urban)
     In urban areas, public participation is
essential    for   creating     and   maintaining
infrastructure   facilities.   Therefore,   Town
Panchayats are effectively implementing this
scheme which encourages public contribution
and fosters a sense of self-reliance.
     In 2021-22, 522 works were taken up in
Town Panchayats, with an estimated value of
Rs.57.28 crore, with public contributions of
Rs.20.03 crore and Government contributions of
Rs.37.25 crore. All projects were completed. In
2022-23, 429 works were undertaken at an
estimated cost of Rs.49.58 crore, with public
contributions of Rs.16.34 crore and Government
contributions of Rs.33.24 crore. 426 works were
completed and 3 works are ongoing.
    In the year 2023-24, 337 projects have
been   undertaken   at   a   total  cost   of
Rs.41.44 crore, with a public contribution of
Rs.11.98 crore and Government contribution of


                      274
                      274
Rs.29.46 crore. Among these, 304 works have
been completed and the remaining 33 works are
at various stages of implementation.
4.7.3   Tamil Nadu        Urban     Employment
        Scheme
     The aim of this scheme is to provide
employment opportunities to the marginalized
communities in Town panchayat areas and
through this, create public assets. As a result,
the livelihoods of these people improve and gain
social security.
     This scheme was implemented as a pilot
project in 37 Town Panchayats in Tamil Nadu.
Under this scheme, every household in these
37 Town Panchayats was surveyed and eligible
beneficiaries were identified. Job cards were
issued to the identified beneficiaries on behalf of
the respective Town Panchayats.
     Under this scheme, 192 projects were
carried out, including the renovation of ponds,
planting of saplings, construction of drains and
the creation of rainwater harvesting systems. So
far, 8,43,951 man days have been generated
and 66,312 beneficiaries have benefited from
it.

                       275
                       275
     Due to the successful implementation of
this scheme in Town Panchayats, in the year
2023-2024, the Government expanded the
scheme to an additional 50 Town Panchayats.
Currently, the scheme is being implemented in
87 Town Panchayats and administrative sanction
has been accorded for 315 projects costing
Rs.35.75 crore. Of these, 133 projects have
been completed and the remaining projects are
in progress.

4.8 e-Governance in Town Panchayats
4.8.1 Online Tax Payment
     The primary revenue sources of Town
Panchayats include tax and non-tax categories.


                     285
                     285
Currently, a computerized receipt payment
system is in place through the Town Panchayats
website. Citizens can pay their tax and non-tax
dues using various payment methods such as
debit cards, credit cards, net banking, UPI, and
QR codes. In addition to that the public can pay
their dues from anywhere by accessing
https://dtp.tn.gov.in.
4.8.2 Online Building Permission
       According      Planning      Permission      for
buildings in Town Panchayats was previously
processed manually. Now, Online services for
approval of layouts and building permission have
been implemented by simplifying the process for
use of the public.

       Through this, people can apply and obtain
permission   for     layout   and   building    permits
online. This service is available across all 487
Town       Panchayats           (website       address:
https://onlineppa.tn.gov.in).

       Additionally, from 22.02.2024 permission
for construction of building up to 3,500 square
feet area for Ground plus First Floor on plots up


                          286
                          286
to 2,500 square feet can be obtained via self-
certification,   with      uploading     of    relevant
documents and payment of fees through online.

4.8.3 Online Grievance Redressal

      Currently,       public    grievances   in   Town
Panchayats       are      received      through     the
“Mudhalvarin Mugavari Website”. The public can
register their grievances through the Toll-Free
Number 1100 or the website and seek redressal.
The grievances registered online are addressed
within a specified time frame and the status of
each grievance is communicated to the public
via SMS.
4.8.4 Online Scheme Monitorin System CM
      Dash Board
      All Capital and Maintenance Works in Town
Panchayats are uploaded to the Chief Minister's
Dash Board which is monitored regularly. This
helps senior officials to track the current status
and progress of all works and provides useful
insights for evaluation.




                           287
                           287
4.8.5 UTIS     (Urban       Tree   Information
     System)
     All the projects and progress of works in
Town Panchayats are uploaded to the UTIS
website which allows the monitoring of the work
status. Through this platform, the current status
and photos of ongoing works are uploaded,
helping senior officials to evaluate and provide
guidance to their subordinates. Additionally, by
uploading and maintaining work-related data,
human effort and time spent on repetitive data
entry are minimized. UTIS services are being
implemented in all Town Panchayats in line with
the services available at the Directorate of
Municipal Administration.

                                292
                                292
5.   Chennai Metropolitan Water Supply and
     Sewerage Board
     Chennai city and the area contiguous to it
were notified as Chennai Metropolitan Area
(CMA) in 1974. Subsequently, with the objective
of exclusively attending to the growing needs
and for planned development and appropriate
regulation    of   water    supply       and   sewerage
services in the Chennai Metropolitan Area with
particular reference to the protection of public
health and for all matters connected therewith,
Chennai      Metropolitan        Water    Supply   and
Sewerage Board (CMWSSB) was established as a
statutory body under Tamil Nadu Act 28 of 1978
during the year 1978. Apart from the above
basic service, Board is now providing several
on-line services in vogue with the growing
Infromation-technology development.

      The Board is managed by a full time
Managing Director. The constitution of the Board
of CMWSSB is as below:




                           293
                           293
Board of Directors

CHAIRMAN
 1    Hon’ble Minister for Municipal Administration
DIRECTORS
 2    Principal    Secretary        to      Government,
      Finance Department
 3    Principal   Secretary         to     Government,
      Municipal  Administration      &    Water  Supply
      Department
 4    Commissioner,
      Greater Chennai Corporation
 5    Managing Director, CMWSS Board
 6    Member      Secretary, Chennai          Metropolitan
      Development Authority
 7    Managing Director, TWAD Board
 8    Executive Director, CMWSS Board
 9    Finance Director, CMWSS Board
 10   Engineering Director, CMWSS Board


      Providing safe drinking water and ensuring
safe disposal of sewage is mandatory for a
healthy environment and good quality of life for
people. CMWSSB, since its formation, has been
providing these services to the citizens of
Chennai city. At present, CMWSSB is providing


                           294
                           294
services  directly    to   91     lakh    people
(approximately) spread over 426 sq.km in
Chennai city through 15 Area offices and
200 Depots. It also caters to the needs of few
urban nodes adjacent to the city and industries.

Vision for the future

  •   Provide functional water and sewerage
      connection to every household in Chennai
      city.

  •   Address the demand-supply gap through
      source augmentation and infrastructure
      strengthening.

  •   Harness rainwater by implementing and
      monitoring rainwater harvesting structures
      in each and every premise in Chennai city
      and prepare    an       Aquifer   Management
      Plan.

  •   Recycle / reuse at least 20% of used
      water.

  •   Reduce non-revenue water (NRW) to less
      than 15%.




                        295
                        295
  •   Provide water supply and underground
      sewerage facilities to all the left out streets
      in Chennai city.

  •   Build adequate sewage treatment capacity
      by     constructing    new   STPs    and
      rehabilitating existing sewage treatment
      plants (STPs).

  •   Prevent sewage outfalls into the city water
      ways viz Adyar, Cooum and Buckingham
      canal and their associated drains through
      short term interception and diversion
      arrangements and long term infrastructure
      strengthening works.

5.1 Water Supply Management
5.1.1 Sources of Water Supply
     Providing safe drinking water in adequate
quantity to the people of Chennai city and to
meet the water requirements of industries, is the
primary and consistent endeavor of the Board.
The water requirement is met by drawing water
from surface sources, ground water sources,
reuse of water, desalination plants and tertiary
treatment reverse osmosis (TTRO) plants.


                        296
                        296
i. Surface Water Sources
      Surface water is the main source of water
supply to Chennai city. Water is drawn from
lakes around Chennai city such as Poondi,
Puzhal,    Cholavaram,     Chembarambakkam,
Kannankottai-Thervoykandigai; and Veeranam in
Cuddalore district besides water received from
the Kandaleru reservoir under Krishna Water
Supply Project.

ii. Ground Water Sources
      About 10 MLD of ground water is drawn
from the aquifers at Tamaraipakkam, Poondi and
Minjur and 20 MLD from own sources in added
areas.

iii. Desalination Plants
     The Chennai city’s water sources are
dependent on monsoon since there are no
perennial water sources for city. Whenever
monsoon fails, the city faces drought. Further,
urbanisation of the surrounding areas and the
rapid growth of the industries led to drastic
increase in demand for water exerting pressure
on scarce water resources. Therefore, as part of
drought-proofing measure and for the long term

                      297
                      297
drinking water requirements of the city,
Government has invested in desalination plants.
Desalination plants play a vital role in fulfilling
the city’s water supply requirements.

  a) Desalination Plant - Minjur
     The first desalination plant with a capacity
of 100 MLD at Minjur on Design, Build, Own,
Operate and Transfer (DBOOT) basis was
inaugurated on 31.07.2010 by the then Chief
Minister, Dr.Kalaignar Karunanidhi. This plant
caters to the needs of the northern parts of
Chennai city viz., Manali, Madhavaram, Ennore,
Kathivakkam and Thiruvottiyur benefiting a
population of about 9 lakh.
  b) Desalination       Plant    -   100   MLD    -
Nemmeli
      Another desalination plant with a capacity
of 100 MLD has been constructed at Nemmeli at
a cost of Rs.914.42 crore (GoI grant of
Rs.871.24 crore and Rs.43.18 crore from GoTN).
The foundation stone for the construction of this
plant was laid on 23.02.2010 by the Hon’ble
Chief Minister when he was Deputy Chief
Minister and Minister for Local Administration.


                       298
                       298
The plant became operational in 2013 and water
from this plant is being supplied to the southern
parts of Chennai viz., Akkarai, Ezhil Nagar,
Kannagi Nagar, Sholinganallur, Neelangarai,
Injambakkam, Karapakkam, Semmancherry,
Thoraipakkam,        Perungudi,      Kottivakkam,
Palavakkam, Thiruvanmiyur and Pallipattu,
benefiting a population of about 10 lakh.

     Now the capacity of this plant is enhanced
from 100 MLD to 110 MLD.

      c) Desalination Plant - 150 MLD -
Nemmeli and laying of product water
pipeline   from     desalination plant to
Pallavaram

      Construction of another 150 MLD capacity
desalination plant was taken up at Nemmeli to
address the demand-supply gap in water supply
in Chennai city. The Government of Tamil Nadu
has accorded revised administrative sanction at
a cost of Rs.1,516.82 crore to implement the
project under AMRUT scheme and financial
assistance from KfW (German Development
Bank).



                       299
                       299
      The project has been completed and
inaugurated by Hon’ble Chief Minister on
24.02.2024. Water produced from this plant is
being supplied to the public living in Velachery,
Uthandi, Alandur, Pallikaranai, Jalladiyanpettai,
Madipakkam,      Pallipattu,     Ullagaram      –
Puzhuthivakkam and MRC Nagar - Nandanam
under the administrative purview of Greater
Chennai Corporation and Pallavaram under the
administrative     purview      of     Tambaram
Corporation, benefiting nearly 9 lakh people.

iv. Tertiary Treated Water
      CMWSS Board has constructed two tertiary
treatment reverse osmosis (TTRO) plants of 45
MLD capacity each at Koyambedu and
Kodungaiyur, including pipeline to convey the
treated used water to the industries in
Irungattukottai, Sriperumbudur and Oragadam
and in Manali–Ennore corridor and Manali–Minjur
corridor, in order to cater to the needs of
industries in and around Chennai city. Both the
plants are operational. At present, 23 MLD of
TTRO water is being supplied to Industries from




                      300
                      300
Kodungaiyur and         15 MLD from Koyambedu
TTRO plant.

      The century old city’s first Water treatment
plant located at Kilpauk is functioning till now
catering to the water needs of the city.

      The existing water treatment capacity of
the CMWSS Board is 1,670 MLD as detailed in
table 5.1 below.

5.1.4 Quality Assurance
     To ensure quality water supply to the
people of Chennai, continuous surveillance,
assessment and monitoring of the water quality
is essential. To ensure the quality of water
supply, a separate quality assurance wing is
functioning in CMWSS Board. Water samples are
collected daily from surface and ground water
sources, water treatment plants, desalination
plants, water distribution stations, subsidiary
head works and the city distribution system.
These samples are tested for physical, chemical
and bacteriological parameters by the quality
assurance wing of CMWSSB to ensure that water

                                303
                                303
quality conforms to IS Standard 10500-2012.
Currently, 300 water samples are taken daily by
the quality assurance laboratory and tested for
water quality.

      A new water and wastewater testing
laboratory with a built-up area of 12,000 sq ft.
comprising separate water and used water
laboratory      sections,    with      advanced
instrumentation and infrastructure facilities has
been set up by the Board at an estimate of
Rs.7.05 crore. These sections include separate
Physio chemical analysis and micro biological
analysis   facilities.  The   laboratory      was
inaugurated by the Hon’ble Chief Minister on
22.11.2022 and NABL accreditation obtained for
the laboratory on 25.07.2024.

5.2 Water Supply Schemes
5.2.1 Schemes under Implementation
(i) Desalination Plant - 400 MLD - Perur
     Chennai city’s present water supply is
1,126 MLD whereas, demand is 1,407 MLD. This
leaves a gap of 281 MLD. In order to bridge this
gap, it is essential to augment the water supply
by identifying additional sources.

                      304
                      304
        Accordingly,     Government          has   accorded
administrative sanction for setting up a 400 MLD
capacity desalination plant at Perur along East
Coast Road, product water transmission main
from the desalination plant to the city, at a cost
of Rs.6,078.40 crore, with financial assistance
from JICA, TNIDF and state budgetary support.

        The    work      in     this   project     involves
construction    of     400       MLD    capacity     SWRO
Desalination    plant,        construction    of   Pumping
station & Reservoirs, Installation of Product
water     transmission        main,    Improvement       of
existing water distribution networks and Power
Transmission Line.

        Hon’ble Chief Minister laid the foundation
stone for this project on 21.08.2023 and work is
in progress.

        The Construction of the Plant is expected
to be completed and commissioned by February
2027.

       On completion of this project, people living
in   Greater Chennai Corporation, Tambaram

     To ensure an equitable water supply,

“Improvement of existing water distribution

                       308
5.2.3   Rehabilitation of Water Treatment
        Plants – Puzhal and Surapet

      Due to aging of the 300 MLD capacity

being pursued.

5.2.4 Ring Main System

      At present, Water Supply to different
zones (WDSs) of Greater Chennai Corporation is
from 6 Water Treatment Plants and 3
Desalination Plants. Water supply from each of
these plants is designed on standalone basis
through Transmission Mains to the designated
areas of the city. Surplus water from one source
cannot be transferred to another source at times
of deficit. Hence, the Ring main project (RMP)

                       309
                       309
has been proposed to connect all the WTPs for
effecting equitable distribution of water to all the
Water Distribution Stations in Chennai city.

Ring main 1 (GCC Area)

    This Ring main will cover all the existing
WTPs and WDSs in the Greater Chennai
Corporation Area (426 Sq.Km) for a length of
about 100 Km with pipe diameter ranging from
1000 mm to 1800 mm.

Ring main 2 (CMA)

     The Ring main will be covering Chennai
Metropolitan Area excluding core city (763
Sq.Km) for a length of about 135 Km with pipe
diameter ranging from 1000 mm to 2000 mm.

     Final DPR for Ring Main 1 (GCC area)
completed by the Consultant and for Ring Main 2
(CMA area) is expected shortly.

5.2.5 Rainwater Harvesting

      Rainwater Harvesting is a pioneer project
of the State of Tamil Nadu and pioneer State in
implementation of rainwater harvesting (RWH)
programme in the country. Tamil Nadu is the


                        310
                        310
first state in India to make installation of RWH
structures mandatory for both existing and new
buildings.

      Installation    of     rainwater      harvesting
structures has been made mandatory for all
buildings irrespective of size and area, while
applying for new/ renewal of water and sewer
connections.

      A      communication       strategy   has   been
devised by CMWSSB to create awareness about
the importance of rainwater harvesting and to
encourage consumers to build RWH structures.
The campaign is carried out every year before
the onset of monsoon by sensitizing citizens
about rainwater harvesting to reap maximum
benefits during the monsoon season.

      In Chennai city, during the year 2022-23,
out of 11.05 lakh buildings, verification was
conducted in 10.27 lakh buildings. In the survey,
it is observed that about 6,67,736 (around 65%)
premises      have   maintained      functional   RWH
structures.


                           311
                           311
5.2.5.1 Proposed Awareness Programme

      To monitor the ground water levels
periodically, CMWSSB has installed digital water
level    recorders  (DWLR)       in   all    the

significant improvement in water levels and
water quality after every monsoon. Readings are
updated on CMWSSB website so that public can
know the water levels. Also, CMWSSB has

                      313
                      313
installed automated rain gauges in all the
15 area offices for real time monitoring.

      Observations from digital water level
recorders (DWLR) in all the 200 Depots reveal
that the average water levels in Chennai city
have increased from 7.83 m in the year 2003 to
3.46 m in the year 2024, which is an increase of
4.37 m. Similarly, there is a substantial
improvement in the water quality as well.

5.3 Sewerage Management

      Collection, treatment and safe disposal of
used water in Chennai city is another primary
duty of CMWSS Board. The sewerage system in
the core city was installed initially in 1917 and
has been divided into Seven sewerage zones.
These zones having macro system covering the
entire city and have independent zonal
collections, conveyance, treatment and disposal
facilities. The sewage generated is collected in
356 sewage pumping stations through a
4,787 km long pipeline network. The sewage is
then conveyed to sewage treatment plants and
disposed safely after treatment.



                      314
                      314
5.3.1     Operation  and  Maintenance                    of
          Sewerage System
     One of the main objective of the
Government of Tamil Nadu is eradication of
manual scavenging. To achieve this objective,
the Board has mechanized the operation and
maintenance of the sewerage system to
eradicate manual scavenging. The obstructions
in the pipeline are removed by jet rodding
machines and the solid waste in the machine
holes are removed by super sucker machines
and desilting machines. At present, 601
machines are deployed for maintaining the
sewerage system. The details are shown in
table 5.3.
Table 5.3

   4      Super sucker machines                  73
   5      Sewer lorries                          03
                    Total                       601

      To    improve    maintenance,             additional
machines      and   equipment    are             procured

Kotturpuram (0.6 MLD).




                            319
                            319
5.3.6 Villivakkam TTUF

       The Villivakkam -Konnur lake is one of the
main water body of the Villivakkam area and is
located adjacent to the thickly populated SIDCO
nagar and drains into the Otteri Nullah.

       CMWSSB is setting up a 5 MLD Capacity
TTUF Plant based on Membrane Bio Reactor
(MBR)    Technology        adjacent    to   Villivakkam-
Konnur        lake    on      Design,       Engineering,
Procurement and Construction basis including
Operation and Maintenance for 7 Years for the
restoration and rejuvenation of the lake with
treated water.

       The     necessary        fund    approval    and
administrative sanction has been accorded for an
amount of Rs.17.97 crore under Singara Chennai
2.O.    The    work   for    establishing    the   plant
commenced on 15.03.2024 and is in progress.
This project will help in rejuvenating the lake by
improving      the    quality     of    water   besides
recharging the ground water.
