                      321
                      321
5.4.2 Vada Chennai Valarchi Thittam

     The capacity of the existing sewage
system of the Chennai Metropolitan Water
Supply and Sewerage Board is insufficient to
meet the growing needs of the burgeoning
population in North Chennai areas of Greater
Chennai Corporation. Therefore, a new project
to improve the sewage and drinking water
infrastructure and to reduce water pollution in
North Chennai has been taken up at a cost of
Rs.946 crore and works are in progress.

5.4.3 Improvement of Existing Sewerage
     System in Core Areas of Greater
     Chennai
      The existing sewerage system in the core
areas    includes    Areas    IV  (Tondairpet),
V (Royapuram), VI (Thiru Vi.Ka. Nagar), VIII
(Anna        Nagar),       IX     (Teynampet),
X (Kodambakkam) and XIII (Adyar) have been
in existence for over 30 years. The capacity of
the Gravity Main, Wells, Pumping Main and
pumping equipment is inadequate to meet the
present population requirements and hence, the
sewerage system could not handle the total


                     322
                     322
sewage generated in core areas. Therefore, it is
necessary to improve the existing Sewerage
system in the project area for the effective
disposal of sewage.

      CMWSSB has engaged a consultant for the
work of “Preparation of Detailed Project Reports
for Improvement of Existing Sewerage System
in Core Areas of Greater Chennai city
(174 sq.km) and Providing SCADA for the entire
Greater Chennai city (426 sq.km)”. DPRs for
each Sewage Treatment plant are being
prepared by the consultant in a phased manner
and the final DPR is expected to be submitted by
the consultant by March 2025.

5.4.4 Projects   under       Chennai     Rivers
      Restoration Trust

      Chennai Rivers Restoration Trust (CRRT)
has taken up the eco-restoration of the Chennai
city waterways on behalf of Government of Tamil
Nadu.    CMWSSB, along with CRRT, has
formulated   mitigation proposals    for the
prevention of sewage outfalls into Cooum and
Adyar rivers and Buckingham canal and its
associated drains. Mitigation measures for

                      323
                      323
Cooum and Adyar rivers at a cost of
Rs.186.19     crore   and  Rs.123.19 crore
respectively have been taken up by CMWSSB
and work is in progress.

     In Adyar, some works (modular STP and
I&D works) have been dropped due to ongoing
and proposed underground sewerage schemes in
added areas. The savings of Rs.64.89 crore from
this is utilised for 9 works for implementation in
Area X and XIII and are under progress to
prevent sewage outfall in Adyar River. These
works would strengthen the existing sewerage
system in these areas.

     The Government has given top priority to
the restoration of Chennai city waterways.
Accordingly, CMWSSB has taken up the proposal
of plugging of sewage outfalls in Adyar and
Cooum rivers, Buckingham canal and their
associated drains.  The proposal includes
interception   and diversion   arrangements,
strengthening / refurbishment        of   existing
sewerage infrastructure.

       The Government have also accorded
revised administrative sanction for a value of

                       324
                       324
Rs.385.38 crore, for this project. Work orders
were issued for 36 works. Out of 36 works,
34 have been completed and the remaining
2 works are in progress. Further, CMWSSB has
taken up the plugging of 23 sewage outfalls in
River Cooum at a cost of Rs.50 crore and the
works are under progress.

5.4.5 Restoration of Adyar River in PPP
      mode

      The Government has committed to create
clean and green living spaces for urban residents
in the State. It is also committed to revitalizing
the major waterways of Chennai including
Adyar,   Cooum,       Buckingham    canal   and
Kosasthalaiyar. In the initial phase, the Adyar
River which flows from Guduvanchery in
Chengalpet     district   through    Tambaram,
Tiruneermalai,    Manapakkam,     Alandur   and
Saidapet before reaching the Bay of Bengal will
undergo restoration and beautification through
the Chennai Rivers Restoration Trust. This
initiative, with an estimated cost of around
Rs.1,500 crore, will be implemented through
public-private partnership. This includes notable


                       325
                       325
features such as the installation of a 70 km long
sewerage pipeline on the banks of Adyar River
as an alternative channel for sewerage
discharge,   establishment      of   14    sewage
treatment plants with cumulative capacity of
110 MLD, and increasing green spaces by
developing four parks along the riverbanks.

      A SPV has been incorporated for
implementing this project as M/s. Chennai Rivers
Transformation Company Ltd (CRTCL) on
08.07.2024.

     The Government have accorded both
administrative and financial sanctions for this
project vide G.O.(Ms) No 50, MAWS dept., dated
14.03.2024.

5.5 Recycle and Reuse

      The State of Tamil Nadu is pioneer in
recycling and reuse of used water. 20% of used
water should be recycled and reused as per the
guidelines of Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and
Urban Transformation (AMRUT 2.0). As part of
water conservation measures, secondary treated
sewage of 23 MLD is being supplied to industries
in Manali area. The secondary treated sewage is

                      326
                      326
further treated by the industries through tertiary
treatment and reverse osmosis (TTRO) and
product water is used for industrial purposes.

      CMWSSB has also set up two TTRO plants
for producing industrial-grade water.

5.5.1 Tertiary Treatment Reverse Osmosis
      Plants

      Two tertiary treatment reverse osmosis
(TTRO) plants of 45 MLD capacity each at
Koyambedu and Kodungaiyur are operational
and supplying water to industries. This ensures
assured and sustainable water supply to
industries and reduces the fresh water demand
from industries.

5.5.2 Tertiary     Treatment   Ultra   Filtration
      Plants

      The CMWSSB has taken up construction of
three tertiary treatment ultra filtration (TTUF)
plants of 10 MLD capacity each at Perungudi,
Nesapakkam and Langs Garden, to ensure
sustainability of water resources to Chennai
City. Among these, Nesappakkam and Langs
Garden TTUF Plant were completed and under


                       327
                       327
operation. The product water from Perungudi
and Nesapakkam TTUF are to be used to
supplement the storage in Perungudi and Porur
lakes respectively and the product water from
Langs Garden plant is supplied to Southern
Railways for wagon cleaning and to the Greater
Chennai Corporation.

       On completion of the Perungudi &
Villivakkam TTUF plants and 54 MLD capacity
STP at Sholinganallur, CMWSSB will have an
overall treatment capacity of 1,026.80 MLD.

5.5.3 Tamil Nadu Urban Local Bodies and
     Chennai Metropolitan Area Septage
     Management (Regulation) Rules, 2022

     The Government of Tamil Nadu has
recognized the importance of Faecal Sludge and
Septage Management (FSSM) as a viable
method of sanitation for smaller cities, towns
and outgrowths in metropolitan cities. In
pursuance of the National Policy on Faecal
Sludge     and    Septage     Management,      a
comprehensive programme has been formulated
for regulating periodic cleaning of septic tanks
and transportation, treatment and disposal of

                      328
                      328
faecal sludge and septage. For the operation and
movement of specified vehicles for the purpose
of transportation of faecal sludge or septage, to
the places identified for decantation and matters
connected thereto, the CMWSSB Act, 1978 has
been suitably amended by the Tamil Nadu
Municipal Laws and Chennai Metropolitan Water
Supply and Sewerage (Amendment) Act, 2022
(Tamil Nadu Act 34 of 2022). The Government
has also issued the Tamil Nadu Urban Local
Bodies and Chennai Metropolitan Area Septage
Management (Regulation) Rules, 2022, vide the
notification issued in Municipal Administration
and Water Supply Department G.O.(Ms)No.183
Dated 31.12.2022.

        The Government has approved and issued
the new septage management regulations and
operative guidelines to regulate the removal of
septage from unsewered areas, operation of
sewer    tanker   lorries   in   the   local   bodies,
prevention of illegal discharge of sewerage, and
thus protect the environment. The Faecal Sludge
and     Septage    Management          and     Septage
Management (Regulation) Rules, 2022, regulate

                        329
                        329
the desludging operators (DSOs), who provide
collection     and     conveyance          services    by
i)   introducing      adequate     legal     provisions     to
prevent      unsafe    disposal;    and      ii)   improving
receiving/treatment       facilities   for     disposal     of
septage.

     Some of the key features included in the new
Septage Management (Regulation) Rules, 2022
are, Licence for collection, transportation and
disposal of faecal sludge and septage, Rules and
regulations with respect to transportation of
faecal sludge and septage, vehicles are fixed
with GPS for vehicle tracking system, operators
are required to maintain a log book containing
the septage / faecal sludge transport trip details,
review of the same, Monitoring the desludging
operations and prohibition of unsafe disposal,
prevent any illegal disposal of feacal sludge and
septage other than the locations notified for
disposal, etc.

   CMWSSB has notified 19 locations which can
be used as a disposal facility by the desludging
operators.

                           330
                           330
      The licensee is required to comply with the
rules and regulations specified and also ensure
that each driver and worker employed
undergoes annual health check-up and shall
submit a record of the same to the licensing
authority. Further, the licensee is also required
to insure each driver and worker employed for a
sum of Rs.10 Lakh towards personal accidental
insurance.
     Failure to comply with the rules shall attract
a fine of up to Rs.25,000/-for the first violation,
fine of up to Rs.50,000/- for second or any
subsequent violation and seizure of the vehicles
along with its equipment for continuing
violation.

5.6 Capacity Building

       The CMWSS Board has established its
training centre in 1982 to impart various
in-house    training programmes,   CPHEEO
sponsored training programmes and special
training programmes for the Board staff as well
as staff from other organisations and states.
Every year, around 42 courses related to the
day-to-day activities of Board and 5 refresher


                       331
                       331
courses sponsored by CPHEEO are conducted.
Many special training programmes including
exclusive tailor-made training programmes for
other Government organizations are also being
conducted.

     As part of the initiatives taken by the
Board to support entrepreneurs /start-ups, the
training    centre    has   conducted   training
programme          in     co-ordination     with
Entrepreneurship Development and Innovation
Institute - Tamil Nadu (EDII-TN).

     It has been proposed to bring all activities of
training centre under an End-to-End IT solution
to introduce web based e-learning courses.

5.7 Use of Technology
5.7.1 Geographical       Information       System
      (GIS)
       The work of preparation of assets and
utility maps using geospatial techniques for
CMWSSB under Asian Development Bank (ADB)
assisted Tamil Nadu Urban Flagship Investment
Programme (TNUFIP) is being carried out
through a consultant engaged by Tamil Nadu


                        332
                        332
Urban Infrastructure Financial Services Limited
(TNUIFSL).

      Under this project, all the underground
and above ground assets and utilities are being
mapped in a GIS platform for optimum
management and maintenance of assets and
better service delivery to the citizens. This
project also covers capacity building programme
for the CMWSSB engineers for managing the
assets using this GIS platform in future.

     For the above project, administrative
approval has been accorded at a cost of
Rs.17.80 crore vide G.O. (Ms) No.8, MAWS
dated 20.01.2023 under AMRUT 2.0 for setting
up of a dedicated GIS control room at the
Board’s Head office at Chinthadripet. It was
completed and inaugurated by the Hon’ble Chief
Minister on 12.08.2024.

     The GIS Project has captured 1,61,776
Machine holes covering a distance of 4,174 Km
Sewer Main and 613.75 Km of Sewer Pumping
Main. Also, nearly 121 WDS are captured in the
15 areas together with 1,159.92 Km of



                     333
                     333
Transmission Main      and    6,222.17    Km    of
Distribution Main.

5.7.2 Monitoring of Water Tankers using
      Global Positioning System (GPS)

     Water is being supplied through hired
water tankers for the streets without water
mains and streets with inadequate pressure
either directly or by filling the HDPE tanks
erected in these localities regularly. Everyday,
nearly 26 MLD of water is supplied through
3,300 lorry trips. These tankers are being
monitored by GPS tracking system to have
continuous vigil, thus ensuring the timely
delivery of water to the right people.

      Consumers can avail the following online
services   through    the   Board’s    website
https://cmwssb.tn.gov.in/
   •   Public grievances and redressal system

   •   Apply for New water/sewer connection
   •   New water connection for industries

5.7.4 Information         Technology         (IT)
      Initiatives

     CMWSSB in coordination with TNeGA
(Tamil Nadu e-Governance Agency) is in the
process of developing an End-to-End IT
implementation and new ERP (Enterprise
Resource Planning) solution at a cost of
Rs.3.49 crore to cover all the activities/services
of CMWSSB. This project is completed and
parallel process is in progress.

5.7.4.1 e-Office Implementation

      In CMWSSB e-Office implementation has
been taken up and all the stages has been
successfully completed. Training to all staff
members of CMWSSB provided by TNeGA. The
implementation of e-Office application is started
from 02.05.2024. All the newly arising files are
created in e-office only. All the pending physical
files with 25 and below pages are scanned and
converted into e-file. Remaining physical files
are being scanned and converted into e-file.


                       335
                       335
Total e-files   created    as   on    31.01.2025   is
23,164.

5.7.4.2 “Septic   Clearance      at    Door   Step”
        App

    CMWSSB has developed a user-friendly
Web and Mobile application called “Septic
Clearance at Door Step” for the consumers to
book the Sewage Tankers and Licensed Sewer
Lorry Operators to register and render their
service, through Tamil Nadu e-Governance
Agency (TNeGA). The application is developed to
facilitate the operator to provide and the
consumers to avail Septic Tank Clearance
seamlessly. One of the key features of the App is
to ensure that the collected sewage/septage is
being properly discharged at the authorized
decanting facilities of CMWSSB. This ensures
that there is no unauthorized discharge of
sewage in open lands and water bodies.

5.8 Chennai City Partnership Programme

      The    Government     have   accorded
in-principle approval   for   Chennai   City
Partnership Program and its Disbursement
Linked Indicators (DLI) vide G.O.(D)No.289,

                          336
                          336
Finance Department dated 16.09.2021. The
participating agencies are Greater Chennai
Corporation, Metropolitan Transport Corporation,
Water Resources Department, CMWSSB and
Chennai      Unified   Metropolitan    Transport
Authority. The partnership adopts a framework
with three result areas to enable a transition to
higher quality, sustainable and resilient services
through

   •   strengthening and professionalizing service
       delivery institutions

   •   improving their operational efficiency and
       service delivery performance and

   •   enhancing financial sustainability.

      It is a multi-sector program for results
operation (PforR) linked to achievement of key
results    and    indicators.   The   program
development objectives (PDO) for CMWSSB are
improvement in service delivery performance
and increase in share of operation and
maintenance costs recovered through user
charges. CMWSSB has to achieve 4 DLIs during
the program period.


                        337
                        337
5.8.1 Metering Policy

      As part of DLI 7 of the Chennai City
Partnership, CMWSSB has to adopt a policy for
metering. The Board, in its meeting dated
23.11.2022, resolved to approve the metering
policy.

  The key objectives of the policy are:

  •   To promote water conservation                by
      encouraging efficient water use

  •   To reduce non-revenue water (NRW) and
      increase cost recovery.

  •   To   ensure   fairness   and   equity   to   all
      consumers in charging for water services.

  •   To achieve 100% metering of all high-rise
      buildings and commercial connections by
      the year 2025 and to achieve 80% of
      domestic connections (G+2 and below
      buildings) by the year 2027.

  •   To set out roles and responsibilities of both
      CMWSSB and consumers in relation to the
      installation of   metered   connections,
      maintenance of the water meters.


                       338
                       338
      Metering policy has been approved and
adopted by the Board. Further, detailed project
report (DPR) for the installation of smart meters
(1,00,000 meters) for commercial, partly
commercial, Industries, Institutions and Multi
Storey Buildings (MSB) for both core & added
areas has been prepared. Tenders will be called
for shortly.

5.8.2 Bulk Water Metering

     Water audit provides essential data for
system performance studies, facility planning
and evaluation of conservation measures. Bulk
water metering helps in water audit and
assessment of non-revenue water (NRW). Based
on the proposal from CMWSSB, Government
have accorded administrative approval for
Rs.43.01 crore under Singara Chennai 2.0 vide
G.O.(D)No.54, MA&WS Department, dated
08.02.2023    for  supply,   installation and
commissioning of electromagnetic flow meters
with IoT technology based data transfer facility
and allied works in water treatment plants,
water distribution stations and filling stations of
the Board including comprehensive operation


                       339
                       339
and maintenance for five years. The works for
installation of bulk water meters are under
progress and expected to be completed by June
2025.

5.8.3 Adoption of e-Procurement Roadmap
      by CMWSSB

      The   Board,   in  its  meeting   dated
23.11.2022,    accorded   approval  for   the
e-procurement roadmap. The roadmap clearly
delineates the types of tenders that will be
invited through e-procurement. It also states
that the General Technical Evaluation (GTE)
module will be adopted from April 2023 for
online evaluation of tenders. Training on GTE
module and evaluation of tenders under quality
cum cost based selection for consulting services
were conducted by NIC for the staff of Contracts
and Monitoring wing during August 2022.

5.8.4 Establishment of Service Level Bench
      Marks in CMWSSB

      CMWSSB has to establish service level
benchmark (SLB) baseline through survey
including household connections and hours of
supply. The Board, in its meeting held on

                      340
                      340
23.11.2022, accorded approval for conducting
the baseline survey work.

       The following five main types of activities
will be taken up to develop a robust baseline:

   •   Validate and update CMWSSB’s consumer
       database

   •   Design and implement a household sample
       survey on access and quality of water
       services

   •   On-site assessment of water supply at the
       household level on a sample basis

   •   Produce baseline estimates, water-services
       related composite     index   and reporting
       system

   •   Produce a mapping and data report of
       un-served pockets in Chennai’s core city
       area.

    A survey of 15,000 samples along with
NRW evaluation was taken up through a
consultant and the entire survey is expected to
be completed by March 2025.




                       341
                       341
5.9 Monitoring Consumers Grievances
5.9.1 24x7 Monitoring and Control Centre
      A 24x7 monitoring and control centre
(MCC) has been established at a cost of
Rs.1.13 crore and was inaugurated by the
Hon’ble Chief Minister on 13.03.2023. The MCC
has a 24 x 7 grievance redressal cell as well as a
video wall to monitor the important activities of
the Board including GPS tracking of mobile water
lorries and sewer maintenance vehicles, SCADA
monitoring of STP, TTRO plants, desalination
plants and monitoring of water filling points
through CCTV cameras. Consumers can dial
044-45674567 (20 lines) and toll-free number
1916 and register their grievances/complaints.
The registered complaints are immediately
transferred online and by SMS to the respective
Depot Engineers to rectify the defects.
Accordingly, rectification of the defects is being
done immediately.

       24 x 7 helpline number 14420 has been
introduced for sewer and septic tank cleaning
process and to report any unauthorized entry
into the machine holes.


                       342
                       342
5.9.2 QR code Poster
    As a new model for citizen focused service
delivery, a standard QR code design poster has
been created. A total of 300 QR Code posters
were installed in 15 Areas, 200 Depots and
85 other prominent places in Chennai city for
making the grievances redressal more user
friendly and easily accessible.

5.9.3 Grievance Redressal Meeting

     The Board conducts Grievance redressal
meeting in all the Area Offices between
10.00 A.M. and 1.00 P.M. on the 2nd Saturday of
every month, to improve the service delivery to
the public/ consumers. Senior officers at the
level of Superintending Engineers preside over
these meetings. The complaints / grievances /
suggestions received during these meetings are
attended immediately.

5.10 Employees’ Welfare
      The Board considers its employees as its
major asset and therefore the Board gives due
importance to their welfare. To improve the
personal hygiene of field workers, various
welfare measures such as provision of uniform

                       343
                       343
cloth with stitching charges, oil, bathing soap,
rain coats, shoes, chappals and safety gears are
provided to the employees periodically by the
Board.

      The following welfare measures are also
provided to the employees:
  •   A dispensary with a full time Doctor and
      Pharmacist and Lab Technical Assistant with
      first aid equipments.
  •   Special medical camps for eye, ear, lungs
      and heart check up.
  •   As per the Government rules, providing
      conveyance allowance to all the differently-
      abled employees of the Board.
  •   As per the Government New Health
      Insurance     Scheme,   providing  medical
      facilities to the employees and pensioners
      of the Board.
  •   Retirement   benefits   are   paid   to   the
      employees on the next day of retirement
      itself.




                        344
                        344
•   Immediate settlement of Death benefits to
    the legal heirs of the deceased employee
    after receipt of the claims.
•   Providing all workers involved in sanitation
    works all essential safety equipments. The
    Hon’ble Minister issued Safety Gadgets to
    2,149 permanent and outsourcing sanitary
    workers of CMWSS Board, at the cost of
    Rs.65.65 lakh on 12.10.2023.
•   To avoid accidents during operation and
    maintenance of the sewerage system,
    conducting   special  awareness   camps
    regarding safety measures to the Board’s
    contractors and contract workers.
•   Following the safety aspects for the sewer
    allied works as per the “The Tamil Nadu
    Prohibition of Employment as Manual
    Scavengers and their Rehabilitation Rules,
    2022”      issued   by    the     Municipal
    Administration    and     Water     Supply
    Department    dated     12.08.2022,   by   the
    Board.
•   Based on the above rules, the contract

5.10.1 Sanitation    Workers      Development
      Scheme

      For the welfare of sanitary workers, the
 Hon’ble Chief Minister launched the “Sanitation
 Workers      Development       Scheme”       on
 09.12.2022.

      Under this scheme, the core sanitation
 workers will be identified through field survey,
 and they will be linked with Welfare Board and
 provided with ID Card to avail various welfare
 schemes of the Government. Survey has been
 conducted for 3,312 core Sanitation workers,
 including 563 permanent workers, and out of
 which, 1289 non-permanent sanitation workers
 have been registered in Tamil Nadu Sanitation
 Workers Welfare Board and provided with ID
 card to avail various welfare schemes of the
 Government. Skill Training in mechanized safety



                       346
                       346
operations will be provided to them on safety
aspects.

      Training has also been provided to the
988 drivers/ workers of De-sludging operators of
licensed sewer lorries on safe disposal methods
of septage as well as appropriate usage of PPEs,
safety gear, safety tools and first aid kits.

5.10.2 Transforming Sanitary Workers into
      Entrepreneurs
      In line with the vision and guidelines of the
Hon’ble Chief Minister of Tamil Nadu, CMWSSB
signed an MoU with the Dalit Indian Chamber of
Commerce and Industry (DICCI) on 28.02.2023
to    transform     sanitary    workers     into
entrepreneurs. This initiative enables the
operation and maintenance of the sewer system
for seven years using specially designed jetting
cum grabbing machines. Preference was given to
39 legal heirs of deceased sanitary workers,
48 core sanitary workers, and 126 individuals
from SC&ST communities below the poverty line,
totaling 213 beneficiaries.

     The Hon’ble Chief Minister of Tamil Nadu
have ordered subsidies of 50% through the

                       347
                       347
Annal Ambedkar Business Champion Scheme to
213 beneficiaries including SC/STs, enabling
them to procure machinery. This scheme is
being implemented with an allocation of
Rs.500 crore.

      Under this scheme, the Hon’ble Chief
Minister of Tamil Nadu flagged off 100 jetting
cum grabbing vehicles to sanitation workers on
06.12.2024, further empowering them to
become entrepreneurs.

     In line with the vision and guidelines of the
Hon’ble Chief Minister of Tamil Nadu, CMWSSB is
committed          to         ensuring         the
socio-economic      development,      dignified
self-employment, and sustainable livelihood of
sanitary workers and their families.

                             351
                             351
6.   TAMIL NADU WATER           SUPPLY      AND
     DRAINAGE BOARD
     The Tamil Nadu Water Supply and Drainage
Board (TWAD Board) is a statutory body
established on 14th April 1971 with the primary
aim of providing comprehensive water supply
and sewage disposal systems for the rural and
urban populations of Tamil Nadu (excluding
Chennai). It works as a key player in
implementing essential water and sewerage
services across the state.
Key Functions and Contributions of TWAD
Board
1. Bulk Water Supply: The Board acts as a
   bulk water supplier to local bodies,
   particularly those relying on distant river
   sources, and over sees Combined Water
   Supply Schemes, benefiting multiple local
   bodies.
2. Water Supply: In addition to providing bulk
   water supply to local bodies; TWAD Board
   has also provided water supply services to
   various   institutions,   including   schools,
   anganwadis,      primary    health    centers,
   veterinary hospitals, and industries.

                      352
                      352
3. Industrial Growth Support: TWAD Board
   formulates and implements water supply
   systems for industrial parks set up by the
   State Government, recognizing the need for
   water resources to support industrial
   growth.
4. Sewage Treatment and Wastewater
   Reuse:       The    Board    is  involved     in
   implementing        underground      sewerage
   schemes for the collection, treatment and
   safe disposal of wastewater. Additionally, it is
   exploring methods for the reuse of treated
   wastewater for industrial purposes, especially
   in light of depleting water resources.
Board of Directors:
•   Chairman: Principal Secretary to the
    Government, Municipal Administration and
    Water Supply Department.
•   Directors:
     o Managing Director, Tamil Nadu Water
       Supply and Drainage Board
     o Additional Chief Secretary to the
       Government,     Water     Resources
       Department

                       353
                       353
     o Additional Chief Secretary to the
       Government, Rural Development and
       Panchayat Raj Department
     o Principal Secretary to the Government,
       Health and Family Welfare Department
     o Special Secretary to the Government,
       Finance Department
     o Managing Director, Chennai Metropolitan
       Water Supply and Sewerage Board
     o Commissioner of Town Panchayats
     o Commissioner of Rural Development and
       Panchayat Raj
     o Director of Municipal Administration
     o Joint Managing Director, Tamil Nadu
       Water Supply and Drainage Board
     o Engineering Director, Tamil Nadu Water
       Supply and Drainage Board
     o Finance Director, Tamil Nadu        Water
       Supply and Drainage Board
    These officials collaborate to ensure the
efficient functioning of the Board in meeting the
water supply and drainage needs of the state.


                      354
                      354
    The Tamil Nadu Water Supply and Drainage
Board (TWAD Board) operates with its
headquarters in Chennai, and Chief Engineers'
offices   located     in    Vellore, Thanjavur,
Coimbatore, and Madurai. Under these four
Chief Engineers’ offices, there are:
    •   16 Circle Offices
    •   85 Divisional Offices
    •   259 Sub-Divisional Offices
6.1 Vision of TWAD Board
•   Design and implement combined water
    supply schemes and stand-alone water
    supply schemes to provide protected water
    supply to rural and urban areas of Tamil
    Nadu, with a focus on adopting the latest
    technologies for timely completion.
•   Design    and   implement     underground
    sewerage schemes in urban areas.
•   Operate and maintain combined water supply
    schemes covering multiple local bodies to
    ensure an adequate water supply to both
    rural and urban localities.




                            355
                            355
•   Install smart water meters in all Combined
    Water Supply Schemes (CWSS) maintained
    by the TWAD Board and monitor water
    supply through the Internet of Things (IoT),
    aiming to reduce Non-Revenue Water and
    ensure a consistent, designed water supply
    to beneficiaries.
•   Take necessary actions for the reuse of
    treated  waste   water  in  underground
    sewerage schemes.
•   Implement      measures     to   ensure    the
    sustainability of water sources.
6.2 Functions of TWAD Board
•   Planning, designing, and implementing water
    supply schemes for rural and urban areas in
    Tamil Nadu.
•   Planning, designing, and  implementing
    underground sewerage schemes in urban
    areas.
•   Operating and maintaining combined water
    supply schemes.
•   Testing and monitoring the quality of water
    supplied.
•   Engaging in research and training activities.

                       356

6.4 Schemes Under Implementation
6.4.1 Water Supply Schemes
    To benefit the people of multiple local
bodies in rural and urban areas, Combined
Water Supply Schemes are being implemented
using rivers, dams and reservoirs as water
sources in areas where local sources have been
depleted and there is a need for drinking water.
     Combined Water      Supply   Schemes   are
designed to provide:
  •   135/90/70 liters per capita per day (LPCD)
      to urban local bodies (Corporations,
      Municipalities, Town Panchayats)
  •   55 LPCD to rural local bodies
     Currently, 25 water supply schemes
covering 15 municipalities, 37 town panchayats,
and 13,565 rural habitations in the districts of
Tiruchirappalli,    Tiruvarur,    Nagapattinam,
Thanjavur,         Perambalur,       Sivagangai,
Virudhunagar, Madurai, Tirunelveli, Thoothukudi,
Ramanathapuram,         Dindigul,  Chengalpattu,
Tiruvallur, Cuddalore, and Namakkal are under
implementation at an estimated cost of
Rs.17,452.72     crore.    These  schemes    are

                       360
                       360
designed to supply 763.72 MLD of water and
benefit a population of 115.09 lakh, with
different sources of funding.
     Out of 25 schemes, 24 schemes are
Combined Water Supply Schemes and 1 is
Stand-alone water supply scheme.
6.4.1.1 Combined Water Supply Schemes
      Out of the 25 schemes, 20 CWSS covering
seven municipalities, 27 town panchayats, and
11,113 rural habitations in the districts of
Ramanathapuram,        Dindigul,   Nagapattinam,
Madurai, Virudhunagar, Thoothukudi, Tiruvarur,
Namakkal, Tirunelveli, Thanjavur, Cuddalore,
Tiruchirappalli, Thiruvallur and Chengalpattu are
under implementation at an estimated cost of
Rs.14,358.41 crore under Jal Jeevan Mission
(JJM) and Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and
Urban Transformation (AMRUT 2.0) funding.
      These schemes are designed to supply
621.38 MLD of water by providing 12,73,064
functional household tap connections (FHTCs),
benefiting a population of 94.36 lakh.

6.4.1.2 Stand-alone Water Supply Schemes
       The Tamil Nadu Water Supply and
Drainage Board (TWAD) also implements
stand-alone water supply schemes for urban
local bodies. These schemes are implemented by
the TWAD Board and then handed over to the
local bodies for operation and maintenance.
      Under AMRUT 2.0, Water Supply Scheme
to Thuraiyur Municipality in Trichy district is
under implementation at an estimated cost of
Rs.108.90 crore. The total designed capacity of
this scheme is 8.57 MLD, which will benefit a
population of 54,000.

6.6.2 Summer Action Plan 2024
      During a discussion led by the Hon’ble
Chief Minister of Tamil Nadu on 27.04.2024, it
was instructed that available water in dams
should be utilized judiciously to meet summer
demands, ensuring adequate water supply for
the next two months.



                       371
                       371
      For ensuring proper water supply to
drought affected districts, Rs.18.70 crore was
allocated to TWAD Board and administrative
approval was granted vide G.O. (Ms) No.339,
Revenue and Disaster Management Department,
dated 04.09.2024.
       One major effort taken was pumping water
below the scour level of Porthiumund Dam to
Pillur Reservoir via Emarald Dam and also
drawal of water from upper Bhavani Dam by
opening scour vent of Dam through Kerala State
via the Bhavani River to address Coimbatore
Corporation’s water deficit in April 2024.
6.6.3 Fengal Cyclone 2024
        During Fengal Cyclone (01.12.2024 to
03.12.2024),      Tiruvannamalai,   Villupuram,
Kallakurichi, Cuddalore, Ranipet, Dharmapuri,
Krishnagiri,    Kancheepuram,     Chengalpattu,
Nilgiris,   and   Salem districts  experienced
unprecedented heavy rainfall. Consequently,
infiltration wells, bore wells, pipe-carrying
bridges, cables, and connecting mains in CWSS
maintained by TWAD Board were submerged or
damaged.


                     372
                     372
      Swift   restoration  efforts  temporarily
revived 76 affected CWSS, ensuring continued
water supply. Administrative approval was
granted for temporary restoration works for the
damages in CWSS under TWAD Board
maintenance in Tiruvannamalai, Villupuram,
Kallakurichi,  Cuddalore,   Dharmapuri,    and
Krishnagiri districts for Rs.5.00 crore vide
G.O. (Ms) No.578, Revenue and Disaster
Management Department, dated 27.12.2024.
       A Post-Disaster Needs Assessment (PDNA)
report was prepared by the National Disaster
Management Authority (NDMA) to assess
recovery and reconstruction needs. The report
proposes Rs.14.95 crore for CWSS recovery
efforts    in   Chengalpattu,    Kancheepuram,
Villupuram, and Kallakurichi districts, pending
approval from the Government of India under
NDRF and SDRF funds.
6.6.4 Northeast Monsoon Floods 2024
      Following the Fengal Cyclone, a depression
led to continuous rainfall in Tamil Nadu from
12.12.2024 to 15.12.2024, resulting in heavy
river flow in Thamirabarani, MullaiPeriyar,
Vaigai,     MoolaVaigai,    Vellar,    Coleroon,

                      373
                      373
Thenpennaiyar,       Palar,    Cheyyar,       and
Kosasthalaiyar rivers.
      As a result, infiltration wells, bore wells,
pipe-carrying bridges, cables, and connecting
mains were submerged or dislocated in CWSS
maintained by TWAD Board across Thoothukudi,
Tirunelveli,  Tenkasi,     Virudhunagar,    Theni,
Perambalur, Ariyalur, Cuddalore, Tiruppur,
Chengalpattu, and Thiruvallur districts. Rapid
restoration efforts revived 110 affected CWSS,
ensuring continued water supply to the public.
6.6.5   Works under Ungal            Thoguthiyil
        Muthalamaichar (UTM)
     The Tamil Nadu Government launched the
“Ungal Thoguthiyil Muthalamaichar” program
and   established   district-level  committees
comprising    Members     of    the  Legislative
Assembly, led by District Collectors, to
implement constituency-level schemes.
      Under this initiative, 19 works worth
Rs.152.38 crore were undertaken by TWAD
Board during 2023-24. Of these, 4 works have
been completed at Rs.41.89 crore, 6 works are
in progress for Rs.39.90 crore and 9 works are
in the tender process.

                       374
                       374
        For 2024-25, 3 projects amounting to
Rs.56.62 crore have been planned. One such
initiative involves replacing pipes and pump sets
in     CWSS      to   Kottur,    Vettaikaranpudur,
Odaiyakulam Town Panchayats and 38 rural
habitations of Anaimalai Union in Coimbatore
District at a cost of Rs.26.00 crore, currently
under process for fund tie-up and administrative
approval. Detailed project reports for the
remaining 2 works are being prepared.

6.7 Special Scheme
Hogenakkal   Combined        Water     Supply
Scheme Phase – II
     The Hon'ble Chief Minister announced on
20.01.2022 that detailed project report for
implementing       the Hogenakkal   Combined
Drinking Water Supply scheme (Phase –II) will
be prepared for enhancing the present level of
water supply to the people in Dharmapuri and
Krishnagiri districts.

                     376
                     376
      A Detailed Project Report has been
prepared for the Hogenakkal Water Supply
Project (Phase-II) to supply 304.83 MLD of
treated water to Hosur Corporation, Dharmapuri
and    Krishnagiri    Municipalities, 16   Town
Panchayats, and 6,802 Rural Habitations in
20 Panchayat Unions in Dharmapuri and
Krishnagiri Districts, with the Cauvery River as
the source. The estimated cost of this project is
Rs.7,955.37 crore.
      The central share of the rural component,
amounting to Rs.2,283.41 crore, has been
approved in the State Level Scheme Sanctioning
Committee (SLSSC) of the Jal Jeevan Mission on
15.06.2023. For the remaining state share of the
rural and urban components, the proposal has
been included in the Japan International
Cooperation Agency (JICA) rolling plan. JICA is
currently finalizing Minutes of Discussion (MoD)
and steps are being taken to conclude the loan
agreement at the earliest.
     Upon completion of this project, a
population of 38.82 lakh will benefit from the
improved water supply infrastructure.

15,463 rural habitations in the districts of
Tiruvannamalai,          Thiruvallur,          Kallakurichi,
Villuppuram, Pudukkottai, Mayiladuthurai, Erode,
Tenkasi, Tirunelveli, Thoothukudi, Thanjavur,
Thiruvarur, Namakkal, Dindigul, Sivagangai, and
Tiruppur    are    in    the    process       of    obtaining
administrative approval. These schemes, with a
total estimated cost of Rs.24,584.29 crore, aim
to benefit a population of 100.92 lakh.


                             379
                             379
6.9   Research,   Design,  Training           and
      Project Management Centre
6.9.1 Research and Design Unit
   The following activities are being carried out
in Research and Design Unit
1. Capacity Building – India and Denmark
   Cooperation
      Under cooperation between the National
Jal Jeevan Mission (NJJM) and the Danish
Environmental Protection Agency, experts from
Denmark     visited   Tamil   Nadu   for    pilot
demonstrations of energy, pump, and flow meter
audits at the Pillur Head Works and Velliankadu
Water Treatment Plant of the CLRA Combined
Water Supply Scheme in Coimbatore District.
     A "Training for Trainers" program is
completed for TWAD Engineers in Tiruchirappalli
and Coimbatore sub training centres.
2. Study for Shut Off Head in Gravity
   Mains
      In TWAD Board, gravity mains are
designed on the basis of Static head. When the
outlet of the pipe is shut water is stagnant inside


                       380
                       380
the whole stretch of the pipeline and Shut off
head is developed leading to pipe burst. This
necessitates higher-class pipes, increasing
project costs. To optimize costs, an analysis of
the actual shut-off head in the pipeline is being
conducted in the pipeline stretch between
Madam and Uthangarai Master Balancing
Reservoir (MBR) in the Hogenakkal Combined
Water Supply Scheme (Phase-I).
3. Solar Power Plant
   The TWAD Board operates and maintains 548
CWSS, supplying safe potable drinking water
across Tamil Nadu (except Chennai Metropolitan
Area). Electricity charges account for around
60% of total operation and maintenance
expenditure.
    To reduce electricity costs, it is proposed to
install solar power plants for operating pump
sets under High Tension (HT) connections using
grid connected solar energy.
    A proposal has been approved by the Board
for engaging a third-party consultant to prepare
a Detailed Feasibility Report (DFR) for installing
a 200 MW solar power plant on land owned by


                       381
                       381
Tamil Nadu Salt Corporation            (TNSC)     in
Thiruporur, Chengalpattu district.
    Further analysis is being carried out to install
solar power plants on vacant lands owned by
TWAD Board at Vellalore in Coimbatore district,
Kayatharu and Therkku Ilanthaikulam in
Thoothukudi district.
4. Development of Software             to   Aid   in
   Scheme Formulation
   To design the optimal pipe diameter for
pumping / gravity mains in Water Supply
Schemes (WSS) and Underground Sewerage
Schemes (UGSS), the TWAD Board proposes to
procure upgraded Economical Cost of Pumping
Main (ECP) software based on Central Public
Health    and    Environmental   Engineering
Organisation (CPHEEO) norms.
5. Ductile    Iron            Restrained      Joint
   Technology
      As an alternative to thrust blocks for
withstanding hydrodynamic forces in water
supply pipelines, the TWAD Board proposes
adopting Ductile Iron Restrained Joints. This
innovative   technology   aims   to    reduce


                        382
                        382
construction time and minimize inconvenience to
road traffic during construction.
6.9.2 Training     and      Capacity   Building
      Activities
      The TWAD Board has a nodal training
centre at its Head Office and sub-training
centres   in    Tiruchirappalli, Madurai, and
Coimbatore. Regular training and capacity-
building programs are conducted for technical
and other staff through these centres.
6.9.2.1 Training Programs 2024-25
    10 training programs in 45 batches have
been conducted for Engineers, Hydrogeologists,
Draughting Officers, Junior Water Analysts,
Administrative and Accounts Staff, Drivers, and
Office Assistants of the TWAD Board. These
programs benefit 1,355 personnel and are
funded under the Jal Jeevan Mission Support
Activity Fund at an estimated cost of Rs.2 crore.
   A "Training of Trainers" course on "General
Pump Audit and Energy Optimization of Pumping
Stations and Overall Introduction to Pump
Maintenance" was conducted under the Danish
Environmental Protection Agency Program. Two

                      383
                      383
batches of 15 engineers each were trained in
Tiruchirappalli (02.12.2024 - 06.12.2024) and
Coimbatore (27.01.2025 - 31.01.2025).
6.9.2.2 Orientation Training Programs
      Orientation training has been provided to
newly recruited Assistant Engineers (88), Junior
Engineers (49), and Junior Draughting Officers
(48), totalling 185 personnel.
      It is proposed to conduct additional
Orientation      Training      Programs       for
100 Assistant Engineers/Junior Engineers and
50 Junior Assistants expected to be appointed.

6.10 Material Testing and Quality Control
     Laboratory
      There are four material quality control
laboratories functioning under the TWAD Board
at Coimbatore, Madurai, Tiruchirappalli, and
Tindivanam. The laboratories in Coimbatore and
Madurai     have    been     awarded   National
Accreditation Board for Testing and Calibration
Laboratories (NABL) certifications.



                       386
                       386
    These laboratories conduct testing on the
following materials:
 •   Cement, steel rods, river sand, crushed
     stone sand, coarse aggregates, bricks, and
     concrete cubes, including mix design of
     concrete.
 •   Filter media.
 •   PVC pipes, UPVC pipes, O-PVC pipes, HDPE
     pipes, DWC pipes, GI pipes, stoneware pipes,
     and DI pipes.
 •   Electric cables.
    During the year 2024-25 (April 2024 to
February 2025), a total of 11,906 samples have
been tested, with an additional 1,000 samples
expected. It is proposed to test approximately
12,000 samples during the year 2025-26.
6.11 Water Quality Testing and Surveillance
     To ensure the supply of safe drinking
water, the TWAD Board has established:
     •   One state-level laboratory at Chennai.
     •   37 district-level laboratories.
     •   51 sub-district level laboratories.


                           387
                           387
    •   24 block-level laboratories.
      These laboratories test the chemical and
bacteriological parameters of water to ensure
quality and take necessary corrective actions
when needed.
•   The state-level water testing laboratory at
    Chennai is NABL accredited and recognized
    as the State Referral Institute for Tamil Nadu
    by the Government of India.
•   Under the Jal Jeevan Mission, NABL
    accreditation    has    been    obtained     for
    31 district water testing laboratories, and the
    accreditation process is ongoing for six newly
    formed districts.
•   Additionally, NABL recognition has        been
    obtained     for    51     sub-district    and
    24 block-level laboratories.

   For the year 2024-25, the targeted 8 lakh
water samples have been tested with funding
from JJM and the State Government.
   In the financial year 2025-26, it is proposed
to test water samples from all drinking water
sources in water supply schemes, schools,


                         388
                         388
Anganwadis, and Functional Household Tap
Connections (FHTCs) established under the Jal
Jeevan Mission.
6.12 Hydrogeological Activities
       The Hydrogeological Wing of the TWAD
Board     is    responsible   for the   scientific
identification,      exploration,    assessment,
conservation, and management of water
resources to ensure sustainable potable water
supply for TWAD Board projects.
•   Groundwater Prospects Atlas maps (Hydro
    Geological Maps) are generated using
    Remote Sensing (RS) and Geographical
    Information System (GIS) technologies to
    identify groundwater potential areas.
•   These maps also aid in selecting locations for
    constructing recharge structures to ensure
    the sustainability of water sources.
•   Block-wise HGM maps are prepared and used
    effectively for hydrogeological fieldwork.
•   District-wise    Firka   maps    have     been
    generated     using    GIS  technology     and
    furnished to field hydrogeologists to visualize


                       389
                       389
     spatial    distribution   of   groundwater
     resources.
     During 2024-25, 950 geophysical survey
works were carried out to assess groundwater
potential.
6.12.1 Sustainable Water Security Mission –
      “SuWaSeM”
     The TWAD Board is a key technical service
provider for rainwater harvesting and acts as the
coordinator for the Sustainable Water Security
Mission (SuWaSeM) to ensure sustainable
drinking water supply in urban areas of Tamil
Nadu.
      This mission focuses on implementing
various rainwater harvesting methods with a
50:50 cost-sharing model between urban local
bodies and SuWaSeM. The key activities under
this mission include:
 •   Rooftop Rainwater Harvesting (RRWH).
 •   Restoration and rejuvenation of water
     bodies.
 •   Campus Rainwater Harvesting (RWH other
     than rooftop).
 •   Stormwater harvesting.

                         390
                         390
  The  total   expenditure   incurred   under
SuWaSeM up to 2024-25 is Rs.834.63 lakh.
6.12.2 Rainwater Harvesting Awareness
     The   TWAD Board actively promotes
Rainwater Harvesting (RWH) awareness among
the general public through:
•   Public campaigns and rallies in all districts.
•   LED display vans traveling across Tamil Nadu
    for public outreach in schools, colleges, and
    common areas (October 2024).
•   Distribution of RWH pamphlets highlighting
    the importance of rainwater harvesting.
6.12.3 Proposed GIS Activities for 2025-26
     To improve monitoring and management of
water supply schemes, the TWAD Board is
leveraging GIS technology:
•   GIS  Integration          of   Water    Supply
    Networks
    o The TWAD Board operates 548 Combined
      Water Supply Schemes (CWSS) across
      37 districts. An amount of Rs.8 crore has
      been allocated for implementation of GIS
      in CWSS monitored in Tiruchirapalli

                        391
                        391
           District. In 2025-26, it is proposed to
           bring all water supply networks and
           associated assets onto the GIS platform.
        o A GIS web application with a dashboard
          will be developed, enabling senior officials
          to efficiently monitor and manage daily
          operations from their offices.
•   Development          of      Web-Based    Mobile
    Applications
    o    A web-based mobile application will be
         developed separately for engineering
         applications and hydrogeological activities
         to update daily field operations efficiently.
•   Creation of Base and Thematic Maps
    o Village, block, taluk, and district-level base
      maps will be hosted on a web server for
      easy access by field officials.
    o Thematic     maps,    including geology,
      geomorphology, lithology, and structural
      features, will also be made available on
      the GIS platform.




                           392
                           392
•   Drone Surveys for River Source CWSS
    o All CWSS that rely on river sources will
      undergo drone surveys to accurately
      capture their source locations.
    o Survey results will be integrated into a
      Geo-Spatial layer, which will include all
      relevant attributes of each water supply
      network in GIS.
       These initiatives are aimed at enhancing
efficiency,   transparency,    and   data-driven
decision-making in water supply management
across Tamil Nadu.

6.13 Electronic Data Processing Wing
   The TWAD Board has implemented an
integrated e-Governance System, TWADNEST,
to manage core functional areas, including:
   •        Human Resource Management System.
   •        Financial Accounting System.
   •        Payroll and Pension.
    •      Billing  of   water    charges     (Demand,
           Collection & Balance - DCB).
     To strengthen Management Information
Systems (MIS) in the TWAD Board, a proposal


                             395
                             395
has been made to re-engineer the existing
TWADNEST software with a new, comprehensive
system      utilizing the  latest   technological
advancements. This enhancement is being
facilitated through the Tamil Nadu e-Governance
Agency (TNeGA).
      In alignment with the Business Reform
Action Plan (BRAP) of the Government of Tamil
Nadu, and for the implementation of Ease of
Doing Business (EoDB) in TWAD Board, a new
water supply connection software application has
been developed. This portal is integrated with
the Single Window Portal at Guidance Bureau,
streamlining the process for new water supply
connections.
    The e-office is being implemented in the
TWAD Board.
      To monitor the progress of ongoing water
supply, sewerage schemes and operation &
maintenance of CWSS, a Project Monitoring
Software System (PMS) has been deployed to
ensure efficient tracking and management of
these schemes.

                                       397
                                       397
6.15 Jal Jeevan Mission
     The Jal Jeevan Mission (JJM) aims to
provide Functional Household Tap Connections
(FHTCs) to every rural household with 55 LPCD
of potable water on a regular basis.
6.15.1 Components of Jal Jeevan Mission
•   Creation of sustainable drinking water
    sources and augmentation of existing
    sources to ensure long-term sustainability.
•   Provision of bulk water transfer where
    required, construction of water treatment
    plants, and strengthening of the distribution
    network.
•   Development of in-village piped water supply
    infrastructure   to   provide    tap   water
    connections to every rural household.
•   Retrofitting of TWAD Board-maintained
    schemes and execution of ongoing schemes
    to meet the minimum service level of 55
    LPCD in rural areas through FHTCs.
•   Implementation of Information, Education,
    and Communication (IEC) activities, human



                       398
                       398
    resource     development,     training,    water
    quality testing, and surveillance initiatives.
6.15.2 State Nodal Department and State
       Nodal Officer
•   The Municipal Administration and Water
    Supply Department is the Nodal Department
    for JJM in Tamil Nadu.
•   The Principal Secretary to Government,
    Municipal Administration and Water Supply
    Department, serves as the State Nodal
    Officer.
•   The Managing Director, Tamil Nadu Water
    Supply and Drainage Board, acts as the State
    Mission Director for JJM.
•   A State Project Management Unit (SPMU) has
    been established to assist the Mission
    Director in implementing JJM activities.
6.15.3 Committees for Implementation
•   Apex Committee: Chaired by the Chief
    Secretary to Government.
•   State      Level    Scheme     Sanctioning
    Committee (SLSSC): Chaired by the
    Principal Secretary to Government, Municipal


                        399
                        399
    Administration     and      Water       Supply
    Department.
•   Executive Committee:        Chaired   by   the
    Mission Director, JJM.
(Constituted vide G.O. (Ms.) No.33, MA&WS
Department, dated 15.02.2022.)
6.15.4 Finance
•   Under JJM, the Government of India and the
    State Government each provide 50% funding
    for the implementation of piped water supply
    connections in rural households.
•   For support activities and water quality
    monitoring and surveillance, the Government
    of India contributes 60%, and the State
    Government contributes 40% of the required
    funds.
6.15.5 Community Contribution
•   To encourage public participation, 5% of the
    capital cost of the in-village component is
    collected from the public in hilly/forest areas
    and SC / ST habitations.




                        400
                        400
•       10% of the capital cost is collected in other
        village panchayats in cash/kind/manpower as
        a community contribution.
6.15.6 Implementation
      JJM is jointly implemented by the TWAD
Board    and the     Rural  Development   and
Panchayat Raj Department.
6.15.6.1 Tamil Nadu Water Supply and
         Drainage Board (TWAD Board)
    •    Designs and implements Combined Water
         Supply Schemes (CWSS) / Multi-village
         schemes covering more than one village
         panchayat.
    •    Retrofitting of TWAD Board-maintained
         CWSS to augment water sources, improve
         electro-mechanical equipment capacity, and
         replace pipelines to enhance water supply.
6.15.6.2        Rural     Development           and
                Panchayat Raj Department
    •    Designs and implements Single Village
         Schemes (SVS), including water source
         creation,     in-village   infrastructure
         development, construction of overhead
         tanks, and FHTC provision.

                           401
                           401
    •    Additionally,  responsible for in-village
         infrastructure works and FHTC provision
         under CWSS / Multi-village schemes
         implemented by TWAD Board.
6.15.7       Special Features      of   Jal   Jeevan
             Mission
    •    Third-party inspection of all JJM works to
         ensure quality.
    •    Functionality assessment for water supply
         quality, quantity, and regularity.
    •    Implementation monitoring      through the
         Integrated    Management         Information
         System (IMIS).
    •    Water quality testing using Field Test Kits
         (FTKs).
    •    IEC activities for water conservation,
         rainwater harvesting, water security, and
         water distribution awareness.
6.15.8 Convergence of Funds
JJM works are carried out using a combination of
funds, including:
•       Fifteenth Central Finance Commission Grant.


                          402
                          402
 •   State Finance Commission (SFC) Grant.
 •   District Mineral Foundation Trust (DMFT)
     funds.
 •   Other grant sources.
6.15.9 Operation and Maintenance Policy
      To ensure uninterrupted water supply to
beneficiaries, the Government of Tamil Nadu has
approved an Operation and Maintenance Policy
under JJM (vide G.O.(Ms.) No.26, MA&WS
Department, dated 27.02.2024).

        Mission

%) households have been provided with
functional household tap connections (FHTCs).

(101%) FHTCs have been provided out of the
total target of 8.09 lakh.

6.15.10.1 100% Coverage (Har Ghar Jal)
          Districts and Villages
      Thirteen (13) districts—Kancheepuram,
Ranipet,     Vellore,  Kanniyakumari,    Theni,
Thiruvallur,   Coimbatore,   Nilgiris, Ariyalur,
Namakkal,       Thanjavur,   Cuddalore,     and
Mayiladuthurai—and 8,323 Village Panchayats
have achieved 100% coverage of Functional
Household Tap Connections (FHTCs) under Har
Ghar Jal.

                                         405
                                         405
6.15.10.2 Details of Works Taken Up
      The Government of Tamil Nadu envisions
to connect every village in the state with a
combined water supply scheme (CWSS) based
on the sustainable river sources.

      Approval has been accorded to take up
45   new      combined     water   supply    schemes,
56   retrofitting   of     combined    water      supply
schemes & 21,258 single village schemes where
local sources are sustainable, at a cost of
Rs.18,228.40 crore. So far, 26 new combined
water supply schemes, 52 retrofitting combined
water supply schemes have been completed and
balance schemes are under implementation.

      Further, DPR has been prepared for an
amount of Rs.7955.37 crore for Hogenekkal
Phase – II for water supply to Dharmapuri and
Krishnagiri    Districts    for    which    the    rural
components have been approved by the State
Level Scheme Sanctioning Committee of JJM and
have been included in the JICA’s rolling plan for
state share (Rural & Urban) fund tie up.

                           406
6.15.11    Nal Jal Mitra       –    Multi-Skilling
           Programme
     Under the Nal Jal Mitra Multi-Skilling
Programme, the Government of Tamil Nadu
aims to skill 12,525 candidates, one from each
Village Panchayat across the state. The program
is implemented in coordination with:
•   Rural  Development    &        Panchayat   Raj
    Department (RD & PR).
•   Tamil Nadu Skill Development Corporation
    (TNSDC).
•   Directorate of Employment and Training.
•   Directorate of Technical Education.

•   Classroom     training     completed       for
    3,816 candidates.
•   1,006 candidates have completed On-the-Job
    Training.
•   1,205 candidates are currently undergoing
    On-the-Job Training.
•   Upon completion of training and certification,
    these candidates will support the Operation &


                       407
                       407
     Maintenance (O&M) of in-village components
     of water supply schemes.
 •   Funds for this program are sourced from Jal

6.15.12 PM-JANMAN Programme
       Under Pradhan Mantri Janjati Adivasi
Nyaya Maha Abhiyan programme, administrative
sanction has been accorded and works are
ongoing for providing water supply through
CWSS to Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group
(PVTG)     habitations  in   Velliangadu  and
Tholampalayam panchayats of Karamadai union
and      No.24    Veerapandi   panchayat   of
Periyanaickenpalayam union in Coimbatore
district with river Bhavani as Source, for an
amount of Rs.11.75 crore.
6.15.13 Geo-Tagging          of   Water   Supply
        Schemes
      In Tamil Nadu, out of 26,210 water
sources of JJM schemes, the geo information of
23,712 sources have been tagged in the JJM
Field User Application by the field Engineers for
effective monitoring of water supply schemes.

                       408
                       408
Further, the geo information of 20,735 scheme
information boards have been tagged. In PM
Gati Shakti portal, out of 101 Schemes, so far
76 Multi Village Schemes have been uploaded.
6.15.14 Extension of Mission Period
    To complete the schemes under Jal Jeevan
Mission early, the Government of Tamil Nadu
have released an advance amount of
Rs.1,300 crore to meet the expenses of the
ongoing works prior to release of funds from
Government of India.
      In this circumstance, the Hon’ble Chief
Minister of Tamil Nadu has requested the
Hon’ble Minister for Jal Shakti, Government of
India to extend JJM upto September 2028, to
release the funds for the schemes already
approved in the State Level Scheme Sanctioning
Committee (SLSSC) and to approve 19 new
schemes for providing balance FHTCs at an
estimated cost of Rs.21,231.87 crore to ensure
sustainable water supply to rural Tamil Nadu.
      In addition, the Hon’ble Minister for
Municipal Administration met the Hon’ble
Minister for Jal Shakti, Hon’ble Minister for


                     409
                     409
Finance, Government of India and        Cabinet
Secretary and apprised on the same.
      Taking these requests into consideration,
GoI has announced extension of JJM till 2028, in
order to achieve 100 % saturation in providing
FHTC’s as per the vision of JJM.




                      410
                      410
7.   Tamil   Nadu    Urban    Infrastructure
     Financial Services Limited
      Tamil Nadu Urban Infrastructure Financial
Services Limited (TNUIFSL) was established in
November 1996, as a Public Limited Company,
by the Government of Tamil Nadu with private
sector participation from ICICI Bank Limited
(ICICI), HDFC Bank Limited (HDFC) and IL and
FS Financial Services Limited (IL and FS).
The authorized capital of the company is
Rs.2.00 crore and the paid-up share capital is
Rs.1.00 crore, of which a sum of Rs.49.00 lakh is
contributed by the Government of Tamil Nadu.
     Tamil Nadu Urban Development Fund,
Water and Sanitation Pooled Fund and various
other Government Grant Funds are managed by
TNUIFSL. It provides a spectrum of solutions
towards      the    development      of    urban
infrastructure,   right   from     concept    to
commissioning of the projects. The main tasks of
the company include project development,
project appraisal, project structuring, resource
mobilization and fund management. The
company has been operating on profit since its



                       411
                       411
establishment and dividends are declared and
paid regularly.
7.1 Tamil Nadu Urban Development Fund
    (TNUDF)
     Tamil Nadu Urban Development is a Trust,
established by the Government of Tamil Nadu, in
November 1996, with private sector participation
from ICICI, HDFC and IL and FS.
     This fund functions as a sustainable
financial intermediary that enhances the flow of
private capital to urban sector in the State of
Tamil Nadu and facilitates Urban Local Bodies to
become capable and sustainable organisations,
sensitive to stakeholders in providing highest
quality of urban services.
     TNUDF has a mission “to contribute to the
improvement in urban quality of life in
Tamil Nadu by facilitating efficient urban
infrastructure asset creation and provision of
urban services through innovative project
development,     independent   appraisal   and
sustainable financing”.




                      412
                      412
7.1.1 Objectives of TNUDF
The objectives of TNUDF are as follows:
➢ Finance urban infrastructure projects, which
  improve the living standards of the urban
  population.
➢ Facilitate private sector participation in
  infrastructure through joint ventures and
  public–private partnerships.
➢ Improve the financial management of urban
  local bodies and enable them to access debt
  finance from markets.
➢ The financial assistance to Urban Local
  Bodies for implementation of basic service
  projects such as roads, bridges, storm water
  drains, sewerage and sanitation, water
  supply, solid waste management and
  commercially viable remunerative projects,
  such as bus stands and market complexes is
  provided by TNUDF.
➢ TNUDF is managed by a corporate trustee
  viz., Tamil Nadu Urban Infrastructure Trustee
  Company Limited (TNUITCL) and is operated
  by a Fund Manager viz., the Tamil Nadu



                      413
                      413
   Urban Infrastructure     Financial   Services
   Limited.
➢ The    total corpus    of  the Fund   is
  Rs.199.60 crore, of which a sum of
  Rs.142.91 crore has been contributed by
  Government of Tamil Nadu and a sum of
  Rs.56.69 crore has been contributed by
  ICICI, HDFC and IL and FS.

7.2.1    Tamil   Nadu    Urban  Flagship
         Investment   Program  (TNUFIP)
         assisted by Asian Development
         Bank (ADB)
       The Tamil Nadu Urban Flagship Investment
Program commenced in the year 2018 with the
assistance of Asian Development Bank. The
objective of this Program is to develop priority
water       supply,  sewerage    and     drainage
infrastructure located within strategic industrial
corridors of Tamil Nadu, support innovative
pilots,     improve   urban    governance     and
strengthen the capacity of State and local
institutions      to   enhance     environmental
sustainability, climate resilience and urban
livability.
      Tamil Nadu Urban Flagship Investment
Program has three phases (Phase-I, Phase-II
and Phase-III). The assistance from ADB is
US$ 502 million for all the three phases.
      The    total outlay     of  Phase-I    was
Rs.3,070.33 crore (US $477.50 million) with
ADB     assistance   of    Rs.1,099.53      crore
(US     $171.00     million).    The      project
implementation period is between November


                       416
                       416
2018     and     June     2023.     The    project
implementation period of Phase-I has been
extended up to June 2025 with an revised total
outlay of Rs.3,318.30 crore (US $477.50 million)
considering the funds available due to exchange
rate variations. Of the above, the ADB assistance
is Rs.1,310.86 crore (US $171.00 million) and
the contribution of the State Government and
other    sources     being   Rs.2,007.44     crore
(US $306.50 million). The funding assistance of
Rs.1,131.03 crore has been committed for
9 sub-projects under TNUFIP Phase-I. Of the
above, 3 sub-projects (Coimbatore Corporation
Solar Development Project, Chennai Metropolitan
Water Supply and Sewerage Board–Water
Supply Scheme in 4 added areas of Greater
Chennai      Corporation     and     Rajapalayam
Municipality Underground Sewerage Scheme)
have been completed and the               balance
6 sub-projects are under various stages of
implementation. The details are furnished
below:

taken up under MID-TN Grant Program are being
identified.

       The loan documents for Tamil Nadu

services in Water Supply and Sanitation sector in

2024 and December 2030. Of the above,

(PforR), Rs.247.20 crore under Investment
Project Finance (IPF) and Rs.6.23 crore is
towards Front End Fee. The funding assistance
of Rs.1,609.65 crore has been committed for
