 7.5   Water and     Sanitation   Pooled   Fund
       (WSPF)
       The Government of Tamil Nadu has
created WSPF as a Trust to cater to civic
infrastructure needs like water and sanitation in
small and medium towns by raising resources
from capital markets. This Trust has been
created as a not-for-profit entity.


                       432
                       432
The objectives of WSPF are:
•   To provide financial assistance for setting up
    infrastructure projects,
•   To mobilize resources from the capital
    market under pooled finance structure,
•   To facilitate the participation of private sector
    in water and used water sectors through
    direct    investment     and    public    private
    partnership,
•   To enable urban local bodies to access debt
    finance from markets and
•   To act as the Nodal Agency on behalf of
    Union and / or State Government for water,
    sanitation and / or any other infrastructure
    projects.
    A sum of Rs.302.30 crore has been
mobilized through taxable bonds and tax-free
bonds by WSPF. Of the above, bonds of
Rs.227.30 crore have been redeemed.
     A sum of Rs.624.04 crore has been availed
as loan from Housing and Urban Development
Corporation Limited for on-lending to Urban
Local Bodies for implementing Vellore Combined


                        433
                        433
Water    Supply   Improvement     Scheme,
Aruppukottai   Combined     Water   Supply
Improvement Scheme, Tindivanam Underground
Sewerage Scheme and Villupuram Underground
Sewerage Scheme.




                   434
                   434
8. CHENNAI RIVERS RESTORATION TRUST

     The Government of Tamil Nadu envisions
providing clean, green and sustainable healthy
environment for all in urban areas. To achieve
this vision, the Chennai Rivers Restoration Trust
(CRRT) has been set up by the Government to
enhance the ecological conditions of rivers,
waterbodies, creek and estuarine eco-system.
     Further, the Chennai Rivers Restoration
Trust has been directed to carry out the primary
functions   of   planning,    coordinating   and
monitoring the activities for the environmental
restoration of rivers and water bodies viz.,
Cooum,       Adyar,     Kosasthalaiyar     River,
Buckingham Canal along with other canals,
drains, estuaries and creeks in Chennai
Metropolitan Area.
      In this direction, the Chennai Rivers
Restoration Trust      is undertaking     various
initiatives to improve the ecology of the city by
engaging various line departments in its
eco-restoration projects.




                       435
                       435
8.1 Completed Eco-Restoration Projects

8.1.1 ‘Tholkappia Poonga’-Eco-Restoration
       of Adyar Creek (58 acres)

      The Government of Tamil Nadu have
initiated a pioneering urban coastal wetland
ecological restoration project for the Adyar
Creek in an extent of 58 acres under Phase-I.
The Government of Tamil Nadu have accorded
administrative sanction of Rs.100 crore for the
restoration of 358 acres of Adyar Creek area and
also to restore the Adyar Estuary area located in
the eastern side of Thiru.Vi.Ka. bridge.

    The Adyar creek and estuary area was
degraded due to infestation of Prosopis juliflora,
indiscriminate disposal of sewage, solid waste
and debris which subsequently resulted in
shrinking of water spread area, reduced tidal
interaction and decrease in biodiversity.

    As Phase-I, the Eco-Restoration of Adyar
creek (58 acres) commenced in 2008 and the
major restoration activities undertaken were to
increase the water spread and tidal interaction
area, plantation of native vegetation such as
Tropical Dry Evergreen Forest species, mangrove

                       436
                       436
species, etc., and landscaping for interactive
environmental programmes. All restoration
activities were completed in 2011 and
continuous maintenance is being carried out.

    Tholkappia Poonga is now functioning as a
Centre   for  environmental  education    and
research. Regular environmental education
programmes are being conducted to impart
knowledge on environment to the community
and    primarily   for    students.   So    far,
1,12,826 students and 6,070 teachers from
1,446 schools have visited the eco-park and
have been sensitized on the significance of
urban wetlands restoration. Moreover, Corporate
Social Responsibility (CSR) funds have been
received from Tamil Nadu Urban Infrastructure
Financial Services Limited (TNUIFSL) for
conducting education programs for Government
school students at Poonga.

    General Public are allowed to visit the
Tholkappia Poonga from Monday to Saturday
(except Public Holidays). Thus far, a total of
32,973 persons have visited the Poonga.
General Public are also allowed for walking


                      437
                      437
inside the Poonga and so far, 24,528 individuals
have visited. Tholkappia Poonga is now a
standing     example       of     urban   wetland
eco-restoration initiative of the Government.

8.1.2    Eco-Restoration     of   Adyar   Estuary
        (300 acres)

     In continuation of the Eco-Restoration of
Adyar creek in 58 acres as detailed above, the
adjoining estuary with an extent of 300 acres
was taken up by the Government.

     The eco-restoration works were taken up
with the components of bund stabilization,
continuous removal of debris and plastics and
removal of invasive species of Prosopis juliflora,
which has now enhanced the tidal interaction
and increased the water spread in the creek and
estuary.   Mangroves     and     Native   riverine
vegetation were planted to rejuvenate and
enhance the biodiversity of riverine ecosystem.

    In the restored Adyar creek and estuary
(358 acres), a recent faunal survey reported
around 424 species of animals such as
molluscans, crabs, dragonflies, butterflies,
fishes, amphibian, reptiles, birds and mammals.

                       438
                       438
This figure stood at 141 before the restoration
activity.

8.2 Ongoing Eco-Restoration Projects

     Under the aegis of CRRT, the Integrated
Cooum River Eco-Restoration Project (ICRERP)
and Adyar River Restoration Project (ARRP) have
been implemented by the Government to
enhance the ecological condition of city
waterways.

      Under these eco-restoration initiatives,
various sub-projects are being executed by line
departments concerned viz., desilting works,
baby canal formation and construction of flood
protection wall by Water Resources Department;
Solid waste removal, fencing, beautification of
bridges, boom deployment and plantation of
native species and riverfront development by
Greater Chennai Corporation, Directorate of
Municipal Administration and Commissionerate
of Rural Development and Panchayat Raj in their
respective areas; Laying interceptor pipelines,
installing modular sewage treatment plants and
underground sewerage system by Chennai
Metropolitan Water Supply and Sewerage Board;

                     439
                     439
Resettlement and Rehabilitation of Project
Affected Families by Tamil Nadu Urban Habitat
Development Board.

8.2.1 Integrated        Cooum         River
      Eco-Restoration Project (ICRERP)
      The   Government of Tamil Nadu have
accorded      administrative sanction  of
Rs.735.08 crore for the implementation of
ICRERP for the stretch from Paruthipattu check
dam to the Cooum river mouth in the Bay of
Bengal for a length of 32 km. The major
restoration components are river channel
improvement, interception and diversion of
wastewater, solid waste removal, rehabilitation
and resettlement,   plantation   and   riverfront
development.

      Thus far, Rs.604.75 crore has been
disbursed to implementing agencies based on
the work progress. All restoration works are
expected to be completed soon (Except
Resettlement and Rehabilitation of remaining
Project Affected Families along Cooum River).




                     440
                     440
8.2.2 Adyar River              Restoration       Project
      (ARRP)
      The ecological restoration of Adyar River
from its origin at Adhanur lake to the river
mouth for a distance of 42 km was initiated by
the Government for Rs.744.60 crore. The major
restoration     works     being     implemented      are
sewage management, solid waste removal, river
channel       improvement,        rehabilitation     and
resettlement,      plantation        and       riverfront
development.

     The on-going eco-restoration activities are
under various stages of implementation by
concerned line departments and are expected to
be    completed         soon      (Resettlement      and
Rehabilitation   of     remaining    Project    Affected
Families   and   Additional       sewerage     mitigation
works in Adyar River).Thus far, Rs.497.36 crore
has been disbursed to implementing agencies
based on the work progress.




                          441
                          441
8.2.3 Restoration of Buckingham Canal and
      its associated Drains and Drains of
      Adyar and Cooum River

      In order to ensure a comprehensive
restoration of waterways in Chennai city, the
Government have sanctioned Rs.1,281.88 crore
for the restoration of Buckingham Canal and its
associated drains and major drains of Adyar and
Cooum River. Preliminary works such as
boundary survey and boundary demarcation
through Differential Global Positioning System
(DGPS) survey and fixing of boundary stone are
being taken up by respective implementing
agencies i.e Water Resources Department and
Greater Chennai Corporation.

8.2.4 Eco-Restoration of Ennore Creek

     The      Government    have    accorded
administrative sanction of Rs.45.99 crore to
Chennai Rivers Restoration Trust for the
Eco-Restoration of Ennore Creek. Now, Habitat
Restoration  (Plantation)   work    is   being
implemented by Forest Department.

     Forest Department has planned    to
complete the plantation works in 2 years

                     442
                     442
(2023 to 2025) and initiated the clearing of
Prosopis juliflora and plantation of mangroves
and its associated species. Out of 1,60,000
numbers of mangroves, 1,50,000 numbers have
been planted so far. Among 2,00,000 numbers
of mangrove associated species, 50,000 saplings
have been planted. Prosopis juliflora has been
cleared in 200 ha within Ennore Creek. In
addition to that, 1,00,000 number of shrubs,
creepers have been procured and planted. Based
on the work progress a sum of Rs.25.00 crore
has been released to Tamil Nadu Forest
Department.

       Creek
       In order to have a holistic approach in

 Final Report is prepared with the restoration
 components such as river channel improvement,
 sewage mitigation, solid waste removal, riverfront

 the implementation of restoration works in
 Kosasthalaiyar River by CRRT.

9.     Tamil   Nadu   Urban     Finance  and
       Infrastructure            Development
       Corporation Limited
      Tamil    Nadu    Urban     Finance    and
Infrastructure Development Corporation Limited
(TUFIDCO) was established by the Government
of Tamil Nadu on March 21, 1990, under the
Companies Act of 1956. Its main goal is to
support Urban Local Bodies and related agencies
in developing urban infrastructure. TUFIDCO
helps by offering stable funding and technical
assistance to make infrastructure projects
successful and to improve cities / towns across
the state.
9.1 Mission Directorate / Nodal Agency
      TUFIDCO      serves  as    the    Mission
Directorate for key programs sponsored by the
Union and State Governments. It acts as a Nodal
Agency to oversee and implement the following
schemes:
     ➢ Smart Cities Mission
     ➢ Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban
       Transformation (AMRUT)




                       447
                       447
  ➢ Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban
    Transformation 2.0 (AMRUT 2.0)
  ➢ Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam
    (KNMT)
  ➢ Singara Chennai 2.0
  ➢ Nagarpura    Salaigal   Mempattu   Thittam
    (NSMT)
9.2   Fund Manager
   TUFIDCO is acting as the nodal agency and
Fund Manager for the following schemes:
  ➢ Urban Infrastructure Development Scheme
    for Small and Medium Towns (UIDSSMT) –
    Revolving Fund
  ➢ State Urban Infrastructure Development
    Fund (SUIDF)

9.4 Atal Mission for Rejuvenation                             and
    Urban Transformation 2.0
          Based on the success of AMRUT, the Union
Government launched a new program called
"Atal      Mission     for    Rejuvenation         and     Urban
Transformation            2.0"          (AMRUT      2.0)       on
01.10.2021. AMRUT 2.0 will help all towns and
cities,         including               25        Corporations,

                                  452
                                  452
137 Municipalities and 487 Town Panchayats, to

water.

       The       main          goals    of      AMRUT         2.0    are:-
(a)   Providing            piped        water       supply          to      all
cities/towns,         (b)        Managing sewerage, waste
water treatment and recycling used water and
(c) Improving water bodies and creating green
spaces.

9.5    Smart Cities Mission
      The Smart Cities Mission was launched by
Union Government on 25th June 2015.
The mission focuses on two main approaches.
First, cities are being developed using an
Area-Based Development (ABD) method, where
each city chooses a specific area to improve.
These areas were selected with input from

                              454
                              454
citizens and are being turned into models that
can be used in other parts of the city. Second,
each city has projects using technology to
improve the entire city, known as Pan-City
Projects. Other important parts of the mission
include setting up a Special Purpose Vehicle
(SPV) to manage the projects, finding different
ways to fund the projects, encouraging healthy
competition between cities and involving citizens
in the process.
     Under this mission, the Union Government
selected 11 cities in Tamil Nadu through a
competition.    These    cities    are    Chennai,
Coimbatore, Madurai, Thanjavur, Salem, Vellore,
Tiruppur,        Thoothukudi,          Tirunelveli,
Tiruchirappalli and Erode Corporations. The
Union Government has allocated Rs.5,390 crore
and the State Government has provided
Rs.5,500 crore for these 11 cities.
      Under     the    Smart    Cities   Mission,
731 projects worth Rs.10,711.27 crore are being
developed for the 11 Smart Cities. Out of these,
715 projects worth Rs.9,706.21 crore have been
completed, while 16 projects worth Rs.1,005.06
crore are in the final stages of completion. The
details are given below.


                       455
                       455
Table 9.7

9.6 Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam
    (KNMT)
    The Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu Thittam
was launched by the Government of Tamil Nadu
in 2021 to fill the infrastructure development
gaps in urban local bodies (excluding Greater
Chennai Corporation). The scheme focuses on
providing 100% water supply tap connections to

                          456
                          456
homes,      sewage     lines, internal    roads,
streetlights, sanitation, upgrading crematoria
and       other     community     infrastructure
improvements. It also aims to address
infrastructure needs in underdeveloped areas,
slums and newly added parts of Municipalities
and Town Panchayats.
    The scheme has a total budget of
Rs.5,000 crore for the period from 2021-22 to
2025-26. From the year 2021 onwards, the
Government of Tamil Nadu is allocating
Rs.1,000 crore every year in the budget for this
program.
     Between FY 2021-22 and FY 2025-26, a
total of 6,751 projects have been approved
under the Kalaignar Nagarpura Mempattu
Thittam (KNMT) for urban local bodies managed
by the Directorate of Municipal Administration
and Directorate of Town Panchayats.     These
projects, at a cost of Rs.4,112.20 crore, are
taken up for improving urban infrastructure in
municipalities and town panchayats across
Tamil Nadu. The details are as follows:

9.7   Singara Chennai 2.0
     The Singara Chennai 2.0 initiative was
launched by the Government of Tamil Nadu in
2021 to enhance the quality of life for its
Citizens residing in Chennai. The aim of the
scheme is to make the city safer and more
livable by improving basic amenities and
creating a comfortable environment. The focus is
on upgrading infrastructure, including water
supply, sewage management, roads, streetlights

and the Chennai Metropolitan Water Supply and

9.8 Nagarpura Salaigal Mempattu Thittam
    (NSMT)
     Proper road infrastructure is crucial for
economic growth in urban areas and improving
residents' quality of life. Many of the roads in
Corporations,     Municipalities    and    Town
Panchayats that are damaged due to sewerage
and water supply projects carried out by Urban
Local Bodies need to be restored. Hence, it is
important for these bodies to focus on road
development,       especially    upgrading    to
all-weather roads.
   In response, the Hon’ble Chief Minister
announced in the Legislative Assembly that
Rs.2,200 crore would be allocated over four
years, starting from FY 2022-23, to improve the
damaged roads. Based on this, the Government
sanctioned      Rs.2,200    crore     for   the
implementation of Nagarpura Salaigal Mempattu

                      461
                      461
Thittam (NSMT) vide G.O.(D)No.423, Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 19.11.2022. Further guidelines for
sanction of funds to the Urban Local Bodies were
issued      in     G.O.(Ms)No.19,      Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department,
dated 13.02.2023.
      Under this initiative, in financial year
2022-23, 8,501 damaged roads were taken up
for   restoration   at   Rs.655     crore, with
8,350 completed and 151 in progress.
In financial year 2023-24, 11,265 road projects
worth Rs.871.88 crore were undertaken,
completing 10,822, while 443 are ongoing.
In financial year 2024-25, 4,785 projects worth
Rs.414      crore   were     sanctioned,   with
2,543 completed and 2,242 still under
implementation.

9.11 TUFIDCO    Infrastructure                        Funding
     Scheme (TIFS)
        The      TUFIDCO          Infrastructure       Funding
Scheme (TIFS) was created to support project
execution by Urban Local Bodies and parastatal
organizations/agencies. These loans are backed
by     shares,     reserves,       surpluses         and   bank
borrowings, with TUFIDCO primarily sourcing
funds     through       bank      loans.     Currently,         the
repayment period for TIFS loans is ten years.




                               466
                               466
10. New    Tirupur    Area         Development
    Corporation Limited
     The Government of Tamil Nadu, in
association with Infrastructure Leasing &
Financial Services Limited (IL & FS) and Tirupur
Exporters Association (TEA), formed the New
Tirupur Area Development Corporation Limited
(NTADCL) in 1995 as a Special Purpose Vehicle
(SPV) to meet the essential water needs of the
industries in Tirupur area and to provide drinking
water supply and sewage treatment facilities to
the local bodies, particularly to Tirupur
Municipality (now Corporation). The Concession
Agreement was signed in the year 2000 between
the Government of Tamil Nadu, New Tirupur
Area Development Corporation and Tirupur
Municipality (now Corporation).
    The project was launched in July 2005 with
an estimated cost of Rs.1,023 crore. Of the total
sanctioned   capital,  Rs.700.30    crore    was
secured    through      bank     loans,    while
Rs.322.70 crore was raised as equity capital.
This equity capital funding also includes
Rs.10.00 crore contributed by the Union
Government through the Tamil Nadu Corporation


                       467
                       467
for   Industrial   Infrastructure   Development
Limited (TACID). Rs.105 crore within the equity
capital funding were provided by a Special
Purpose Company (SPC), the Tamil Nadu Water
Investment Company Limited (TWICL), formed
through     a    54:46    partnership   between
Infrastructure Leasing and Financial Services
Limited (IL&FS) (Rs.50.00 crore) and the
Government of Tamil Nadu (Rs.55.00 crore).
10.1 Unique aspects of the Scheme
      The Tirupur Water Supply and Sewerage
Project (TWSSP) was implemented for the first
time in the country under Public-Private
Partnership (PPP) to provide drinking water and
sewage treatment to the Tirupur Municipality
(now Corporation). The scheme was designed to
provide 185 MLD (expandable to 250 MLD) of
water to meet the water requirement of the
textile and allied industries in and around
Tirupur and to provide a 15 MLD (expandable to
30 MLD) sewage treatment plant for the Tirupur
Corporation. Furthermore, 31 health complexes
with low-cost sanitation facilities have been set
up for the urban poor through this scheme.



                      468
                      468
     The water quality supplied by NTADCL is of
highest standard and is tested and certified by
the in-house laboratory of the company, which is
accredited to the National Accreditation Board
for Testing and Calibration Laboratories (NABL).
Further, NTADCL also has its own in-house
meter testing facilities approved by Fluid Control
Research Institute (FCRI) (Palakkad, Kerala),
under the Ministry of Heavy Industries,
Government of India, to check any metering
error to its customers. The company has been
providing quality drinking water to households
and industries for the past two decades without
any interruption.
10.2 Support from        the   Government      of
     Tamil Nadu
     This water supply scheme was designed on
a cross-subsidisation model. That is, water is
supplied to the residential areas on a subsidized
basis from the profit generated through the
water supplied to the industries. However, due
to the Zero Liquid Discharge Order (ZLD) issued
by the Hon’ble Madras High Court in 2011, the
revenue of the company was affected as the



                       469
                       469
industrial water requirement     started   falling
below the estimated level.
     In order to overcome the financial crisis
faced by the company, a Corporate Debt
Restructuring package was implemented in
2011-12 and the interest rates on the bank loans
taken by the company were reduced. Also, a
part of the loan was converted into shares. The
Government      of   Tamil     Nadu     provided
Rs.150 crore as Equity Share Capital and
increased water supply commitments to Tirupur
Corporation and nearby areas.
10.3 Domestic Water Supply
     NTADCL is supplying about 106.30 MLD of
drinking water for domestic use to Tirupur
Corporation, Tamil Nadu Water Supply and
Drainage Board (TWAD) and Village Panchayats.
Out of this, 38.54 MLD is supplied to Tirupur
Corporation, 11.06 MLD to Tamil Nadu Water
Supply and Drainage Board and 5.65 MLD to
Rural Local Bodies, totalling 55.25 MLD of water
is supplied as concessional quantity and 51.05
MLD of water is supplied as additional quantity.
Concessional quantity water is supplied to
Tirupur Corporation at a rate of Rs.12.23 / KL

                      470
                      470
and to Tamil Nadu Water Supply and Drainage
Board (TWAD) and Village Panchayats at a rate
of Rs.8.95 / KL. The additional quantity of water
is charged at a rate of Rs.29.28 / KL.
10.4 Industrial Water Supply
     Currently, NTADCL is supplying 51.78 MLD
of water to various industries located in and
around Tirupur. Water is being supplied to
around 650 industrial customers. This includes
644 individual customers and the following 6
industrial clusters:
1. Palladam    Hi-Tech     Infrastructure   Private
   Limited
2. Palladam Water Projects Private Limited
3. Palladam Hi-Tech Textile Park
4. Somanur Water Projects Private Limited
5. Tirupur Sultanpettai Water Projects Private
   Limited
6. Pongalur Water Projects Private Limited
     The above customers belong to various
sectors/industries like textile industries, rice
mills, dairy industries, educational institutions,
hospitals etc. The price of water is determined
depending on the classification of the above


                         471
                         471
industries. At present, the price charged for
water supplied to the following 4 categories of
industries is as follows:

     Therefore,    the   New      Tirupur   Area
Development      Corporation      is    providing
approximately 158.08 MLD of water for the
needs of domestic and industries.
10.5 Business expansion in Industrial
     sectors
     New Tirupur Area Development Corporation
(NTADCL) has been continuously trying to
identify new customers to expand its water
supply business to industries. As a result,
Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) have
been recently signed with 3 major industrial
clusters in Tirupur zone. These include Tirupur
Palladam Water Project LLP (for 5 MLD),

                         472
                         472
Senjerimalai Water Project LLP (for 4.5 MLD)
and Kudimangalam Water Project LLP (for 4
MLD). These 3 major industrial complexes are
expected to come into operation in the first half
of FY 2025-26, thereby providing an additional
13.5 MLD of water to the industrial requirement.
   Also, for the first time, the company is in the
process of finalizing the agreements for
supplying water to the following three Industrial
Parks to be developed by the Tamil Nadu
Industrial Development Corporation (TIDCO) and
the State Industries Promotion Corporation of
Tamil Nadu Ltd., (SIPCOT):
1. Aerospace Industrial Park, Sulur
2. Defence Corridor, Varapatti
3. New    Semiconductor        Industrial   Park,
   Kethanur
     It is estimated that by providing water to
the above 3 industrial parks, there will be an
additional offtake of 7.5 MLD of water.
      Furthermore, the company is continuously
exploring and implementing new business
opportunities in this area.



                       473
                       473
10.6 Financial Performance for FY 2023-24
     (Unaudited)
     In the financial year 2023-24, the company
earned a revenue of Rs.220.60 crore. This is
5% less than the previous year's revenue of
Rs.232.20 crore. The main reason for this
revenue decline is due to lower offtake in
additional quantity of about 20 MLD by the
Tirupur Corporation. Despite the decline in total
revenue, the profit after tax (PAT) increased by
8.4% to Rs.108.14 crore. Of the total revenue,
Rs.209.13 crore was earned from water supply.
In addition, Rs.7.00 crore was earned from
sewage treatment and Rs.4.47 crore from
consultancy fees. The accumulated financial
losses have been reduced to Rs.78.84 crore.
10.7 Corporate Social Responsibility
      As part of corporate social responsibility
efforts, the company has donated Rs.4.57 crore
till the financial year 2023-24. In 2024-25, the
company has donated Rs.1.97 crore to the
Tamil     Nadu     State  Disaster   Management
Authority (TNSDMA) for flood relief.




                      474
                      474
10.8 Impact of the Project
     Before the implementation of the Tirupur
Water Supply and Sewage Treatment Project by
NTADCL, groundwater and well water were the
only     source     of    water    for   industrial
establishments. Due to the huge growth of
textile industry and its related business activities
in Tirupur and its surrounding areas, sufficient
water was not available to the industries.
Therefore, this project has not only played a
significant role in the development of the textile
industry in Tirupur and its surrounding areas and
in providing clean drinking water to the people
of the area, but has also helped in preventing
the significant depletion of groundwater in the
area.




                        475
                        475
11.    Tamil Nadu Water            Investment
       Company Limited
      Government of Tamil Nadu formed Tamil
Nadu Water Investment Company Limited
(TWIC) in the year 2000 as a Special Purpose
Vehicle under Companies Act, 1956 as a water
investment Joint Venture Company with
Infrastructure Leasing and Financial Services
Ltd (IL&FS). TWIC as an investment company
had promoted New Tirupur Area Development
Corporation Limited (NTADCL) which has
implemented Tirupur water supply project with
a capacity of 185 MLD at a cost of
Rs.1,023 crore as the first Public Private
Partnership in water sector.
      TWIC has been actively involved in
developing and implementing water projects
across several sectors and has expertise in
water distribution and sewerage systems,
recycling,    desalination,  river  restoration,
industrial effluent management and developing
projects from concepts to implementation
through a model aimed at reducing life cycle
costs with a sustainable framework.



                      476
                      476
     TWIC       provides Advisory, Project
Management Consultancy (PMC) and Operation
and Maintenance (O&M) services in the
following fields:
1. Urban Water and sewerage systems.
2. Tertiary Treatment with Ultra-Filtration or
   Reverse Osmosis (TTUF/TTRO) for reuse of
   secondary treated sewage.
3. Sea Water Desalination.
4. Industrial effluent management and recycling
   including Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD).
5. Lake and river pollution abatement.
6. Capacity building and training.
    TWIC has expertise in preparation of
Feasibility Reports (FR), Detailed Project
Reports (DPR), bid management, project
management and O&M of various facilities in
water, Industrial effluent and sewerage sector.
11.1    Noteworthy projects completed
      TWIC has been actively engaged in
implementation of various projects across
Tamil Nadu including the following:

     Effluent Treatment Plant (CETP) for SIPCOT
     Perundurai, Erode
 14. Preparation of DPR for 5 MLD, ZLD CETP,
     for   Odisha    Industrial  Infrastructure
     Development Corporation (IDCO).
 15. The R&D projects through a grant from
     Indo- German Science and Technology
     Centre (IGSTC) “Effect based monitoring
     demonstrates    efficiency of electrically
     driven water treatment processes to
     remove salts and micro pollutants from
     process water”.

Odisha).

Investment Company for the financial year
2023-24 has increased to Rs.33.24 crore from
Rs.25.71 crore in financial year 2022-23.




                      493
                      493
12. Sustainable Development Goals
      Municipal Administration and Water Supply
Department is giving high priority to achieve
Sustainable Development Goals through various
schemes before 2030.
a. Good Health and Well Being (Goal 3) -
   comprehensive healthcare services, including
   antenatal     and    postnatal   care,   child
   healthcare, basic diagnostics, and treatment
   for communicable and non-communicable
   diseases, all in one place. Under the National
   Urban Health Mission (NUHM) and the World
   Bank-funded      Chennai    City   Partnership
   Programme, efforts are being made to
   enhance and upgrade both physical and
   human healthcare infrastructure.
b. Quality Education (Goal 4) - Schools are
   being holistically transformed with various
   initiatives across the states.
  •   Physical  infrastructure    - modernised
      classrooms & labs, spacious multipurpose
      spaces, designed toilets etc.
  •   Digital Infrastructure - Establishment of
      Smart Classrooms / Hi- Tech labs under

                      494
                      494
      various programs to enable wider reach of
      education   &   visualised  method     of
      learning.
  •   Sports & Extra-Curricular activities -
      Promotion of Self Defence Programs,
      Targeted Sports Coaching Programs,
      development     of  Sports facilities &
      provision of equipment
  •   Health    &    Nutrition   -    Widespread
      implementation of Mid-day meal scheme
      and Chief Minister’s Breakfast Scheme
c. Gender Equality (Goal 5) - a slew of projects
   under the Nirbhaya scheme for the benefit of
   women, such as access to modern toilets,
   livelihoods, safety etc. A gender lab has been
   already established in GCC to look into
   gender issues and come up with solutions.
d. Clean Water and Sanitation (Goal 6) –
   Sustainable Development Goal through
   sustainable      water      management and
   sanitation. Initiatives include:
 •    Restoring water bodies to raise water
      tables, promoting water conservation, and
      enhancing the surrounding environment.

                      495
                      495
 •   Integrating rainwater harvesting into new
     stormwater      drainage    systems    for
     sustainable water capture and reuse.
 •   Converting wells into recharge structures
     to replenish groundwater resources.
 •   Modernizing public and community toilets
     to ensure equitable access to safe and
     hygienic sanitation facilities.
e. Affordable and Clean Energy (Goal 7) -
   conversion of all existing streetlights to
   energy-efficient LED lighting completed.
   Furthermore,     to    promote    sustainable
   transportation and improve citizen well-
   being, the GCC is actively encouraging a shift
   towards non-motorized transport options,
   including walking, cycling, and the use of
   electric bikes (E-bikes). Roadside parks,
   centre medians and traffic island parks are
   created to improve green cover for the
   benefit of general public. Lands reclaimed
   after bio-mining works for greening.
f. Sustainable Cities and Communities (Goal
   11) - measures are being taken to create
   more    inclusive,  safe, resilient, and


                      496
                      496
   sustainable cities and human settlements.
   These measures prioritize reducing the
   adverse per capita environmental impact of
   Chennai through improvements in air quality
   and municipal waste management.
g. Partnerships for Goals (Goal 17) - The
   Government of Tamil Nadu and the World
   Bank is engaged in the Chennai City
   Partnership Programme for achieving various
   disbursement linked indicators and Result
   Areas      covering    multiple     sustainable
   development       goals.    These      includes
   (a) Enhance efficiency and transparency of
   public procurement in GCC, (b) Strengthened
   health system through improved access to
   services and quality of care, (c)Increased
   availability of pedestrian-friendly multiutility
   streets      (Complete     Streets)     project
   addressing all underground utilities covering
   a length of 11.6 km and so on.




                       497
                       497
Way Forward
       With   urbanization    and     the    civic
infrastructure demands necessitated by rapid
urbanization    in    focus,    the     Municipal
Administration and Water Supply Department is
striving to ensure equitable growth and
development of all the urban areas in the State.

       Driven by the Vision of the Government,
the Municipal Administration and Water Supply
Department is focusing on providing quality civic
infrastructure,   enabling     a     decent   and
comfortable living to the citizen, while ensuring
the safety and sustainability of the environment.

       The department is focusing on building
and improving a governance eco-system with
people centric approach at its core, receptive to
public demands and accountable to the citizen.
The department is striving to achieve an
inclusive system where access for quality and
equitable civic infrastructure to every part of the
town and every       section   of   the   society   is
guaranteed.
       During the current year the department
will endeavor to consolidate the achievements of

                       498
                       498
the past four years in its pursuit for a glorious
urban ecosystem serving the aspirations of the
people, with quality roads, stormwater drains,
energy efficient street lighting, advanced waste
processing systems, green spaces, knowledge
centres and piped water supply etc.

      Under the dynamic leadership of Hon’ble
Chief Minister, Municipal Administration and
Water Supply Department will continue to make
significant contribution in achieving inclusive and
vibrant urban areas complete with all and
adequate civic infrastructure, serving as growth
engines.


                     K.N. NEHRU
